# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **ABI (unstable; decision 10.19):** `anira_plan_report_find(report, const anira_plan_query* query, uint32_t* plan)` in `anira/abi/handler.h`: the first plan, in dense-index order, with every property the query names. `anira_plan_query` (Tier 2, `ANIRA_STRUCT_PLAN_QUERY`, `ANIRA_PLAN_QUERY_INIT`) holds a variant (`ANIRA_VARIANT_ANY`, `0xffffffffu`, matches every variant; 0 is the first) and an `anira_backend_id`: `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` match every engine and provider, a custom engine is `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with its `engine_id` and a custom provider `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` with its `provider_id`, each compared as a string. A record shorter than the caller's header reads its missing tail as the INIT holds it. `[thread-safe]` and `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, allocation-free over the report's rows, so a host looks the plan of a backend choice up on the thread that applies it with `anira_handler_set_plan`. A miss is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_FOUND` (a new status, -23, for every lookup that finds nothing that matches), with `plan` untouched; NULL arguments, a query below its head and a backend that breaks the pair rule are `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`. In `anira.hpp`, `anira::PlanQuery` (an aggregate: `report.find({.backend = {.engine = ANIRA_ENGINE_ONNXRUNTIME}})`, `report.find({.backend = {.provider = ANIRA_PROVIDER_COREML}})`) and `PlanReport::find(const PlanQuery&) const noexcept`, which returns `std::optional<uint32_t>`. Tests: `AbiEngine.FindLooksAPlanUpByEngineProviderAndVariant` (two custom engines on one pipeline, a custom provider given, absent or another, provider alone, engine and provider together, the variant, the short records, the refusals) and `AbiCxx.PlanReportFindIsTheFirstRowWithTheQuerysProperties` (every engine of the build against the report's rows); gate 6 and `anira_header_cxx20` call it from an `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` function.
- **Web (`@anira-project/anira`; decision 10.19):** `PlanReport.find(query?)` over `anira_plan_report_find`, with the type `PlanQuery` (`{ variant?, engine?, engineId?, provider?, providerId? }`, the fields of `anira::PlanQuery` flattened; a field left out matches every plan: `ANIRA_VARIANT_ANY`, `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE`, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE`). It writes the `anira_plan_query` on the module's stack, so it allocates nothing on the heap, and returns the plan index `InferenceHandler.setPlan` takes, or null for a miss (`ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_FOUND`; where C++ returns an empty optional); a query against the pair rule throws an `AniraError` (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`). `Pipeline.addEngine` calls `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_engine`. Tests: `web/test/plan-report.test.ts` (the record the entry reads, the defaults, null, the refusals) and the browser page `plan-find.html` (the bundled gain config and a JavaScript engine's entry: the JavaScript engine by its id, the built-in ONNX Runtime engine by its kind, a provider alone, misses, pair-rule refusals, then `setPlan` on the answer and a render that is the JavaScript engine's).
- **C++ (`anira/anira.hpp`):** the handler half of section 6. `anira::InferenceHandler(Context&, const Pipeline&)` over `anira_handler`: `prepare(Hard | Async | Contract | ContractHandle)` returning the `PlanReport` (a view, valid until the next prepare), `plan_report()`, `num_entries()`; the Hard entries as `noexcept ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` forwarders over `Tensor` (`process` over one slot of each side or over every slot, `push_data`, `pop_data`, each with an optional `delivered` out-parameter; no `float***` spelling: a float host builds its planar tensors once with `Tensor::from_host_planar`) and their `_wait` twins (`process_wait`, `pop_data_wait`); `set_static_input(slot, const Tensor&)` / `get_static_output`; `get_latency(slot)`, `get_latencies()`, `get_available_samples`; `reset`, `rt_error`, `set_plan` / `get_plan`; `native()`. `anira::InferenceThread(Context&)` over `anira_inference_thread` (`start`, `stop`, `execute`, `run_loop`, `should_exit`, `is_running`, `has_exited`) for a context without pool threads; `anira::log_rt`. `set_model` / `set_backend` arrive with the variant axis: select a plan of the report with `set_plan`.
- **Extras:** the bundled models' processing as 3.x processors and engines, for the examples and the benchmarks: `extras/models/hybrid-nn/HybridNNProcessor.h` (`HybridNNProcessor`, an `anira::Processor` whose `pre_process` pops the model's 256 windows of 149 old and one new sample with one `RingView::pop_windows`), `extras/models/BypassEngine.h` (`BypassEngine`, the custom engine `org.anira.examples.bypass` with `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_NEEDS_NO_MODEL`: the newest samples of each row of the input window into the output, the bypass of the CNN and of the HybridNN model) and `examples/benchmark/advanced-benchmark/ClearEngine.h` (`ClearEngine`, `org.anira.examples.clear`: zeros, the advanced benchmark's baseline). The CNN model needs no processor: anira's default fill is the receptive-field window `CNNPrePostProcessor` built.
- **ABI (unstable):** `<anira/anira_all.h>`, generated by `tools/abi/gen.py` from the registry's new `umbrellas:` list: every header of the C ABI in registry order, the draft headers of `anira/abi/draft/` and the platform companion `anira/anira_em.h` (which declares nothing outside WebAssembly) included. An umbrella is an include list beside the platform headers in `include/anira/`; gate 4 compiles it alone as C11 and C++17.
- **Web (`@anira-project/anira/engines/onnxruntime-web`):** `onnxRuntimeWeb({ warmup })`, the inferences the engine runs over zeros of the model's input shapes right after it creates a session (a dynamic extent taken as 1; default 0, as before), so the stream's first inferences do not pay onnxruntime-web's first-run setup. A contract's `"warmup": { "fixed": n }` warms anira's built-in engines only; a JavaScript engine is not warmed up by anira (the engine descriptor has no warm-up pass), and its `load` runs at the first call on a model, inside the stream. Given its own `model`, the engine creates the session, and so warms up, in its `init`, before the inference Worker's loop; `ModelConfig.fromUrl(anira, url, { fetchModel: () => false })` then keeps the page from fetching the file twice. `process` and the warm-up share one run path (`run`). `ort-threads-worker.js` of the browser test warms up twice, and the output stays exact.
- **Web (`@anira-project/anira/engines/onnxruntime-web`):** `onnxRuntimeWeb({ numThreads })`, the intra-op threads one inference of the ONNX Runtime Web engine runs on (default 1, as before). onnxruntime-web's module starts `numThreads - 1` Workers of its own in the engine's `init`, before the inference Worker's loop, and `_OrtInit(numThreads)` gives its environment a global pool of that size, which every session of the Worker shares. anira's own ONNX Runtime engine cannot have this on the web (the module starts no pthread), so this engine is the way to shorten one inference where anira's inference threads cannot run several at once: a stateful model, or one large model on one handler. Measured with onnxruntime-web 1.26 in node on Apple silicon (Asahi Linux) over the realtime inference workshop's stateful RAVE model (2048 samples at 44.1 kHz, a 46.4 ms block): 37.3 ms median and 54.9 ms worst per inference on one thread, 18.5 and 20.5 ms on two. Through this engine in Chromium 152 (anira-web-example's RAVE demo, an offline render that waits for each inference, so its time per block is one inference and anira's copies): 50.0 to 50.8 ms per block on one thread, above the block, and 36.6 to 37.6 ms on two; four threads took 47.5 ms (one run), so more is not better where some cores are slow. The engines of one worker module share its runtime and so one count; an engine asking for another fails its `init`, as does a count that is not a whole number of at least 1. `_OrtInit` now runs once per runtime instead of at every engine's `init`. `ort.html` of the browser test renders the gain model again through a worker module with `numThreads: 2` (`ort-threads-worker.js`), exact. An intra-op count for every engine, native too, is #222.
- **Web (ABI, `anira/anira_em.h`):** an audio node serves every Streamed slot of the handler through `anira_handler_process_multi`. `anira_em_audio_node_create(handler, audio_context, input_slots, num_input_slots, output_slots, num_output_slots, param_slots, num_param_slots, tensor_slots, num_tensor_slots, flags, wait_ms, out_status, out_node, err)` takes the Streamed slots it serves in place of `input_channels`, `output_channels`, `input_slot` and `output_slot`: input k of the node feeds the k-th input slot and output k plays the k-th output slot, each with the slot's channels (from the prepared port), and NULL for a side serves every Streamed slot in slot order. The render callback calls `anira_handler_process_multi` (`_multi_wait` with `wait_ms`) once per quantum, with the handler's empty tensors at every other position, so a model with several streams keeps BYPASS on every output, the wait credit and one miss callback per block; driving one handler from two nodes, one slot each, kept the streams aligned but lost those three. The creation refuses a slot that is not Streamed, a slot listed twice, a non-float32 ring and a Streamed slot no input or output serves, unless `ANIRA_EM_AUDIO_NODE_FLAG_PARTIAL_STREAMS` (new define) leaves it out. The single-slot node uses the multi call too: the single entry processors into the same per-slot arrays and calls the same manager path (`handler.cpp`), so there is one path; not measured separately. **Breaking (Web, typed layer):** `createAudioNode` takes `inputSlots`, `outputSlots` (default every Streamed slot of the side; the channel counts come from the model) and `partialStreams` in place of the single-slot options `inputChannels`, `outputChannels`, `inputSlot` and `outputSlot`, which the alpha.2 documentation showed for `configureAudioWorklet`; a single-stream model needs none of them, a page that set them drops them (or lists its slots). The migration page says so. `anira_handler_process`'s doc says a model with several Streamed inputs is driven by one multi call per block. `audio_nodes.rst` gains "A Model with Several Streams". The browser test's `streams.html` runs a two-stream model (a mono, b stereo) through one node exactly, the refusal of an unserved slot, and a slow engine under BYPASS: every bypassed block carries the anchored input on both outputs (Chromium and Firefox).
- **Web:** `measureRenderGeometry(context, { hold? })`, the render geometry a Hard contract for a handler streaming through an audio node declares, per the Web Audio API: `quantum`, the frames of every `process()` call, the context's `renderQuantumSize` (fixed for the context's life, `setSinkId` included: *Rendering an Audio Graph*), else 128 where the browser has no such attribute (Firefox and Safari today; a `"hardware"` context reporting 0 before it runs is refused), and `burst`, the frames the context renders back to back, always measured: the callback's size is never exposed ("a varying number of sample-frames"), and `baseLatency` is not portable (Chromium: its callback buffer, kept from the first device after `setSinkId`; Safari: one quantum; Firefox: 0), so the library never reads it. An `OfflineAudioContext` renders quantum by quantum (a burst of one quantum); on a running `AudioContext` a probe `AudioWorklet` takes the longest run of back-to-back `process()` calls in whole quanta (441 frames per callback: runs of 3 and 4 quanta of 128, a burst of 512; a quantum at least the callback's size: runs of 1), or the frames per device callback over the quantum, rounded up, whichever is more (timed by a Worker with a microsecond clock; the worst case can be rarer than the window: 481 frames at a quantum of 480 is a burst of 960), watching 300 ms after a 100 ms lead-in and on until that settles, at most 2 s. `hold` takes the context's running `AniraAudioNode`s: each is held while the probe measures (`audioNode.hold(true)`, the new `anira_em_audio_node_hold`: silence, its handler left alone, a hold bit of its own beside the re-prepare's) and released after, whatever happens, so the audio thread is quiet. A live context renders each device callback as a run of quanta, so a handler prepared for one quantum cannot complete an inference within a run and missed about half the quanta live (the gain model: latency 1024, 364-374 of 750 quanta missed); at the measured geometry its latency covers the run (2560) and none misses. The geometry is explicit: nothing re-prepares on its own. `audioNode.prepare(contract)` (`anira_em_audio_node_prepare`) re-prepares the handler of a running node: the node's render callback is held off (silence) while the main thread waits for the quantum in flight and re-prepares, then resumes (about 2 ms in headless Chromium); `anira_handler_prepare` refuses with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` while an audio node streams through the handler (`anira_handler.m_audio_nodes`). After an output-device change the page measures again holding its nodes (`measureRenderGeometry(ctx, { hold: [node] })`), re-prepares through the node and re-reads the latency; anira does not listen for `sinkchange`. Both examples create their context with `renderSizeHint: "hardware"` where the browser has render sizes (feature-tested by `renderQuantumSize`), measure the geometry, delay a dry path by the handler's latency (a `DelayNode`) and re-measure with the node held on `sinkchange`; their check pages verify the dry delay is `latency / sampleRate`. `npm test` covers the run detector and the early stop on synthetic timestamps (loaded schedules, callbacks of 480, 441 and 192 frames, a quantum of 2048 over a callback of 1024) and the release of held nodes on failure; `live.html` (Chromium) requires the measured burst to equal the device's (the test reads `baseLatency` as its oracle), idle and with a node held whose JavaScript processor spins 8 ms per inference (unheld it measured 512 of 2048), 0 misses at it, the refused `handler.prepare`, a node re-prepared mid-stream, and contexts of `renderSizeHint` 256, 480 and `"hardware"` (Chromium: 128) measuring their quantum and the device's burst with 0 misses; `quanta.html` renders offline contexts of 256 and 480 frames through the node, exactly.
- **Web (`@anira-project/anira`):** the typed layer over the generated bindings, the package's whole runtime: `Anira.create({ memory, wasmBinary, abiVersion })` instantiates the module on the page's main thread (its `locateFile` maps the glue and the binary to `new URL(..., import.meta.url)`, which bundlers see) and boots the main registry, with `abi`, `module`, `memory`, `version`, `setLogHook`, `drainLog` and `startLogPump`; `Context.create(anira, { threads, workerModule, logLevel, json })` with `threads`, `ready()` and `destroy()`; `Pipeline.create(anira).addInference(models).addEngine(id, { flags, hooks }).addProcessor(name, { hooks, flags })`; `Handler.create(context, pipeline).prepare(contract)` with `latency`, `setStaticInput`, `createAudioNode` and `destroy`; `ModelConfig`, `Contract`, `createAudioNode`, `defineProcessor`, `defineEngine`, `keyOf`, `engineTensors` as before. The ONNX Runtime Web engine is a factory for a worker module, `defineEngine(ONNXRUNTIME_WEB_ENGINE_ID, onnxRuntimeWeb({ model }))` from `@anira-project/anira/engines/onnxruntime-web` (its init fetches the model and creates the session before the Worker's loop). The example `web/examples/processor-and-engine/` (in place of `custom-worklet`): a TypeScript processor (`gain-processor.ts`, one module defining the processor on the main thread and in the worklet) and a TypeScript engine (`double-engine.ts`, the context's worker module), bundled by Vite (`?worker&url`), type-checked and built by `npm run examples`, its check page run by the browser test, under `vite build` and under Vite's dev server. Unit tests of the registry and of the bindings over stand-in modules (`web/test/registry.test.ts`, `bindings.test.ts`).
- **Web (build):** the module's glue works under Vite's dev server (decision D2): `cmake/wasm-glue-rewrite.cmake`, a post-link step of `AniraWeb`, rewrites the pthread pool's literal `new Worker(new URL("AniraWeb.js", import.meta.url))` (which the dev server turns into a worker import with its client, which an AudioWorkletGlobalScope cannot load) to go through `locateFile`, matched over either quote and any whitespace so the minified glue of a Release build and the `-O0` glue of a Debug build (whose options object also differs: `ASSERTIONS` appends the worker's ID to its name) are both rewritten, and fails the build on a glue without it; the Wasm Workers' import of the worker module is a template literal, which the dev server leaves alone but cannot analyse (it warned "The above dynamic import cannot be analyzed by Vite" of `AniraWeb.js`, once per server), so the same step marks it ``import(/* @vite-ignore */ `${url}`)``, matched over any whitespace in both glues (an `EM_JS` body cannot carry the comment: the preprocessor strips it), and fails the build on a glue without that import too; the dev server no longer warns. A glue rewritten already is left as it is. `web/test/browser/run.mjs` runs the example's check page under the dev server as its last page.
- **Web:** the audio path in C. `anira_em_audio_node_create(handler, audio_context, input_channels, output_channels, input_slot, output_slot, param_slots, num_param_slots, tensor_slots, num_tensor_slots, wait_ms, out_status, out_node, err)` (`src/platform/emscripten/em_audio.cpp`) makes a Wasm Audio Worklet node of a prepared Hard handler: the first node of a context starts the context's worklet thread, a node of n AudioParams registers the processor `anira-node-n` once per context, and the creation completes on the main thread's event loop (`out_status` turns from `ANIRA_INCOMPLETE` to the result; `written_later: true` in the registry keeps such a parameter a pointer in `bindings.ts`). The C render callback hands the node's buffers to the handler as planar host tensors without a copy, calls `anira_handler_process` (or `_process_wait` with `wait_ms`), applies the miss rule (`ANIRA_MISSED` keeps the policy's fill, a failure is silence, the undelivered rest is silence) and allocates and locks nothing; a quantum other than the declared one is silence; the creation refuses a handler whose Hard block range excludes the context's render quantum. Static float32 scalars are AudioParams of the node (k-rate, automatable), Static tensors of any shape are written whole from the main thread with `anira_em_audio_node_set_static_input` (a seqlock block per slot), and `anira_em_audio_node_destroy` stops the callback (a quantum inside it finishes first) and releases the node. A JavaScript processor runs its worklet phases on the worklet scope's registry, which anira boots over the worklet's instance at every create. TypeScript: `createAudioNode(anira, audioContext, handler, { inputChannels, outputChannels, inputSlot, outputSlot, params, staticInputs, waitMs, workletModules })` returns `{ node, handle, params, setStaticInput, destroy }`; it loads `worklet-scope.js` (a monotonic `performance` for the scope, without which `std::chrono::steady_clock` fails on the audio thread) into a context before its first node. The module exports `emscriptenGetAudioObject`; the node gates of `test/abi` link `-sAUDIO_WORKLET` too. The browser test's `worklet.html` renders an `OfflineAudioContext` through the node in Chromium and Firefox: the pool's engine, a JavaScript processor on the worklet's registry (`worklet-processor.js`), the gain as an AudioParam automated mid-render, then as a tensor slot written with `setStaticInput` on a second handler.
- **WebAssembly:** the context's inference threads are Wasm Workers anira starts, as natively they are OS threads. `InferenceThread::start()` creates a Wasm Worker on the module's shared memory (`src/platform/emscripten/em_worker.cpp`: a 4 MiB stack and the thread-local block, from the heap), which imports the context's worker module, awaits the boot of its scope's registry and then runs the loop; `stop()` waits for it to leave the loop (a Worker still importing never enters it) and terminates it. So `anira_context_config_set_threads` and `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO` (half the cores, now on WebAssembly too: `default_num_threads()`) size a real pool on the web, and `anira_inference_thread_start` starts a Wasm Worker for a user-driven thread (it was a flag before). Two `anira/anira_em.h` entries: `anira_em_context_config_set_worker_module(config, url, err)`, the one web-only field of a context config (the first context's module is the pool's, a later one naming another is warned about), and `anira_em_inference_threads_in_loop()`, the threads inside their loops now, which a page waits for before it waits for inferences on the main thread (a Worker starts only once the main thread yields). `InferenceThread::any_loop_active()` counts a started Wasm Worker during its import, so a wait right after the prepare is not refused. The node gate `anira_em_pool` (`test/abi/em_pool.c`, `em_pool_module.mjs`) run a JavaScript engine on the pool.
- **Web:** one model config file for native and web. `ModelConfig.fromUrl(anira, url)` (`web/src/ModelConfig.ts`) fetches a `*.model.json`, parses it with `anira_model_config_from_json` and, for every model entry whose engine the module compiled in (`anira_enabled_engines`, exported as `enabledEngines`) or a custom engine's, fetches the entry's file relative to the config's URL and attaches it as bytes (`anira_model_config_set_model_bytes`, copied); the other entries keep their paths. `fetchModel` chooses the entries, `fetch` the fetcher; `fromJson` takes the text and a `baseUrl`, `attachBytes` patches one entry, `toJson` writes it back. `Contract.fromUrl` / `fromJson` read a `*.contract.json` (`anira_contract_from_json`), `Contract.hard({ blockMin, blockMax, rate, budgetMs, warmup })` builds one, `setGeometry` sets a Hard geometry a file left to the host. The browser test's `config.html` loads `extras/models/model-pool/gain.model.json` and `gain.contract.json` as they are in the repository (`run.mjs` serves `extras/models` at `/extras/models/`; `build_web` restores or fetches the model fixtures first) and runs the ONNX entry on the module's built-in ONNX Runtime engine: the output is the input times the Static gain after the handler's latency, in Chromium and Firefox. `run.mjs` takes the pages to run after the browser.
- **Web (ABI tooling):** `web/src/abi/bindings.ts`, generated by `tools/abi/gen.py` from `abi/anira.yml` beside `enums.ts` and `layout.ts`: the class `AniraBindings` over one module instance, one typed method per entry point of the module (240: the promised, draft and Emscripten ones) under its C name, so no hand-written wrapper can drift from the ABI. The binding makes the `anira_error` on the instance's stack and throws a failed `anira_status` as an `AniraError` with the entry's message (an entry without one with the status text); a `const char*` argument is a JS string (its `size_t len` computed), what an entry writes through its out parameters is returned (one value, or an object by parameter name), a `_to_json` returns its text, an `anira_bool` is a `boolean`, a 64-bit integer a `bigint`, a pointer or a size a `number`; an enumeration entry keeps its pointers. `AniraWeb.abi` is the main instance's, every instance of `createAniraWasm` carries its own (`AniraModule.abi`), and the engines and processors get it in their env (`JSBackendEnv.abi`, `JSProcessorEnv.abi`). The package's own calls go through it (the JavaScript engine and processor registration, the log hook, the inference thread, the instance stacks, the Static input blocks, the ONNX Runtime Web engine's model read), except the worklet's per-quantum process call, whose status is its result. `AniraError` moved to `web/src/AniraError.ts` (still exported by name). The module exports the runtime methods the bindings use (`stringToUTF8OnStack`, `lengthBytesUTF8`, `stackAlloc`, `HEAPU8`, `HEAP32`, `HEAPF64`, `HEAP64`, `HEAPU64`). `getAniraVersion`, `drainAniraLog` and `startLogPump` take anything with an `abi` (an `AniraWeb`) instead of the raw module. `web/.prettierignore` lists the generated `enums.ts` and `bindings.ts`.
- **ABI (unstable):** `anira_custom_engine_detach(engine)` detaches a handle from the engine's lifetime: from then on the handle names the engine for `anira_pipeline_add_engine` only while a pipeline, a handler or a loaded model holds it (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` once the engine was released) and keeps it alive no longer; `anira_custom_engine_destroy` still frees it. For a caller that adds an engine to pipelines yet to come without keeping it alive itself.
- **ABI (unstable):** `anira/abi/context.h`, `anira/abi/core.h` and `anira/abi/thread.h`, the runtime half of section 4 and the user-driven threads of section 6 (ABI 0.2; 114 promised names). `anira_context_create` / `destroy` / `probe` / `capabilities` over this copy's core: the config is reconciled into the core the way sessions are (the first user's config takes effect whole; later contexts and sessions reconcile per field: log level most verbose wins; wait strategy, drain mode, queue capacity and interval first win with a warning; the thread count only shrinks, never to zero; once the last user is gone the next config takes effect whole), the config's sink is registered for the context's lifetime, its `ANIRA_LOG_FLAG_DISABLE_PLATFORM_SINK` and `ANIRA_LOG_FLAG_TRACE_FAILURES` are held while it lives (counted across contexts), a device block is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` in this pre-release. The Host-only capability report: `anira_capabilities_backends` / `domains` / `ext_kinds` / `edges` / `edge` over the Tier-2 records `anira_backend_id` and `anira_edge_info` (stride-explicit enumeration: `out == NULL` asks for the count, a short buffer returns `ANIRA_INCOMPLETE`), one host edge per backend row at `ANIRA_RUNG_STATIC`; `anira_enabled_engines` without a context. `anira_context_byte_image_bytes` (the dense encoding), `anira_num_inference_threads` (the core's pool size: 0 before the first handler), and in `anira/abi/core.h` the steady clock `anira_now_ms` / `anira_now_ns` (`[callback-safe]`, `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`) and the shutdown family `anira_shutdown` / `anira_release_core_if_idle` / `anira_has_core`. `anira_inference_thread_create` / `run_loop` / `execute` / `start` / `stop` / `has_exited` / `should_exit` / `is_running` / `destroy` over `Core::make_inference_thread`; `has_exited` is a new atomic set on every loop exit; `start` returns a status (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` while running, `ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` when the operating system refuses the thread) instead of failing silently, and the 2.x `InferenceThread::start()` returns the same as a `bool` (the pool start throws instead of spinning forever on a refused thread). Tests: `test_Context`, `test_Thread` in `test_abi`; gate 4 covers both headers.
- **Log sinks:** an `anira_log_fn` set on a context config receives every record the platform sink would, as the `anira_log_record` projection of `anira/abi/log.h` (`level`, `flags` with `ANIRA_LOG_RECORD_REALTIME` / `ANIRA_LOG_RECORD_CONTRACT_VIOLATION`, `dropped_before`, `sequence`, the timestamps, `group`, `message`), filtered by that context's level. Behind it one registry per copy of anira (`anira::detail::add_log_sink` / `remove_log_sink` in `anira/utils/Logger.h`, one `thl::Logger` callback installed while a sink exists): two contexts in one copy are two sinks over one core, `anira_context_destroy` waits for its sink's in-flight calls and is refused with one Error record from inside that sink. The test collector (`test/support/log_record_collector.h`) is such a sink.
- **Presence gates:** `anira_abi_link` (the generated `test/abi/generated/link_probe.c` takes the address of every promised and draft entry point and links `anira::anira` the way a consumer does, on every leg that builds the tests, static ones included), `anira_symbol_baseline` (`cmake/abi-symbols.cmake`: every promised and draft name is in the shared library's real export table; presence mode, the "nothing else" half follows the export cut), and the WebAssembly link on the generated `web/src/abi/exports_wasm.txt` (`-sEXPORTED_FUNCTIONS=@...`): from now on a registry entry without a definition on Emscripten fails `build_web`.

- **ABI (unstable):** `anira/abi/handler.h`, the first slice of section 6's handler surface (ABI 0.2; 132 promised names). The plan report as the Tier-2 records `anira_plan_info` / `anira_plan_slot` / `anira_plan_ext` behind `anira_handler_plan_report` and the stride-explicit enumerators `anira_plan_report_num_plans` / `plans` / `slots` / `exts` (one plan per candidate with a model entry, dense-indexed, Host rows only, the wait strategy the core runs; `anira_plan_report_to_json` of the design document is cut); `anira_handler_set_plan` / `get_plan`, one relaxed store. The Hard entries take host tensors and have their own entry below (`anira_handler_process` / `push_data` / `pop_data`, their `_multi` forms and the `_wait` twins): `[driver-thread]`, `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, and every one returns an `anira_status`: `ANIRA_OK`, `ANIRA_MISSED` (a new positive status: the block's inference had not completed, the miss policy filled the memory, every delivered count is 0, nothing is recorded) or a failure, where every delivered count is 0 as well; never waiting: a block whose inference has not completed is an on_miss event. The `_wait` twins are `[any-thread, blocking]`: `timeout_ms` explicit, `ANIRA_WAIT_CONTRACT` = the contract's `wait_ratio` times the block duration (the call's block on the process forms, `block_max` on the pop forms), `ANIRA_WAIT_FOREVER`; the completion semaphore under `wait_ratio > 0`, a 1 ms poll otherwise; without an inference thread inside its loop the twin does what the nonblocking entry does and returns `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` at once, the count holding what that delivered; `anira_handler_get_latency` / `get_latencies` (index-aligned with the output list, one entry per output tensor, 0 for an output that is not Streamed), `anira_handler_get_available_samples` (a status, the count through `size_t*`), `anira_handler_reset`, and `anira_handler_rt_error`, the last real-time failure recorded on the handler (a contract violation of an entry above, or `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` after a failed inference; `ANIRA_ERROR_CAPACITY` never; last-wins; cleared by prepare and reset). Refused at prepare in this pre-release: an Async contract, `ANIRA_BUDGET_MEASURED` and `ANIRA_WARMUP_UNTIL_STABLE` (`ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`; the estimator follows), and a ring dtype that differs from its spec's while the pipeline's processor does not fill the phase that moves that ring (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`; a processor that fills it takes the conversion on itself, since nothing in anira converts). - **ABI (unstable):** `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE` in `anira_phase` (`anira/abi/enums.h`): the engine call itself, on the inference thread between `ANIRA_PHASE_BEFORE_INFERENCE` and `ANIRA_PHASE_AFTER_INFERENCE`, whether the running plan's engine is built in or a registered custom engine's `process`. No processor callback runs in it; a failed engine call is logged under its word (`inference failed in session N: the engine of plan P returned S`). Nothing in the library reads the enum before `abi/processor.h`; `test/abi/header_cxx17.cpp` pins the order.
- **The plan report in the log:** every successful `anira_handler_prepare` logs the report it built as Info records of the group `anira.capi`, on the control thread: a head line (`plan report: plans N, input slots I, output slots O, selected plan P`), then per plan its row (variant, engine or custom engine id, provider, budget), one record per input and output slot (the tensor's canonical name, the domains, the edge and allocate classes, the wait strategy, the recipe, the reason when the row carries one) and one per extension it consumes (host, kind, consumer). One record per row keeps a line readable and well inside the synchronous logger's 1023 characters (these records do not pass the real-time queue, whose records hold 255). Visible when the context's log level is `ANIRA_LOG_INFO` or `ANIRA_LOG_DEBUG` (every level is compiled into every build type); compiled out under `ANIRA_WITH_LOGGING=OFF`. Tests: `AbiPrepare.ThePlanReportIsLoggedAtInfo`, `AbiPrepare.ThePlanReportIsNotLoggedUnderAnErrorLevel`.

The factories `anira_tensor_init_host` / `pinned` / `cuda` / `gl_buffer` / `vulkan` / `opaque_fd` / `wgpu_buffer` / `dmabuf` zero the caller's record and then fill it, `[thread-safe]` `[callback-safe]` `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, and return nothing: a dtype of 0, a rank above `ANIRA_MAX_RANK`, a NULL shape with a rank above 0 or a negative extent leave the record all-zero, which no filled record is; every argument is read before the record is zeroed, so `shape` and a token may point into the record being filled (`anira_tensor_init_host(&t, p, t.dtype, t.ndim, t.shape)` re-points a tensor), and the previous content is overwritten, never released; a NULL fence, a timeline semaphore of 0 and a negative `sync_fd` mean `ANIRA_SYNC_NONE` (anira never fabricates a fence), a token passed by pointer is copied and that copy is the hand-off, a `sync_fd` of 0 or more is owned by the record's `acquire` from there on, and a refused call takes neither the token nor the fd. Planar host memory is part of the tensor, contained: `ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR = 8` is appended to `anira_tensor_flags`, the `planes` arm holds `ptrs`, `count` and `reserved`, `anira_tensor_init_host_planar(tensor, planes, count, dtype, ndim, shape)` fills it from one pointer per plane of axis 0 (the channel pointers of an audio host; `planes` is `const void*`, so a `float**` and a `const float* const*` convert without a cast in C and in C++; `count` must equal `shape[0]` and the rank be 1 or more, else the record stays all-zero) and `anira_tensor_plane(tensor, plane, dtype)` reads one plane with `byte_offset` applied. Tests: `test_Tensor` (`AbiTensor`, `AbiTensorSync`, `AbiTensorDlpack`: zero-then-fill of all thirteen factories byte for byte, the planar tensor, the NULL rules, fd ownership over a pipe, the bridge over the tests' own spelling of dlpack.h in `test/abi/dlpack_producer.h`) in `test_abi`; gate 3 pins 216/24/24 and every offset through the union arms, gate 4 covers the header from C11, C++17 and alone.
- **ABI (unstable):** the Hard entries over host tensors, under the bare names of `anira/abi/handler.h`, which includes `anira/abi/tensor.h` now (ABI 0.2; 160 promised names): `anira_handler_process(handler, in, in_slot, out, out_slot, delivered)`, `anira_handler_process_multi(handler, inputs, num_inputs, outputs, num_outputs, delivered)`, `anira_handler_push_data(handler, in, slot)`, `anira_handler_push_data_multi(handler, inputs, num_inputs)`, `anira_handler_pop_data(handler, out, slot, delivered)`, `anira_handler_pop_data_multi(handler, outputs, num_outputs, delivered)` (`[driver-thread]`, `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`) and the four `_wait` twins `anira_handler_process_wait`, `process_multi_wait`, `pop_data_wait`, `pop_data_multi_wait` (`[any-thread, blocking]`; a `_wait` twin is its bare form with `double timeout_ms` appended as the last parameter: `anira_handler_process_wait(handler, in, in_slot, out, out_slot, delivered, timeout_ms)`, `anira_handler_pop_data_wait(handler, out, slot, delivered, timeout_ms)`, and likewise the two multi forms). A host block is one `const anira_tensor*` per slot, an output's included: a descriptor is never written, the memory it names is, so a host builds its tensors once and reuses them. The logical shape is `[channels, samples]` (`[1, values]` for a Static slot), the sample count is `shape[1]` (no `num_in` / `num_out`), and the memory is planar (`ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR`: these are the entries that accept the flag), one block read by `strides` (contiguous `{samples, 1}`, interleaved `{1, channels}`) or one packed block (all-zero strides), for any channel count; the two sides of a call may be described differently, and one tensor handed over as input and output is in place (there is no `_inplace` name). The tensor's dtype must be the slot's (the ring dtype of a Streamed slot): nothing converts, and another dtype is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`. A malformed descriptor is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` (a rank other than 2, which includes the zeroed record a refused factory leaves, a domain other than `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST` and `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST_PINNED`, `ANIRA_TENSOR_READ_ONLY` on an output, `shape[0]` other than the slot's channel count, a negative `shape[1]`, and with samples: NULL memory, a plane count other than `shape[0]`, a NULL plane, a negative stride, a stride of 0 on an axis longer than 1 unless every stride is 0, a channel that does not start on a multiple of the element size), a flag bit this library does not know is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, and the check order is fixed: rank, domain, flags, shape, dtype, memory and strides, the input before the output. Every tensor of a call is validated before anything is pushed, so a refusal writes no ring. An empty tensor (`shape[1] == 0`; its memory arm is not read, NULL is legal) leaves a slot out; the multi forms take exactly one tensor per slot, Static slots included, and refuse another `num_inputs` / `num_outputs` with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`. `delivered`, single and multi, is a nullable pure out parameter written on every return: zeroed first, then `shape[1]` of each output on `ANIRA_OK` (clamped to the tensor's size for a Static output), 0 on `ANIRA_MISSED` and on a failure, and on the `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` of a `_wait` twin what the nonblocking stem delivered. It is never read: the request is `shape[1]` of each output tensor, so a miss leaves nothing to refill. `release`, `manager_ctx` and `acquire` are not read: the memory is borrowed until the call returns. There is no float-only family of entries: a host that holds channel pointers builds one planar tensor over its pointer array with `anira_tensor_init_host_planar`, once, and stores `shape[1]` before each call. `anira_handler_rt_error` latches one more kind, `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` (`anira::RtKind::NotSupported`). Tests: `test_HandlerTensor` (`AbiHandlerTensor`) in `test_abi`: every description against absolute expectations (each channel its own ramp, late by the reported latency) for 1, 2, 3 and 6 channels, crossed descriptions, in place, the multi forms with a Static slot and the clamped count, the single forms beside unused slots, the three miss policies, every refusal with its status, its latched record and the check order, and every descriptor compared byte for byte around its call; the named comparisons that stand in for one oracle (the entries and the 2.x handler share the copy path, and a pass-through cancels a symmetric stride bug): a white-box look at the send ring after `anira_handler_push_data` under every description, a planar against an interleaved description over one script with missed blocks under the three policies, bit for bit, and an interleaved stereo block with the Static gain as `[1, 1]` through `anira_handler_process_multi` against the 2.x `anira::InferenceHandler` in one binary on the bundled stereo gain model (`stereo_gain_with_custom()`), on every plan this build has an engine for and on the custom row (through a real engine that covers two channels; three and six channels run through the engine-free pass-through); `test_Prepare` runs its miss sequence over planar and over packed tensors, under `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK` too, `test_HandlerWait` drives the four tensor twins (a deadline is `ANIRA_MISSED`, `ANIRA_WAIT_FOREVER` delivers); `test_rt_contract.c` calls the six nonblocking entries from an `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` function, `header_c.c` and `header_cxx17.cpp` call one.
- **ABI (unstable):** the fourth miss policy, `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK = 3` (appended to `anira_miss_policy`; `"on_miss": "callback"` in a contract file), with the backup function `anira_miss_fn` and its setter `anira_contract_hard_set_miss_fn(contract, fn, user_data)` in `anira/abi/config.h`, which includes `anira/abi/tensor.h` now (`[main-thread]`; `ANIRA_ERROR_WRONG_CONTRACT` on an Async contract, `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL contract, a NULL `fn` clears the pair; 161 promised names). `typedef anira_status (ANIRA_CALL* anira_miss_fn)(anira_handler* handler, const anira_tensor* inputs, uint32_t num_inputs, const anira_tensor* outputs, uint32_t num_outputs, void* user_data) ANIRA_NONBLOCKING;` is the first `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` function-pointer typedef of the ABI (`[driver-thread]`; a `_wait` twin calls it on the waiting thread). It fills a whole missed block, every slot at once and whatever the slots' dtypes: one call per missed block on the thread that called the entry, with the very arrays the copy path was handed, so nothing is built on a miss: the caller's own tensors under a tensor entry (a single-tensor form on a side with several slots passes the handler's array: the caller's descriptor in its slot, empty tensors beside it, which name no memory, since a staged slot is the empty tensor of prepare again when its call returns and no pointer of an earlier call stays in it), and empty inputs in a pop. The input block of a process form is already pushed and still intact in host memory, in place too. `ANIRA_OK` means the outputs are filled; any other status makes anira zero-fill every requested output. Either way the block counts as missed: the entry returns `ANIRA_MISSED` with a delivered count of 0 and the stream stays time-aligned, and a `_wait` twin without an inference thread still returns `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`, after the function ran. The function must not call a Hard entry, `anira_handler_reset` or `anira_handler_prepare` of the same handler, and under clang a function converted to `anira_miss_fn` must itself be declared `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`. The two setters work in either order; the policy without a function is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at `anira_handler_prepare`, naming `on_miss` (that is where a contract parsed from a file, which cannot carry a function, is caught: set the pair on the parsed contract first); the pair is copied with the contract and must outlive the prepared handler. Prepare also refuses `ANIRA_MISS_BYPASS` when a streamed output's ring dtype differs from the anchored input's (it cannot fire while every ring is float32). Scheduler: `InferenceManager::set_miss_hook(hook, ctx)` and a `case ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK` in the starvation path of `process_output`, called once before the per-output fill; the accounting of a missed block is the same under every policy. Tests: `AbiHandlerTensorMiss` in `test_HandlerTensor` (the function declared `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, no assertion inside it; an allocation planted in it is reported by RealtimeSanitizer through `anira_handler_process`), `test_Json`, `test_Handles`, `test_anira_hpp`; `test_rt_contract.c` holds a nonblocking `anira_miss_fn` body that reads and fills its tensors through the `[callback-safe]` accessors and hands it to the setter, so `-Werror=function-effects` proves the conversion clean.

A pipeline holds at most one processor (a second `anira_pipeline_add_processor` is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`); the processor owns every phase it fills for every slot and composes what it does not handle itself by calling the default bodies, explicitly, in its own code. A processor is a descriptor, `anira_processor_desc` (Tier 2, 104 bytes on LP64 and 60 on ILP32, no implicit padding; `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_DESC_INIT`): `struct_size`, `abi_version`, `user_data`, `consumed_kinds` (`"<host>:<kind>"` strings that join the consumed-or-fail walk and appear as `anira_plan_ext` rows whose consumer reads `processor`), `flags` (the processor's real-time promise: `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST = 1u` says `pre_process` and `post_process` allocate nothing, lock nothing and block on nothing, `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_HOOKS = 2u` says the same of `before_inference` and `after_inference`, both in `anira/abi/enums.h`; 0 promises nothing. `anira_handler_prepare` checks the promise against the placement: under a Hard contract a filled `pre_process` or `post_process` runs on the driving thread and requires the PRE_POST bit, else `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the flag; under an Async contract nothing is required; a bit the header does not define is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` at add), the four phase slots `pre_process`, `post_process`, `before_inference`, `after_inference` (`anira_processor_fn`, called as `(ctx, prepared, user_data)`, a typedef without a real-time attribute: the promise differs per processor and is the descriptor's, not the type's; an author who wants clang's check declares the function `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`), `reset` (`anira_processor_reset_fn`, `(ctx, prepared, user_data)`: the first chunk of a new stream, after prepare and after `anira_handler_reset`, before that chunk's `pre_process` and on its thread, with the chunk's context in the new phase `ANIRA_PHASE_RESET = 7`, where the accessors answer the role only and refuse a ring or a model tensor; what a processor keeps between chunks starts over there), `prepare` (`anira_processor_prepare_fn`, `(info, user_data, out_prepared)`, called at the end of every `anira_handler_prepare`, after the plan report is built and while the handler counts as prepared, with an `anira_prepare_info`, the record of the new header `anira/abi/lifecycle.h` (which `processor.h` and `engine.h` include) that the engine's prepare receives too, a Tier-2 record of 72 bytes on LP64 and 48 on ILP32 without implicit padding, `ANIRA_PREPARE_INFO_INIT`: the handler, the plan report, `num_entries`, a template of the model end of every slot of either list, State tensors included, in the spec's dtype and shape at the pinned window with the slot's host domain, all-zero strides and no memory, the canonical names, the `flags` of the prepare (`ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE = 1u` of `anira/abi/enums.h` when the handler's inferences run one at a time and in order, a model declared `ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL` or with a declared State pair, with a reset at every stream start, 0 otherwise; informational for a processor, what an engine builds its per-stream state on) and a `reserved` word; the processor hands back a per-handler `prepared` pointer through `out_prepared`, which every phase call, the reset and the unprepare of that handler receive beside the registration's `user_data`, so what a processor keeps per handler, its per-entry scratch, lives behind `prepared` and never behind the `user_data` two handlers of one pipeline share; a status other than `ANIRA_OK` fails the prepare with `the processor refused prepare`, and no unprepare follows), `unprepare` (`anira_processor_unprepare_fn`, `(prepared, user_data)`: once per successful prepare, at the next `anira_handler_prepare` once the old session is released and at `anira_handler_destroy`, after the last phase call of the handler; `ANIRA_PHASE_UNPREPARE = 8` names it), `init` (`anira_processor_init_fn`, `(info, user_data)`, `[main-thread]`: once per registration, at the first `anira_handler_prepare` of a handler of the pipeline, on that caller's thread, before that handler's prepare, with an `anira_init_info`, the other record of `anira/abi/lifecycle.h` (Tier 2, 24 bytes on LP64 and 20 on ILP32, `ANIRA_INIT_INFO_INIT`): the `log_level` in effect in this copy of anira, `num_threads` (the size of the inference-thread pool, what `anira_num_inference_threads` answers; 0 when the context brought its own threads or on a target without threads), a `reserved` word and the `context` of the handler whose prepare reached the registration first, whose capabilities are legal to query, valid until init returns and never kept; it may allocate and log and call the `[callback-safe]` entries, never one that takes the lifecycle lock; what a processor keeps for its whole registration lives behind `user_data`, whether init filled it or the caller did before `anira_pipeline_add_processor`; a status other than `ANIRA_OK` fails that prepare with `the processor refused init` and leaves the registration uninitialised, so the next prepare calls init again; the new phase `ANIRA_PHASE_INIT = 9` names it, appended to `anira_phase` with the engine's `ANIRA_PHASE_LOAD = 10` and `ANIRA_PHASE_UNLOAD = 11` (`web/src/abi/enums.ts` follows, with the two flag bits); NULL: nothing to initialise) and `release` (`anira_processor_release_fn`, `(user_data)`: once, when the last carrier dies, after every unprepare, whether or not init ever ran). One lifecycle, one slot order from the innermost level out (the per-chunk calls, `reset`, `prepare`, `unprepare`, `init`, `release`), one callback shape `(ctx, prepared, user_data)`, two shared records and one naming scheme on both descriptors: the engine descriptor of `anira/abi/engine.h` (below) is the processor's twin with one level more, its `load` and `unload` between `unprepare` and `init`, and the flag defines carry `FLAG` on both sides. The descriptor carries no domain: a processor works at the host end of every slot and never crosses one. The processor has no name: with one per pipeline a name identifies nothing (a record's ambiguity between handlers is the handler's, not the processor's), so every record about it says `the processor` and the consumer column of its `anira_plan_ext` rows reads `processor`; `anira::Processor` of `anira/anira.hpp` takes no constructor argument. The phases of one chunk: `pre_process` runs on the thread where the host end is produced (the driving thread under Hard) and takes the host end of every slot, the ring of a Streamed tensor, to the model tensor, chunking and every conversion included; on an inference thread anira feeds the State inputs, `before_inference` runs, anira crosses the edge into the engine's domain, the engine runs, anira crosses the edge back, `after_inference` runs, anira captures the State outputs; `post_process` takes the model tensor of every output slot back to the host end. A NULL phase slot means anira's default body runs (the window pop or push of every Streamed slot; a Static slot is materialised ahead of `pre_process` and captured behind `post_process` by anira itself). `anira_pipeline_add_processor` copies `min(struct_size, sizeof)` bytes into a refcounted carrier that the pipeline and every handler created from it share, so `release` fires exactly once, with the last of them. Every phase callback receives an `anira_processor_ctx`, the fourth Tier-1 record (64 bytes, align 8, `ANIRA_STRUCT_PROCESSOR_CTX`, so `anira_sizeof` answers 64 now; rows in `abi/layout-0.txt` and `web/src/abi/layout.ts`): `phase`, the `engine` and `provider` of the plan the chunk was stamped with, `variant`, `num_inputs`, `num_outputs`, `ticket` (`ANIRA_TICKET_INVALID` under Hard), `entry` (the entry this chunk occupies, `0 .. anira_handler_num_entries(handler) - 1`, the same value in all four phases of the chunk and distinct across the chunks in flight at once, reused once the chunk completed: the index of a per-chunk scratch a processor sizes in its prepare function, where the handler counts as prepared, so that what `pre_process` pops or computes is found again by `before_inference` on another thread without an allocation), and four pointer slots: `frame`, an opaque pointer that is anira's, valid for the duration of the callback and never dereferenced by a processor, and three reserved slots that read NULL. `anira_handler_num_entries(handler)` (`[main-thread & prepared]`, `uint32_t`, 0 while unprepared) is the count. The record carries no tensor and no ring: a callback asks per slot, the tensor's position in the model config's list of its side, through six context accessors (`[driver-thread | inference-thread] [callback-safe] ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`, no `anira_error*`), each returning an `anira_status` and filling an out-parameter that it resets on any status but `ANIRA_OK`. `anira_processor_input_role(ctx, slot, &role)` / `anira_processor_output_role` answer what the tensor is, the `anira_role` of its spec, the first question about a slot the processor does not know from its model config (`ANIRA_ROLE_FORCE32`, which is no role, on a refusal). `anira_processor_input_ring(ctx, slot, &ring)` / `anira_processor_output_ring` hand out the ring of a Streamed tensor in the phase that moves it (`pre_process` for an input, `post_process` for an output). `anira_processor_input_tensor(ctx, slot, &tensor)` / `anira_processor_output_tensor` fill a borrowed descriptor of the model end of the slot, the tensor the engine binds: the inputs in `pre_process` (a Streamed or a Static input; a State input has no host end there, it is fed behind `pre_process`) and `before_inference` (every role, the State inputs fed), the outputs in `after_inference` (every role, the State outputs not yet captured) and `post_process` (Streamed and Static; a State output was captured ahead of it). The descriptor is built by the call over the memory the tensor has right now (an engine may swap it between two inferences), so a processor asks again in every callback and keeps nothing of it. A slot always has a role; asking for a ring or a model tensor the slot does not have in this phase (a ring of a Static or a State tensor, an input ring outside `pre_process` or an output ring outside `post_process`, the host end of a State tensor in `pre_process` or `post_process`, a side outside its phases) is a bug in the processor and not an answer: a processor knows what a slot is, from its model config or by asking the role first, so the accessor answers `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` with the out-parameter reset (NULL, an all-zero record) and latches it into `anira_handler_rt_error` with one record per kind naming the entry, the slot and the phase, as a slot at or beyond the side's count and a NULL out-parameter are `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, latched the same way, on the inference threads too; a NULL ctx names no handler to record into. Every ring accessor still takes a NULL ring and returns 0 for it. The two default bodies read the rings off anira's own frame and ask nothing a slot lacks, so a pipeline without a processor records nothing. Further accessors may be appended in a later version; the record's layout does not change for them. The rings stay inside anira: the new handle `anira_ring` and its ten accessors `anira_ring_dtype`, `anira_ring_num_channels`, `anira_ring_available`, `anira_ring_available_past`, `anira_ring_pop_block`, `anira_ring_peek_past_block`, `anira_ring_push_block`, `anira_ring_push_fill`, `anira_ring_discard` and `anira_ring_pop_windows` (`[driver-thread] [callback-safe] ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`); a data accessor states the dtype it believes the ring holds and, when it is another one, returns 0, moves nothing and records `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` in `anira_handler_rt_error` with one latched record (a channel out of range or NULL memory: `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`; a NULL ring: 0). The two default bodies `anira_processor_default_pre_process` (the receptive-field fill of the 2.x default processor, channel `c` of a tensor at `c` times its elements per channel) and `anira_processor_default_post_process` (the plain push) are callable from a processor ("call super"), real-time by construction, and return `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` for a slot whose ring dtype is not its tensor's, leaving it untouched. anira counts: after `pre_process` every Streamed input ring must have given up exactly its hop per channel and after `post_process` every Streamed output ring must have gained it, a shortfall being repaired (discarded, or topped up with zeros) and either deviation recorded as `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` with one latched record naming the phase, the tensor and both counts (the guard of a buggy processor); a phase callback that returns anything but `ANIRA_OK` puts its status into `anira_handler_rt_error` with one latched record naming the phase and makes the chunk deliver zeros at its stream position (a failed `pre_process` is not submitted, a failed hook skips the engine call or zeroes the outputs, a failed `post_process` is topped up). The six context accessors and the two defaults carry the dual thread tag `[driver-thread | inference-thread]`: `pre_process` and `post_process` run on the thread that drives the Hard entry, the two hooks on an inference thread; anira's side is real-time whatever the flags say. `test_rt_contract.c` gains a phase callback over every accessor and both defaults, declared `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` by its author and handed to the four slots; `header_c.c` and `header_cxx17.cpp` pin the descriptor's initializer, the typedefs in its slots, the two shared records with their `INIT`s (`test/abi/generated/test_layout.c` their layout), the three phase numbers, `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE` and the 64 bytes; `AbiProcessor.ARefusedInitFailsThePrepareAndIsTriedAgain` pins the init rule.

At run time a registered engine runs on the C handler as a built-in one does: A model entry that names an added id (`anira_model_config_add_model_path` / `_add_model_bytes` with `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` and the id) is a plan like a built-in engine's: the first `anira_handler_prepare` that reaches the engine object calls its `init` with the core's facts, the session's create (under the lifecycle lock) calls `load` once per loaded model anira pools with the engine's `anira_engine_load_info` (the entry's row, the variant, the engine-side template of every slot at the pinned window, the name each slot binds to, `instances`: the model's `max_instances` clamped to the pool for a stateless model, 0 for one whose handlers are exclusive), and once the plan report is built the prepare calls `prepare` once per handler and loaded model with the shared `anira_prepare_info` and the loaded pointer; every inference is one `process` call over the `anira_engine_ctx` the scheduler builds, a shared handler's on a claimed slot of the loaded model, an exclusive handler's flagged `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE` with `instance` 0 on what its prepare built; `reset` runs for an exclusive handler at the first inference of a new stream (after prepare, after `anira_handler_reset`) with that inference's context and its prepared pointer; `unprepare` fires once per successful prepare at the handler's next prepare or its destroy, `unload` once per successful load when the last handler holding the loaded model is re-prepared or destroyed, after every unprepare of it, and `release` once, when the last reference to the engine object dies. anira never opens a custom row's path or reads its bytes: the engine's load does, through the config getters on the variant the record names. Two handlers share one loaded model when they run the same engine object on an equal variant resolved to equal tensors, whichever pipelines they were created from (the pool key is the model record, the whole variant as canonical JSON, since the engine's load may read any of it, and the engine object; two engine objects never share, even over equal descriptors; whether a handler is exclusive is no part of the key): a model declared `ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL` or with a declared State pair is loaded once too, with `instances` 0, and every handler of it is prepared under `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE`, its inferences running one at a time and in order under the dispatch gate. A status other than `ANIRA_OK` from `init`, `load` or `prepare` fails the prepare naming the engine and the level ("the engine 'org.example.gain' refused load: it returned N (...)"), owing no unload or unprepare for the refused level; one from `process` fails the chunk as an engine failure does: `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` on `anira_handler_rt_error` with one latched record naming the status, zeros at the chunk's stream position, a State pair keeping its last good value. The plan report says `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with the id in `anira_plan_info`, the descriptor's flags in `engine_flags` and `ANIRA_BINDING_ENGINE` in every slot row of the plan; the extension rows of a registered engine's plan name it by its id, since the engine's `consumed_kinds` join the consumed-or-fail walk keyed by the id (its `model:` kinds read its own entries alone, its other kinds any host, while it is a candidate). Several registered rows on one variant are several plans (`anira_handler_set_plan` switches between them), resolved by row, never by the 2.x backend they map to. - **ABI (unstable):** `anira_contract_set_host_domain(contract, canonical, domain)` in `anira/abi/config.h` (ABI 0.2; 185 promised names), `[main-thread]`, common to both contract kinds like `anira_contract_set_edge_cost`: the declared host-end domain of one tensor, any tensor of either side, Streamed, Buffer, Static and State alike, by canonical name; `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST` for every tensor never set. It is the domain anira allocates the ring, the model tensor, the Static store and the state buffers of the slot in, and the domain all four processor phases, the feed and the capture work in; the planner joins it to the engine's domain per slot with the edge of the registry's rows, and the plan report's slot rows carry it as `domain_in` of an input and `domain_out` of an output against the engine's domain on the other side. A processor carries no domain of its own: the ring, the model tensor and the tensors the context accessors fill share the declared one, readable from the `domain` field of any tensor an accessor fills or from the plan row. Resolved at `anira_handler_prepare`: a name that matches no tensor is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, and in this pre-release any domain but `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST` is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` naming the tensor (the declaration is data; the runtime allocates in host memory only). `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL contract, a NULL or empty name, or a domain that is no `anira_domain` value. In a contract file the top-level key `"host_domains": {"<name>": "<domain word>"}`, beside `"hard"` or `"async"` like `edge_cost`, the words the lower-case suffixes of `anira_domain` (`"host"`, `"host_pinned"`, `"cuda"`, ...). C++: `anira::ContractHandle::host_domain(name, Domain)`.
- **ABI (unstable):** `anira_handler_set_static_input(handler, slot, tensor)` and `anira_handler_get_static_output(handler, slot, out)` in `anira/abi/handler.h` (ABI 0.2; 182 promised names), `[driver-thread]` and `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`: the whole-tensor entries of the Static tensors. A Buffer spec is not served this way: a Buffer tensor is a per-job payload that arrives with the Async contract, so `anira_handler_prepare` refuses a Buffer spec under a Hard contract with `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, naming the tensor (the spec stays valid in a model config and a model file; a persistent side input under a Hard contract is the Static role). The rule is the handler's: the 2.x bridge (`anira::v3compat::to_inference_config`) is unchanged and keeps carrying a Buffer tensor as a 2.x non-streamable tensor. A Static tensor has one description everywhere, the whole tensor in the spec's shape and dtype (any dtype, the spec's; a Channel axis of any extent), over one block of host memory read and written by its strides (all-zero strides are packed row-major). The handler keeps the values in a store of its own, one typed buffer per Static slot, sized and zeroed at `anira_handler_create`, never resized, untouched by `anira_handler_prepare` and `anira_handler_reset`, so both entries are legal on an unprepared handler, a value set before prepare survives it, and a processor's `prepare` may call them. Each slot has a single-writer sequence counter over atomic words: a reader never sees a torn tensor and the driving thread never waits (under a Hard contract both sides of a slot are the driving thread; a host that writes from another thread breaks the tag and can make the driving thread retry). The processor runner materialises every Static input into the model's input tensor when the chunk is formed, ahead of the processor's `pre_process` (where `anira_processor_input_tensor` reads it), and captures every Static output from the model's output tensor behind the processor's `post_process`; a chunk that completed as zeros (dropped, or failed in a processor or in the engine: `SessionElement::ThreadSafeStruct::m_completed_as_zeros`) and a failed `post_process` capture nothing, so the store holds what the model produced last. 'Fits' is a check, never a clamp: a slot out of range or a Streamed slot, another rank or extent (an empty tensor and a zeroed record included), NULL or misaligned memory, a negative stride, a stride of 0 on an axis longer than 1 unless every stride is 0, and `ANIRA_TENSOR_READ_ONLY` on the output are `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`; `ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR` or an unknown flag is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`; another dtype is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`; checked in the order slot, domain, flags, shape, dtype, memory, and every refusal but the NULL handler is recorded in `anira_handler_rt_error` with one latched record. `ANIRA_DOMAIN_HOST_PINNED` is accepted. A non-float32 spec is still refused by the validator (the built-in engines bind float only), so a store of another dtype is reachable through the handle body alone until the engine adapters arrive; `test/abi/test_HandlerStatic.cpp` covers an int32 and an int16 store that way.
- **ABI (unstable):** the role `ANIRA_ROLE_STATE = 3`, appended to `anira_role` in `anira/abi/enums.h`, and `anira_tensor_spec_set_state_source(spec, output_canonical)` in `anira/abi/config.h` (ABI 0.2; 183 promised names; `[main-thread]`, no `anira_error*`, the shape of `anira_tensor_spec_set_latency`): `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL spec, a NULL or empty name, or a spec whose role is not `ANIRA_ROLE_STATE`; the name is copied and resolved when the model is validated. `gen.py --diff-against` reports both as additions. `web/src/abi/enums.ts` and `exports_wasm.txt` follow.
- Declared state passing, by pointer over two buffers per pair. A model whose state is explicit (an output tensor that is the next inference's input: an exported GRU hidden state, a streaming-convolution cache) declares the pair in its model config and anira runs the feedback itself; no `before_inference` / `after_inference` processor is needed for it. Both halves carry the role State (`"role": "state"` in a model file) and the pairing is stated once, on the input: `"state_source": "<the output's canonical name>"`, or `anira_tensor_spec_set_state_source` (`state_source` on an output spec, or a value that is not a string, is `ANIRA_ERROR_JSON` with the key path; `anira_model_config_to_json` writes the key on the input half). The handler owns the state: two buffers per pair, on the port of the State input, each in the spec's shape and dtype and starting on a 64-byte boundary, both zeroed at `anira_handler_create` and at every `anira_handler_prepare`; the session and the 2.x scheduler know nothing of it. On the inference thread the processor runner binds the model's State input to the read buffer (the state the last inference produced) and the model's State output of the pair to the write buffer as the first step of an inference, ahead of the processor's `before_inference` (which sees the read side through `anira_processor_input_tensor` and may read or alter it), and flips the pair as the last step, behind the processor's `after_inference` (which sees the write side through `anira_processor_output_tensor`): what an inference wrote is what the next one reads, and anira copies nothing (the engine binds the two buffers as it binds every slot: ONNX Runtime in place both ways, LibTorch's input in place and its result copied out, ExecuTorch, TFLite and LiteRT copied both ways, a registered engine as its prepare bound them, the one the `anira.v2.custom` row runs on included). Both steps are field fills without an allocation or a lock. A failed inference (an engine failure, a non-OK `before_inference` or `after_inference`) does not flip, so the read side keeps the last good state; a dropped chunk never runs and does not advance it. The state is the handler's and not a session's or a processor's, so it survives `anira_handler_set_plan`. A State spec may stand anywhere in the inputs list and in the outputs list, so the lists follow the model file's own tensor order. Validation, at `anira_handler_create` and again at `anira_handler_prepare`, `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the tensor: a State input without a source; a source that names no output, or an output of another role; a State output named by no State input, or by two; a source on an output spec or on a spec of another role; two halves of unequal dtype or shape; a Time axis, a window, a time ratio or a latency on a State spec; a ring dtype or the anchor naming one. `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` in this pre-release: a transposed layout on a State spec (a view layout prepares). A pair of another dtype than float32 is legal (its buffers never travel through the queue, whose storage is float32 in this pre-release): a registered engine binds what its prepare accepts (an int32 pair runs), a built-in adapter refuses it at prepare with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the engine and the tensor. A Channel axis of any extent is legal, as on every non-Streamed spec. Initial values other than zeros and host-readable state are not part of this pre-release. `test/abi/test_State.cpp` runs the engine-free twin of the example models' `StatefulAccumulatorNetwork` against its closed form, the same accumulator as a registered C engine (the pointer identity of the two buffers through a processor's hooks, a failed inference that does not promote, a reset that zeroes the read side alone, the state surviving a plan switch between two registered engines, an int32 pair) and the bundled file on every engine of the build (the pointer identity on each, the closed form, and on ONNX Runtime the pair bound by name under other canonical names).
- The bundled model fixture `extras/models/model-pool/stateful_accumulator.model.json` / `.contract.json` (`k_stateful_accumulator_model_json` / `k_stateful_accumulator_contract_json` in `extras/models/model_files.h`): example-models' `StatefulAccumulatorNetwork` in stereo, the pin of `extras/fetch-models.cmake` moving to `d1bc5cf` (the commit that adds the model). A 64-sample stream with a declared State pair around it, `state_in` first in the inputs and `state_out` last in the outputs (`"role": "state"` on both, `"state_source": "state_out"` on the input; per channel the running sum and a block counter), so `processed_data = data + state_in[..., 0]` and the whole stream has a closed form. The file names rows for LibTorch, ONNX Runtime and ExecuTorch only: the TFLite export orders its outputs `[state_out, processed_data]` (the README of example-models; the input side keeps the declared order) and the engines bind tensors by position until they bind by name, so it names no `tflite` or `litert` row. `test/abi/test_State.cpp` runs the file on every engine of the build it names against the closed form, across host blocks above and below the hop, after `anira_handler_reset` and after the contract file's fixed warm-up (which runs inside the processor when it loads and reaches neither the state nor the stream); `test/abi/test_ExtrasFixtures.cpp` checks the file field by field, since the 2.x bridge refuses a State spec and there is no 2.x oracle.
- **C++ (not ABI-stable):** custom processors in `anira/anira.hpp`, over `anira/abi/processor.h`: `anira::Processor`, `anira::Processor::Prepared`, `anira::InitInfo`, `anira::PrepareInfo`, `anira::ProcessorContext`, `anira::RingView`, `anira::Pipeline::add_processor` and `anira::TensorSpec::state_source`. A processor is a subclass of `anira::Processor`, the registration, handed to a pipeline with `Pipeline::add_processor(std::make_shared<MyProcessor>())`; a `Pipeline` holds at most one (a second `add_processor` throws `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`). The registration states the phases it fills (`phases()`, pure virtual: an OR of `Processor::k_pre_process`, `k_post_process`, `k_before_inference` and `k_after_inference`, `Processor::phase_bit(phase)`, read once when the processor is added; only those phases reach the `anira_processor_desc`, the others stay NULL, which keeps the C rule exact: a processor of `post_process` alone leaves the default `pre_process` running, whatever it overrides), its real-time promise (`flags()`, `virtual`, default 0: the OR of `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST` and `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_HOOKS` that `add_processor` writes into `anira_processor_desc::flags`; under a Hard contract a processor that fills `pre_process` or `post_process` must promise the former or `anira_handler_prepare` refuses it with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` by name), `consumed_kinds()`, `init(const InitInfo&)` (`virtual`, the base body empty, may throw: once per `add_processor` of the processor, by the first `anira_handler_prepare` of a handler of that pipeline, on its caller's thread, before that handler's prepare, with the facts of the core in effect; what the processor keeps for its whole registration, a lookup table, a thread of its own, is built here as a member of the object; a throw fails that prepare with the mapping `prepare`'s throw gets, the registration counts as uninitialised and the next prepare calls `init` again) and `release()`; `prepare(const PrepareInfo&)` (pure virtual, may throw) is called once per `anira_handler_prepare` of each handler, after the plan report is built, and returns `std::unique_ptr<Processor::Prepared>`, the C lifecycle's per-handler `prepared` pointer as a class: the four phase functions `pre_process`, `post_process`, `before_inference` and `after_inference` (a `ProcessorContext&`, an `anira_status`, `noexcept`, no real-time attribute) and `reset(ProcessorContext&)` (`noexcept`; the first chunk of a new stream, before its `pre_process` and on its thread, the context answering the role only) are the `Prepared`'s virtuals, so what a processor keeps per handler, its per-entry scratch sized by `PrepareInfo::num_entries()`, is a member of the `Prepared` and never of the registration two handlers of one pipeline share. `PrepareInfo` is the shared record `anira_prepare_info` as views, the class an `Engine::Loaded`'s prepare receives too: `handler()` (the `anira_handler*`), `report()` (a `PlanReport`), `num_entries()`, `inputs()` / `outputs()` (`std::span<const Tensor>`, the templates), `input_names()` / `output_names()`, `flags()`, `exclusive()` (whether `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE` is set: the handler's inferences run one at a time and in order, with a reset at every stream start), `native()`; `InitInfo` is `anira_init_info` as views: `log_level()` (an `anira_log_level`), `num_threads()`, `context()` (the `anira_context*` of the handler whose prepare reached the registration first, valid until `init` returns), `native()`. Why the ownership spelling: the `Prepared` is polymorphic and lives on the heap; `prepare` returns a `std::unique_ptr` because anira is its one owner from then on (the trampoline releases it into the C `out_prepared` on success and deletes it at the C unprepare, once per successful prepare, at the next prepare of the handler or its destroy; a throw destroys nothing that was not returned; a null return is `ANIRA_ERROR_INTERNAL`), the registration is a `std::shared_ptr` because it is shared by the pipeline and every handler created from it. A processor that fills `pre_process` or `post_process` owns the phase for every slot and composes the default through the base class (`Processor::Prepared::pre_process(ctx)` is `anira_processor_default_pre_process`, `Processor::Prepared::post_process(ctx)` is `anira_processor_default_post_process`); the base `before_inference`, `after_inference` and `reset` do nothing, and the reset slot is always filled. A bit that names none of the four phases, a `flags()` bit the C header does not define, and a null processor, are `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` at `add_processor`. `ProcessorContext` is the `anira_processor_ctx` with the six context accessors on it, one C call per method: `phase()`, `engine()`, `provider()`, `variant()`, `num_inputs()`, `num_outputs()`, `ticket()`, `entry()` (the chunk's position in the handler's inference queue, below `anira_handler_num_entries`), and per slot `input_role(slot, Role&)` / `output_role`, `input_ring(slot, RingView&)` / `output_ring` and `input_tensor(slot, Tensor&)` / `output_tensor`, each returning the C status and filling its out-parameter (a refused ring leaves a `RingView` of no ring, which is false; asking for what the slot does not have in this phase is the processor's bug and is recorded, as in C). `RingView` is the ten `anira_ring` accessors over `std::span<T>`, the element type mapped onto its `anira_dtype` at compile time (float, double, bool, the fixed-width integers; nothing converts, so another type than the ring's moves nothing and returns 0 like the C entry); `pop_windows` refuses a span too short for its windows. Everything a phase function reaches is `noexcept`, allocates nothing and returns a status or a count. Lifetime: `add_processor` hands the pipeline's carrier a heap copy of the `std::shared_ptr<Processor>` as `user_data`, so the registration lives at least as long as the last pipeline or handler that carries it; `Processor::release()` runs once per `add_processor`, when that last carrier dies, after every `Prepared` of it was deleted, and the copy is deleted there (a refused `add_processor` deletes it at once and never calls `release`). A throw never crosses the C boundary: an `init` or a `prepare` that throws fails `anira_handler_prepare` with the status of an `anira::Error`, `ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` for `std::bad_alloc` and `ANIRA_ERROR_INTERNAL` for anything else, and `what()` goes to the log under the group `anira.hpp`, because the C callback carries no error record. `TensorSpec::state_source(output_canonical)` is `anira_tensor_spec_set_state_source`. Deviations from the design document's sketch, stated in the header: `Processor::prepare` takes a `PrepareInfo` whose handler is the `anira_handler*` (no handler class before the runtime cut-over) and returns the `Prepared`, `phases()` and `flags()`, `input_tensor` / `output_tensor` in place of `model_input` / `model_output` references, `entry()`, spans in place of pointer and count, no domain parameters and no single-callable form, one custom processor per pipeline. Gate `anira_header_cxx20` probes the classes (the registration abstract, the `Prepared` concrete, both non-copyable, every per-chunk virtual `noexcept`, `init` and `prepare` not; `AbiCxx.InitRunsOncePerRegistrationAndAThrowFailsThePrepare` runs the init rule through a C handler) and, on clang 20 or later, compiles with `-Werror=function-effects` and calls every method of `ProcessorContext` and `RingView` from an `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` function, so a method that allocates or calls a blocking function is a compiler error; API pages for the classes.
- **C++ (not ABI-stable):** custom engines in `anira/anira.hpp`, over `anira/abi/engine.h`: `anira::Engine`, `anira::Engine::Loaded`, `anira::Engine::Prepared`, `anira::EngineLoadInfo`, `anira::EngineContext`, the shared `anira::InitInfo` and `anira::PrepareInfo` (above) and `anira::Pipeline::add_engine`.
