An engine is a subclass of `anira::Engine`, the engine object, constructed with its reverse-URI id (`explicit Engine(std::string id)`, read back by `id()`), added to pipelines with `Pipeline::add_engine(std::make_shared<MyEngine>())`, and named by a model entry (`ModelConfig::add_model_path(id, path)`); the plan is then `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with the id, every slot `ANIRA_BINDING_ENGINE`. The object states its promise (`flags()`, `virtual`, default 0: the OR of `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_*` that `add_engine` writes into `anira_engine_desc::flags`, reported in `anira_plan_info::engine_flags`), `consumed_kinds()` (the walk keyed by the id), `init(const InitInfo&)` (`virtual`, the base body empty, may throw: once per C engine of the object, by the first `anira_handler_prepare` that reaches it, under the core's lifecycle lock, before the engine's first load, with the facts of the core in effect; what the engine keeps for its whole life, a device context, a thread pool, a weights cache, is built here as a member of the object; a throw fails that prepare, the C engine counts as uninitialised and the next prepare calls `init` again) and `release()`; the levels below are the C lifecycle's as classes. `load(const EngineLoadInfo&)` (pure virtual, may throw; `[main-thread]` under the lifecycle lock, so it may allocate, read files and log but must not call an entry that takes that lock) is called once per loaded model anira pools (two handlers running the same `Engine` object on an equal model configuration resolved to equal tensors share one, whichever `Pipeline`s they were created from, a stateful model included: it is loaded once, with no shared instance) and returns `std::unique_ptr<Engine::Loaded>`, the C `loaded` pointer as a class: the weights and the executors of the shared instances (`EngineLoadInfo::instances()` of them, never two calls at once on one) live there, and `load` binds the slots by the record's names, so every slot of the plan reports `ANIRA_BINDING_ENGINE`. `Loaded::prepare(const PrepareInfo&)` (pure virtual, may throw) is called once per handler that runs the model, after the plan report is built, with the record the processor's prepare receives, and returns `std::unique_ptr<Engine::Prepared>`, the C `prepared` pointer as a class: `process(EngineContext&)` (pure virtual, `noexcept`, an `anira_status`, no real-time attribute; on `ctx.instance()` of the `Loaded`, or on this object's own executor when `ctx.exclusive()`) and `reset(EngineContext&)` (`noexcept`, the base body empty; the first inference of a new stream of an exclusive handler, with that inference's context, right before its process; never for a handler whose model is stateless) are the `Prepared`'s virtuals; under `PrepareInfo::exclusive()` the handler's calls claim no shared instance, so the `Prepared` builds the executor they run on and what it keeps per stream, a stateless handler's `Prepared` routes its calls to the `Loaded`'s instances, and nothing an inference needs is a member of the `Engine`. `EngineLoadInfo` is `anira_engine_load_info` as views: `row()`, `model()` (the variant's `anira_model_config*`), `model_count()`, `model_engine(i)` (an `anira::EngineRef`, the pair), `model_path(i)`, `model_bytes(i)` (the config getters, `noexcept`, empty for what an entry has not; anira never opens a registered row's path, the engine's load does), `inputs()` / `outputs()` (`std::span<const Tensor>`, the engine-side templates), `input_names()` / `output_names()` (the name each slot binds to), `instances()` (0 for a model whose handlers are exclusive), `native()`. `EngineContext` is the `anira_engine_ctx` as a view: `instance()`, `entry()`, `ticket()`, `flags()`, `exclusive()` (whether `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE` is set), `loaded()` (the C loaded pointer: the `Engine::Loaded` the call runs on, which its `Prepared` holds already), `inputs()` (`std::span<const Tensor>`), `outputs()` (`std::span<Tensor>`), `native()`; every method `noexcept` and a field read, so a `process` composed of them and the `Tensor` reads is nonblocking (gate `anira_header_cxx20` calls them from an `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` function, under `-Werror=function-effects` on clang 20 or later). Lifetime and ownership: the object is the engine's identity, as `anira_custom_engine` is in C. Its first `add_engine` creates its C engine (`anira_custom_engine_create`, under the object's id) with a heap copy of the `std::shared_ptr<Engine>` as `user_data`, and every later addition of the same object, on another `Pipeline`, adds that same C engine while a `Pipeline` holding it lives (the object holds its C engine weakly, every `Pipeline` strongly, so nothing cycles; the plugin-instance idiom is one object per binary behind a function-local `std::weak_ptr`); the C engine keeps the object alive at least as long as the last pipeline or handler that carries it; `Engine::release()` runs once per C engine, when its last reference dies, after every `Loaded` of it was deleted, whether or not `init` ever ran, and an addition after that creates a fresh C engine; `load` and `prepare` return a `std::unique_ptr` because anira is their one owner from then on (the trampoline releases the `Loaded` into the C `out_loaded` and deletes it at the C unload, when the last handler holding the model is re-prepared or destroyed, after every `Prepared` over it; the `Prepared` into `out_prepared`, deleted at the C unprepare, once per successful prepare, at the handler's next prepare or its destroy, after its in-flight inferences drained); a throw never crosses the C boundary (from `init`, `load` and `prepare` alike: an `anira::Error` its status, `std::bad_alloc` `ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY`, anything else `ANIRA_ERROR_INTERNAL`, `what()` to the log under the group `anira.hpp`, a null return `ANIRA_ERROR_INTERNAL`), and the handler's prepare fails naming the engine and the level. `add_engine` throws `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a null engine before the C call and what `anira_custom_engine_create` and `anira_pipeline_add_engine` refuse (an id without a `.` or with the prefix `anira.`, a `flags()` bit `anira/abi/enums.h` does not define: `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, before the C engine exists; an engine whose id the pipeline already has: `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`), keeping no copy; a refusal after the call created the object's C engine drops that C engine again, and `release()` answers it. Gate `anira_header_cxx20` probes the classes (the three abstract, non-copyable and non-movable, `process` and `reset` `noexcept`, `init`, `load` and `prepare` not, the member-pointer type of `add_engine`, `EngineKind` the enum); `AbiCxx` runs a C++ engine end to end through a C handler (the records, the context, the plan report, the consumed extension under the id, the exception mapping of every level, one `Loaded` per pooled model and one `Prepared` per handler deleted once at the re-prepare or the destroy of its handler, `reset` at every new stream: `AThrowingEngineLoadOrPrepareFailsTheHandlersPrepare`, `InitRunsOncePerRegistrationAndAThrowFailsThePrepare`, `APreparedPerHandlerDiesWithItsUnprepareAndResetSeesEveryNewStream`, `OneEngineObjectOnTwoPipelinesSharesOneLoadedModel`); API pages for the classes.
- **ABI (unstable):** the read-back of `anira/abi/config.h` (ABI 0.2; 226 promised names): thirty-two `[main-thread]` entries that answer what the setters and the loaders stored, a file-loaded handle alike, appended after `anira_contract_from_json`. The tensor spec: `anira_tensor_spec_name`, `_dtype`, `_role`, `_ndim`, `_latency`, `_state_source` (value getters) and `_axis(spec, i, &tag, &extent)` (a hole below the rank reads `{ANIRA_AXIS_ANY, 0}`), `_window`, `_time_ratio` (status getters). The model config: `anira_model_config_num_inputs` / `_num_outputs`, `_input(config, i)` / `_output(config, i)` (the config's own copy of the spec, for the spec getters only, valid until the next `add_input` or `add_output` moves the specs), `_default_engine` (the pair: a status, the engine and its nullable id), `_state`, `_max_instances`, `_anchor`, `_tensor_name(config, model_index, canonical)`, `_model_ext(config, model_index, kind)` (the typed record of a known extension kind on one entry, set as a struct or as JSON; `NULL` for an unknown kind) as value getters, and `_tensor_layout(config, model_index, canonical, &count, axes)` with the count protocol of `anira_handler_get_latencies` (`NULL` axes asks for the length, a short array is `ANIRA_INCOMPLETE`, no record is count 0). The contract: `anira_contract_hard_geometry`, `_hard_budget`, `_hard_warmup`, `_hard_on_miss`, `_hard_miss_fn`, `_hard_wait_ratio`, `_hard_ring_dtype(contract, index, &canonical, &dtype)` (an enumeration in bytewise name order, beside the value getter `_hard_num_ring_dtypes`) as status getters answering `ANIRA_ERROR_WRONG_CONTRACT` on an Async contract, and `anira_contract_edge_cost` for both kinds. The context config: `anira_context_config_threads` and `anira_context_config_log(config, &desc)` (every log field in the `anira_log_desc` of the setter, written within its `struct_size`). A value getter answers a no-value constant for `NULL` (0, `NULL`, `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE`, a `_FORCE32` value) and takes no `anira_error`; a status getter writes its out-parameters on success only, after the checks in the order `NULL` handle, `NULL` out-parameter, contract kind, index. Tests: `test/abi/test_ReadBack.cpp` (suite `AbiReadBack`); gate 4 (`header_c.c`, `header_cxx17.cpp`) covers every shape.
- **ABI (unstable):** the declared stream latency of an output on a Hard contract (ABI 0.2; 229 promised names): `anira_contract_hard_set_latency(contract, canonical, samples)` names one output by canonical name and sets its stream latency in samples of that output, what `anira_handler_get_latency` / `_get_latencies` report for the slot and what its receive ring is primed with, replacing the figure anira computes (the adaptation, the queue, the wait credit and the model's internal latency); every output never named keeps the computed figure. `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` at the setter for a NULL contract, a NULL or empty name or a figure above `INT32_MAX`, `ANIRA_ERROR_WRONG_CONTRACT` on an Async contract; `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at `anira_handler_prepare` for a figure below the model's internal latency (the spec's `latency`), on an input, on a non-Streamed output and on a name that is no tensor. The read-back pair `anira_contract_hard_num_latencies` / `anira_contract_hard_latency(contract, index, &canonical, &samples)` (the enumeration shape of `_hard_ring_dtype`), and the contract-file key `"latencies": {"audio_out": 4096}` in the hard object (non-negative integers, at most `INT32_MAX`; a version 2 document carries none). It is what the 2.x custom latency of `InferenceHandler::prepare(host_config, custom_latency, ...)` did, minus the silent clamp to the internal latency, and what the compat shim's two custom-latency `prepare` overloads will stand on. Tests: `test_Handles`, `test_Json`, `test_Prepare` (`LatencyRulesAreConfigAtPrepare`: the four refusals, a two-output model whose declared slot reports 4096 while the other keeps its computed figure, and the 4096 primed zeros), `test_ReadBack`.
- **C++ (not ABI-stable):** the read-back in `anira/anira.hpp`, over the getters of `anira/abi/config.h`. `anira::SpecView`, a trivially copyable view of a tensor spec whose every read is `noexcept` (`name()`, `dtype()`, `role()`, `ndim()`, `axis(k)` as `{tag, extent}` with `{ANIRA_AXIS_ANY, 0}` for a hole and at or beyond `ndim()`, `window()` as `{min, max, overlap}`, `time_ratio()` as `{num, den}`, `latency()`, `state_source()`), handed out by `TensorSpec::view()` and by `ModelConfig::input_spec(i)` / `output_spec(i)` (the config's own copy, valid until the config is mutated, moved or destroyed). The `ModelConfig` getters `input_count()`, `output_count()`, `default_engine()` (an `anira::EngineRef`), `state()`, `max_instances()`, `anchor()` (`noexcept`), `tensor_name(i, canonical)`, `tensor_layout(i, canonical)` and `model_ext<Ext>(i)` (`std::optional<Ext>`; `detail::ExtTraits<ext::Entry>` gains `k_kind` and `read`), the indexed ones throwing `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` out of range like `model_path(i)`. `anira::Hard` gains `ring_dtypes` (`std::map<std::string, DType>`) and `latencies` (`std::map<std::string, uint32_t>`) between `wait_ratio` and `edge_cost`, still an aggregate, minted through `anira_contract_hard_set_ring_dtype` / `_set_latency`; `ContractHandle::hard_latency(canonical, samples)` beside `hard_ring_dtype`; and `ContractHandle::hard()` reads a Hard handle back into the aggregate, a loaded or legacy one alike, with `budget_value` rounded to the nanosecond (`std::chrono::round`: the handle stores double milliseconds, and a `duration_cast` would truncate 4.1 ms to 4099999 ns), throwing `ANIRA_ERROR_WRONG_CONTRACT` on an Async handle. The alpha.1 deviations that a handle could not be read back into a `Hard` aggregate (`take_legacy_contract`, a loaded contract file) are closed. Tests: `test_ReadBack.cpp` (suite `AbiReadBackCxx`), `test_anira_hpp`'s `ContractHandleMintsAHardAggregate` reads through `hard()`; gate 4 (`header_cxx20.cpp`) pins the view, the getters, the maps and a designated initializer that names them.
- **ABI (unstable):** the engine word's additions (ABI 0.2). A default provider beside the default engine: `anira_model_config_set_default_provider(config, provider, provider_id)` (a provider of the enum, or `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` with a custom name, the pair rule; `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` sets none; `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL config, an unknown provider or a pair against the rule) with the read-back `anira_model_config_default_provider(config, &provider, &provider_id)`, and the key `"default_provider"` of a model file beside `"default_engine"`, spelled as an entry's `"provider"`: the handler starts on the first plan in plan-table order of the default engine (of any engine without one) on the default provider, so a default engine with several plans no longer starts on the first of them. One rule for both defaults: what the configuration alone decides is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at `anira_handler_create` (a default engine naming no model entry, as before; a default provider no entry of the default engine could run, every one pinned to another provider: "default_provider 'x' runs no model entry ..."); when no plan runs the default here (the engine not in this build or not a candidate, the provider not a candidate or not usable here) the handler starts on the default engine's first plan, else on plan 0, and `anira_handler_prepare` logs one Warning naming what was asked and the plan it starts on (new for the default engine too, which fell back silently). The processor context hands out both pairs: `anira_processor_engine(ctx, &engine, &engine_id)` and `anira_processor_provider(ctx, &provider, &provider_id)` (`[callback-safe]`, `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING`; the id NULL beside every value but CUSTOM and its out-parameter nullable; a NULL value out-parameter is refused and latched like the six slot accessors') read the record's value fields and two of its pointer slots, `engine_id` and `provider_id` (were `reserved_ptr0` / `reserved_ptr1`; `anira_processor_ctx` stays 64 bytes with every offset unchanged). `anira_edge_info.to_engine_id`, a tail field after `to_provider_id` (`ANIRA_EDGE_INFO_INIT` gains its NULL): the custom engine's id on the rows of `anira_pipeline_capabilities_edge`, NULL for a built-in engine; a caller whose header ends before it reads the rows without it.
- **C++ (not ABI-stable):** `ModelConfig::default_provider(provider)` and `default_provider(name)` (a custom provider by its name) with the getter `default_provider()`; the pin getter `ModelConfig::model_provider(i)` beside `model_engine(i)` (the setter `model_provider(i, provider)` existed; `model_provider(i, name)` pins to a custom provider by its name); every getter of a pair returns it whole, `anira::EngineRef{kind, id}` / `anira::ProviderRef{kind, id}` (trivially copyable aggregates; `id` empty beside every value but CUSTOM): `ModelConfig::model_engine(i)`, `default_engine()`, `model_provider(i)`, `default_provider()`, `EngineLoadInfo::model_engine(i)` and `provider()`, `ProcessorContext::engine()` and `provider()` over the two processor accessors.
- **Compat (C++, header-only):** `anira/compat/v2.hpp`, the 2.x source-compatible face of anira over the 3.x C ABI: `namespace anira::v2`, C++20 over `anira/anira.hpp` and the `anira/abi` headers, nothing of the 2.x tree, the names of v2.3.0. A 2.x host includes it in place of `<anira/anira.h>`, adds `using namespace anira::v2;` and runs on the C handler. The configuration half: `InferenceBackend` under the 2.x names with the `anira_engine` values (every enumerator in every build; `to_engine` / `to_backend` / `names` / `is_available`; `CUSTOM` is `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with `k_custom_engine_id`, `"anira.v2.custom"`), `ModelData`, `TensorShape`, `ProcessingSpec`, `InferenceConfig` (a copyable value whose 2.x constructors write a version-2 document and load it through `anira::ModelConfig::from_json`, the one upgrade path, then read the 2.x fields and getters back from the handles; the backend-qualified shape getter applies the entry's axis layout; `model_config()`, `model_config_copy()`, `hard()`), `JsonConfigLoader`, `ContextConfig` (`to_context_config()`), `HostConfig`, `LogConfig` and the log enums. The processor half: `RingBuffer` and `BufferF` as views of a slot's ring and model tensor, `PrePostProcessor` with the 2.x virtuals, bodies, atomics and helpers, `LegacyProcessor` (a 2.x processor as the `anira::Processor` of a 3.x pipeline), `PassthroughEngine` (the 2.x `BackendBase::process` as an `anira::Engine` under `anira.v2.custom`) and the `Context` statics. The handler: `InferenceHandler` over one `anira_handler` built from a private copy of the model config, the custom engine (the caller's `anira::Engine&` or the `PassthroughEngine`) and the `LegacyProcessor`, with every 2.x member under its 2.x signature: `prepare` over the legacy contract with the host geometry and the reference tensor as the anchor (an unchanged prepare only resets the stream), the two custom-latency forms over `anira_contract_hard_set_latency`, the process, push and pop forms over planar float tensors and the `_multi` entries and their `_wait` twins, the non-streamable values through the processor's atomics, `set_inference_backend` over the plan report; `native()` and `rt_error()` are additions. What differs from 2.x (errors are `anira::Error`; nothing loads before `prepare`; single forms carry one slot; `push_data` never waits; the engine code is the host's; the not-source-compatible members) is the compat page of `docs/sphinx/migration.rst`. Tests: `test/abi/test_Compat.cpp` (suites `AbiCompatConfig`, `AbiCompat`, `AbiCompatForm`), the differential proof against the live 2.x classes on the same arguments (every getter, the loader, every plan through the five forms, the extras' CNN and HybridNN processors copied verbatim, the Static values, misses, latencies, waits) and the shim's own rules; the consumer gates `anira_header_compat` (the shim alone under the strict flags, `-Werror=function-effects` on clang 20 or later over an `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` probe) and `anira_header_compat_with_v2` (beside `<anira/anira.h>` in both orders); the install consumer `consumer_compat`. The api pages of every `anira::v2` class and of `anira::SpecView`, `anira::EngineRef` and `anira::ProviderRef` are in `docs/sphinx/api`.
- **ABI (unstable):** fractional block sizes on the Hard contract. The geometry's `block_min` and `block_max` are doubles, so a model with no stream at the host rate (every stream slower than one sample per host block) gets a fractional anchor block, the 2.x `samplesPerBlock / 2048.f` idiom: `contract.hard_geometry(samples_per_block / 2048.0, samples_per_block / 2048.0, sample_rate / 2048.0)`. The rules, in `anira_contract_create_hard`, `anira_contract_hard_set_geometry` and the contract file's `block_min` / `block_max` (any JSON number now): both blocks 0 (unset) or both finite and > 0, `block_min <= block_max`, a rate of 0 or more; a NaN, infinite, negative or half-zero geometry is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` (`ANIRA_ERROR_JSON` in a file) with a message naming the value. The block reaches the 2.x core's float `HostConfig`, from which `LatencyCalculator::rationalize` recovers the rational the host meant (1/3 and 1/4 exactly); a flexible window under a fractional `block_max` is pinned from the anchor hop like any other (Changed, below). The per-call rule is the latency calculation's accounting: by its k-th call the host has moved `floor(k * b)` samples of a stream whose block is `b`, so a call carries `floor(b)` or `ceil(b)` (0 or 1 under 0.25); a larger count is refused (Changed, below), and a pop within the bound that the stream cannot cover is `ANIRA_MISSED` (documented in `anira/abi/handler.h`, `usage.rst` section 1.3 *Fractional blocks*, `latency.rst`). Tests: `AbiContract.TheGeometryRules`, `AbiJsonContract.FractionalGeometry`, `AbiTranslate.AFractionalGeometryIsTheCalculatorsRational` and `AFractionalBlockPinsTheFlexibleWindowFromTheAnchorHop`, `AbiHandlerTensor.AFractionalBlockMovesASampleOnceAWholeOneHasAccumulated` and `AHostAheadOfTheFractionalBlockStarves`, `AbiCompat.AFractionalBlockMatchesTheOracle`.
- **ABI tooling:** the registry knows platform headers. A header with `platform: emscripten` (`file: anira_<name>.h`, its own `guard:`) is generated into `include/anira/` beside the umbrellas, its body inside `#ifdef __EMSCRIPTEN__` (an empty companion on every other target, so a native consumer may include it); it declares functions, callbacks, typedefs and defines only, and no header may include it. Its names go to the new `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` and to `web/src/abi/exports_wasm.txt`, never to `abi/symbols-0.txt`, `abi/symbols-draft.txt`, the link probe or the layout test's include list; its defines reach `web/src/abi/enums.ts`, and `abi/anira.json` records the key. Its first user is `anira/anira_em.h` (below), whose nine names make up `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt`.
- **ABI (unstable):** `anira/anira_em.h`, the Emscripten companion of the C ABI (registry `platform: emscripten`, ABI 0.2, ten names in the new `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` and in `web/src/abi/exports_wasm.txt`; an empty header on every other target). JavaScript reaches processors, engines and the log through the registry of the scope that runs the C call, `globalThis[Symbol.for('anira.registry')]` (`{ version, processor(Module, key, phase, a, b), engine(Module, key, phase, a, b, idPtr), log(Module, recordPtr), boot(Module) }`, `ANIRA_EM_HOOKS_VERSION` 1), called with the instance's `Module`: `anira_em_processor_desc_init(desc, js_key, hook_mask, processor_flags)` fills an `anira_processor_desc` whose slots are C trampolines into the registry's `processor`, one per bit (`ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_PRE` 1, `_POST` 2, `_BEFORE` 4, `_AFTER` 8, `_PREPARE` 16, `_RELEASE` 32, `_RESET` 64, `_UNPREPARE` 128, `_INIT` 256) and whose flags are the stated `processor_flags`, never derived from the bits (a Hard contract's prepare refuses a pre/post/reset hook without `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST`), and `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_processor(pipeline, js_key, hook_mask, processor_flags, err)` adds it; `anira_em_engine_desc_init(desc, js_key, hook_mask, engine_flags)` fills an `anira_engine_desc` whose `process` (always), `reset` and `query` (`ANIRA_EM_ENGINE_HOOK_RESET` 1, `_QUERY` 2) cross into the registry's `engine`, its flags the stated `engine_flags`, and `anira_em_pipeline_register_js_engine(pipeline, engine_id, js_key, hook_mask, engine_flags, err)` creates the engine under its id with the flags JavaScript cannot write, adds it and drops the handle. The trampolines keep `{js_key, engine id}` in a heap record freed by the descriptor's `release`; a hook that throws is caught in its thunk, reported with `console.error` (rate-limited), and fails the call (`ANIRA_ERROR_UNKNOWN` for a processor, `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` for an engine), so no JavaScript exception unwinds through a C frame. `anira_em_set_log_hook(enable)` registers the registry's `log` as a log sink beside the C sinks. The threads and the audio path have their own entries (below). Implemented in `src/platform/emscripten/em_hooks.cpp`, compiled into `anira` on the Wasm binary format only. The generator wraps a platform header's body in `#if defined(__EMSCRIPTEN__) || defined(DOXYGEN)`, so the reference documents it from any host.
- **ABI gates on WebAssembly:** the option `ANIRA_WITH_ABI_TESTS` (default OFF) adds `test/abi` alone, the one test option `cmake/validate-options.cmake` allows on WebAssembly, and the configure and build preset `wasm-abi` (`wasm-release` plus the option, `build/wasm/Abi`) builds AniraWeb and the gates together. On Wasm `anira_header_c11` / `anira_header_cxx17` compile every header, `anira/anira_em.h` with its declarations, as members of ALL; `anira_abi_layout` is a node executable whose table `cmake/abi-layout-check.cmake` (new `LAYOUT_RUNNER`) diffs against `abi/layout-0.txt`; `anira_em_smoke` (`test/abi/em_smoke.c` with the hook object of `test/abi/em_smoke.pre.js`) checks the hook version, a stack and thread-local block installed with `anira_em_init_instance`, a throwing hook caught in its trampoline, a JavaScript processor (pre/post/reset/init/prepare/unprepare/release) and a JavaScript engine (process doubles its input; a second engine's query drops a provider from the capabilities) on one Hard handler driven by `anira_inference_thread_execute`, a pre_process hook registered without `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST` refused at the Hard prepare (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`), the JavaScript log hook, and the link's thread-local block within 1024 bytes (552 in the smoke's link of anira, tanh-lib and ONNX Runtime; the digest's probe P5 measured 553 on a link of the tree before this pull request, with the probe's own object; the two figures come from two different links, and the gate holds the smoke's link to the budget). The target `anira_abi_wasm_gates` runs both under node. `build_web` builds `wasm-abi`, runs the gates and checks that `web/wasm/AniraWeb.d.ts` exports every name of `abi/symbols-0.txt`, `abi/symbols-draft.txt` and `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` (plus `_malloc`, `_free`) before the npm build. Natively `header_c.c` and `header_cxx17.cpp` include `anira/anira_em.h`, an empty companion there, and the per-file self-containment wrappers cover it on every leg.
- **ABI gates on WebAssembly:** `anira_em_smoke_thread` (`test/abi/em_smoke_thread.c`, decision 9.12), a user-driven inference thread for real: the test creates a Wasm Worker of its own (`emscripten_malloc_wasm_worker`) on the module's memory, which runs `anira_inference_thread_run_loop` while main pushes blocks through a Hard handler with `anira_handler_process_wait` (a C engine doubles them; 15 of 16 at the handler's latency), then the stop: `has_exited` within 100 ms (the parked loop woken by the stop's notify), the teardown. `anira_abi_wasm_gates` runs it after `anira_em_smoke`.
- **Web CI:** a browser test of the built package. `build_web` installs Playwright 1.64.1 with headless Chromium and Firefox in the job and runs `web/test/browser/run.mjs` in both: pages served with COOP/COEP headers. `index.html` checks `crossOriginIsolated`, `Anira.create()` and that a refused call throws its `AniraError`, runs a JavaScript engine that doubles its input times a Static gain on a pool of two Wasm Workers (`pool-worker.js`) and a JavaScript processor in the worklet (`worklet-processor.js`), renders an `OfflineAudioContext` through the handler's audio node (`waitMs`, since an offline render outruns real time) with the gain an AudioParam automated mid-render, requires the output to be the input times 2 * gain after the handler's latency, serves a tensor Static input through `setStaticInput` on a second handler, and stops the pool. `config.html` loads `extras/models/model-pool/gain.model.json` and its contract unchanged and runs them on the built-in ONNX Runtime engine, `ort.html` runs the ONNX Runtime Web engine; the runner then opens the example's check page, built and under Vite's dev server. Locally: `npm install --no-save playwright@1.49.1 && npx playwright install chromium firefox && node test/browser/run.mjs [firefox]` after `npm run build && npm run examples` and `cmake -P extras/fetch-models.cmake`. Firefox's `OfflineAudioContext` cannot `suspend`, so the checks schedule changes as automation instead.
- **Web:** the audio node serves the model's Static inputs: a float32 scalar as an AudioParam of the node (`params`; the C callback pushes its k-rate value each quantum it changed), any shape and dtype as a seqlock block in the shared heap (`staticInputs`, `audioNode.setStaticInput(slot, values)`, `anira_em_audio_node_set_static_input`): the write makes the word odd, writes, makes it even; before each quantum's process call the callback copies a block whose word is even, unchanged across the copy and changed since its last push, and hands the copy to `anira_handler_set_static_input`, so a torn value is never pushed and the handler keeps the last good one until the next quantum. The callback never waits and allocates nothing for it.
- **Web:** a complete example with a TypeScript processor and engine, `web/examples/processor-and-engine/`: `gain-processor.ts` (`INPUT_GAIN_PROCESSOR`, `defineProcessor`: `prepare` / `unprepare` on the main thread allocate its per-handler memory, `preProcess` in the worklet scales the model's audio tensor by the Static input `gain`, ramping from the previous block's gain, allocation-free; `reset` forgets the ramp), `double-engine.ts` (`defineEngine`, a doubling engine standing in for the model, the context's worker module), `main.ts` (the module, the context with its worker module, the model config, the pipeline with `addEngine` and `addProcessor`, the handler with a Hard contract of one render quantum, the audio node with the processor's worklet module, the teardown) and `vite.config.ts` (the worker and worklet modules as one file each through `?worker&url`, the COOP/COEP headers). `npm run examples` type-checks it with `strict` against the built package's types and builds it, `build_web` runs that, and the browser test renders its `check.ts` page offline with an automated gain (0 error up to float32 rounding). The guides include its files (`literalinclude`). Beside it `web/examples/model-file/`, the page of the basic-usage guide: `main.ts` loads `extras/models/model-pool/gain.model.json` and its contract unchanged and runs them on the built-in ONNX Runtime engine through the audio node, `check.ts` renders it offline with the model's Static gain automated mid-render (0 error), its `vite.config.ts` serves the repository's `extras/models` at `/extras/models/` to `vite` and `vite preview`; `npm run examples` builds both examples and the browser test runs both check pages.

### Changed

- **Examples:** the JUCE and CLAP plugins declare the host's real block range on the Hard contract instead of a fixed block: the JUCE plugin `hard_geometry(1, samplesPerBlock, sampleRate)` in every variant, both handlers of variant 7 included (JUCE names the largest block only), the CLAP plugin `hard_geometry(max(1, minFrameCount), maxFrameCount, sampleRate)`. Every call already carried its own block. The reported latency, which both report to the host, covers every block size of the range: at 48 kHz and a 512-sample maximum the CNN reports 4602 samples instead of 3584, SimpleGainNetwork 1024 instead of 512, GuitarLSTM (the CLAP plugin's model) 767 instead of 512. Under a fixed contract, a host sequence of varying blocks missed 314 of 480 blocks of SimpleGainNetwork and 159 of GuitarLSTM. `examples.rst` and `usage.rst` (section 4.1) describe the range and its price. The benchmarks keep fixed blocks: they choose the block themselves.
- **Benchmarks:** the advanced benchmark selects its model config and its contract in one call: `model_and_contract(model, buffer_size)` returns both (`ModelAndContract`, the contract loaded from the model's contract file), replacing `model_config_of`, whose name said half and which handed the contract file back through an out-parameter. `docs/sphinx/benchmarking.rst` shows the new shape. The simple and cnn-size benchmarks and the fixture had no such out-parameter.
- **The core on the 3.x types (internal; no behaviour change, every figure, stream and copy-path golden as before):**
  - **The processor runs on the chunk.** `Core::pre_process`, the inference thread and `Core::post_process` call the handler's `capi::ProcessorRunner` with the chunk itself, and each phase returns its status; the 2.x `PrePostProcessor` leaves the session, `Core` and the inference thread, with the runner's struct table and its lookup by buffer address, the chunk's status field and the per-claim re-pointing of the descriptors. The plan's 2.x backend leaves the core with it (`PlanRequest` / `PlanSlot::m_legacy_backend`, `SessionElement::plan_backend`).
  - **The chunk's storage is anira's:** one allocation per chunk, every slot's buffer on a 64-byte boundary, the descriptors built once by the session's prepare; a State slot has no buffer (its pair is bound per inference). The 2.x `BufferF` vectors of the chunk are gone.
  - **The ring of a Streamed slot lives in its stream port** (`capi::StreamPort::m_ring`), sized in place by every session prepare; the session reads it through the port vectors (`SessionElement::send_ring` / `receive_ring`), and `m_send_buffer` / `m_receive_buffer` with the placeholder rings of the Static and State slots are gone.
  - **The logger's level is `anira_log_level`** (D11): `anira::set_log_level` / `get_log_level` / `to_thl_log_level` and the ONNX Runtime and LiteRT mappings take the C enum, `default_log_level()` moves into `anira/utils/Logger.h` (`ANIRA_LOG_ERROR` in a release build, `ANIRA_LOG_INFO` otherwise, as before), and `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO`'s count is `anira::Core::default_num_threads()`, beside `sanitize_config`.
  - **The session reads the 3.x configuration directly; the translation into the 2.x objects is gone** (`src/capi/v3_to_v2.{h,cpp}`). The C handler installs the contract snapshot (`m_contract`) and what the validator derived for its variant (`m_derived`, a `capi::Derived`, which now carries `m_exclusive`) before it builds the session, and the session, `Core`, the manager, the inference thread and the latency calculation read them, with the model config, by reference: the extents, hop and channel count of every tensor, the anchor, the exclusivity, the contract's geometry, budget and wait ratio. The handler's `m_inference_config` and `m_host_config` are gone. `SessionElement` and `InferenceManager` take the model config, the derivation and the Hard contract, `SessionElement::prepare` / `Core::prepare_session` / `InferenceManager::prepare` take no host configuration, and `LatencyCalculator` takes `(const capi::Derived&, const capi::HardContract&, uint32_t num_instances)` (the scheduler headers forward-declare the private types). The session's instance count is resolved by `Core::create_session` into the session (`m_num_instances`; 1 for an exclusive session, as before) instead of being written back into the caller's configuration. The geometry stays double from the contract to the core: the host block in hops comes from the contract's own `block_max` (the float narrowing of the 2.x `HostConfig` goes), and a slot's block in samples (`LatencyCalculator::block_samples`, the most one call carries and the held block of `ANIRA_MISS_HOLD_LAST`) from that rational alone. Every figure of the suites is unchanged, the fractional geometries included. `validate`'s `backend_of` is `engine_enabled`, the missing-geometry refusal moved from the translator into the handler's prepare (same status, same message), and `test_Translate` is `test_Validate`, which checks what the validator derives instead of the 2.x objects made from it.
- **Tests: the pins the 2.x suites held come back on the 3.x core.**
  - `CoreLifecycleTest.AutoThreadsAreHalfTheHardwareThreadsAtLeastOne`: `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO` against the closed form over `std::thread::hardware_concurrency()` (`Core::default_num_threads`), the prospective pool size and the pool a first session builds (the 2.x `CoreConfigValues.PlatformDefaults` held the formula).
  - `ContextConfigMismatchTest.TheCoreClampsAQueueCapacityOutsideItsRange`: a configuration that reaches the core with a log-queue capacity of 10 or 100000 gets a queue of 64 or 65536 records and the warning naming both figures; 64 is not reported. One fresh core per figure (`release_core_if_idle`).
  - `ExtrasFixtures.ThePrepareResolvesTheFilesInstanceCount`: the bundled files leave the instance count to anira, and a prepare on a pool of three threads resolves it to 1 on the stateful RNN (an exclusive session) and to 3 on the gain model.
- **Tests: the arms that ended with the 2.x runtime, or compared the 3.x side with itself, check an independent oracle again.**
  - `anira_test::EngineOracle` (`test/support/engine_oracle.h`) reloads the record of a C handler's plan on an executor of its own and runs the model directly in the engine room on windows built by hand (`receptive_window`, `batched_windows`): the oracle of a neural model, which has no closed form.
  - `AbiHandler.TheCnnStreamIsTheModelOnItsWindows` streams the steerable-nafx CNN through a C handler with no processor on every plan and expects the latency priming, then per block the CNN on its 15380-sample window. It replaces `CnnMatchesTheTwoPointXHandler`, whose ending claimed a CNN case in `test_InferenceHandler`, which runs GuitarLSTM.
  - `AbiCompat.CnnMatchesThroughTheUsersProcessor` and `HybridNnMatchesThroughTheUsersProcessor` check the C handler's whole stream against the oracle as well as against the shim.
  - `AbiHandlerTensor.AnInterleavedBlockMatchesAPlanarBlockOnEveryPlan` checks both layouts against the closed form (the input delayed by the latency, gain 1).
  - `AbiProcessor.NoProcessorRunsTheWindowFillEndToEnd` (a window-sum engine, hand-computed stream) and `PopWindowsLaysOutEveryWindowWithItsHistory` (a hand-computed layout with an offset and four batches) replace the ended `NoProcessorEqualsTheV2Default`, `TheCnnProcessorIsTheDefault` and `PopWindowsEqualsTheBatchedPop`. The default fill's unit cases (`DefaultPreProcessFillsTheWindowHeadWithHistory`, `DefaultPreProcessTreatsAMultichannelTensorAsChannels`) are the C twins of the deleted 2.x ones.
  - Each oracle fails on a window shifted by one sample.
- **Tests: the white-box suites leave the 2.x runtime** (the core on the 3.x types).
  - **The copy-path recordings run on C handlers' own sessions** (`anira_test::oracle::HandlerSession`, `test/support/copy_oracle.h`). `CopyPathOracle` and `TensorStems` drive the tensor stems of a C handler's `InferenceManager` directly, below the C entries and their block rule, over the C twins of the 2.x configurations the transcripts were recorded on. `NonStreamableRouter` keeps the Static values in the handler's Static ports, and `PlanarFloatAdapter` is sized from the ports. `AbiHandlerCopyOracle.MatchesTheV2CopyPath` is now `MatchesTheManagerCopyPath`, with the same stems of a C handler as its referee. The 2.x `GateBackend` and the gates built over it are gone. Both goldens are unchanged.
  - **The scheduler suites run on C handlers.** `test/support/session_cases.h` describes a case by its slots (elements, channels, hop), a geometry, the budget, the wait ratio and the instances. The sizing and latency tables of `SessionElement` and `InferenceManager` are written this way, and the lifecycle, failure, plan-switch, one-sided, pooling, ordering and user-thread suites run the same way; every expected figure is unchanged. `test_CoreConfigMismatch` is renamed `test_ContextConfigMismatch`. A few cases went with the 2.x paths they tested:
    - the roundtrip fallback for a backend without a model;
    - `set_backend` on a table without that backend, and the 2.x default plan table;
    - the manager's clamp of a custom latency below the model's own (the C prepare refuses that contract; `RefusesAFigureBelowTheInternalModelLatency`, formerly `ClampsToInternalModelLatency`, asserts the refusal and that the internal latency itself is accepted);
    - the empty plan table (the validator refuses a pipeline with no plan).
  - **The logger and ExecuTorch entry-point suites run on C handlers too.** `anira_test::Instance` (a context and a prepared handler over the mono pass-through) is shared by every suite that only needs a live session. `ExecuTorchModelFunction` names the method through the model entry's `entry` extension.
  - **The C ABI suites no longer use the 2.x handler as scaffolding.** `test_Context`, `test_Log`, `test_Firewall` and `test_Thread` get their live session from `anira_test::Instance` or a C handler. `test_ExtrasFixtures` compares what the validator derives for every bundled file (the shapes, the TensorFlow layout, the channels, hops and latencies, the budget, the warm-up, the state) with the 2.x fixture literals, with no 2.x `InferenceConfig` involved.
  - **Every differential arm carries its 3.x expectation beside the comparison with the 2.x runtime.** `anira_test::DelayLine` is the closed form of a sample-wise stream (the processed input behind the reported latency), and `anira_test::gain_peak` is the gain model's Static output. `test_Handler`'s oracle and `test_Compat`'s runtime arms check every block against them, and the latencies against literals of the closed form. `test_Compat`'s configuration half checks every getter against its value computed from the constructor's arguments (`expect_getters_of`), and the loader against the values of the documents.
  - **The 2.x runtime leaves the tests.** `test/support/v2_handler.h` (the 2.x handler the white-box suites and the arms drove), `test/support/v2_objects.h` and `test/support/extras_v2_processors.inc` are deleted, and no test builds a 2.x session any more. The arms keep their 3.x side: `test_Handler`'s oracle checks the closed form only, and `test_Compat`'s runtime half drives the shim's handler alone (`Shim`, `KernelShim`). `AnInterleavedBlockMatchesAPlanarBlockOnEveryPlan` compares two C handlers of the stereo gain model, one fed interleaved and one planar. `CnnMatchesThroughTheUsersProcessor` and `HybridNnMatchesThroughTheUsersProcessor` run the extras' two 2.x processors once, as a 2.x user's program on the shim, against a C handler with the default fill and with the extras' `HybridNNProcessor`. Ended with their 2.x side: `CnnMatchesTheTwoPointXHandler` (no closed form; the CNN runs in `test_InferenceHandler` and the examples), `PopWindowsEqualsTheBatchedPop` (`test_RingBuffer` pins `pop_windows`), `NoProcessorEqualsTheV2Default` and `TheCnnProcessorIsTheDefault` (the default fill is pinned by `HistoryFillsTheWindowHead` and `MultichannelTensorIsNotAWindow`). The router and the float adapter keep the C handler's store only.
- **Examples:** lean over `anira.hpp`.
  - **Backend choice (JUCE and CLAP):** the parameter listener stores the choice in an atomic. `processBlock` / `process` applies it through `PlanReport::find` and `set_plan`, over the report of the last prepare (nothing changes when the build or the model has no such plan). This replaces the plan tables built at prepare.
  - **Pipeline:** one chained expression, `Pipeline().add_engine(...).add_inference(...).add_processor(...)`, in the handler's initializer.
  - **JUCE variant 1:** gives each model entry the embedded file named like its path in one loop over `BinaryData::namedResourceList`; there is no per-engine switch any more.
  - **JUCE variant 7:** the encoder and the decoder are two instances of one small struct, a model with its contract, handler and plan report.
  - **Benchmarks:** the cnn-size and advanced benchmarks run one candidate per run (plan 0). The simple benchmark finds its ONNX Runtime plan with `PlanReport::find`. The fixture's `ProcessBlockFixture::plan_of` is gone.
  - **Web:** `web/examples/model-file` picks its plan in the report of every prepare (the first and the device change's re-prepare) with `PlanReport.find` and `InferenceHandler.setPlan`; both web examples set the page's `AudioContext` options where they create it, without a one-call helper.
- **Breaking (C ABI, pre-release surface; decision 10.18):** the pipeline's custom stage is a *processor*. `anira/abi/stage.h` is `anira/abi/processor.h` (guard `ANIRA_ABI_PROCESSOR_H`); every `anira_stage_*` name is `anira_processor_*` (`anira_processor_desc`, `anira_processor_ctx`, `anira_processor_fn` and the `_prepare_fn` / `_unprepare_fn` / `_reset_fn` / `_init_fn` / `_release_fn` typedefs, the accessors `anira_processor_input_role` / `_output_role` / `_input_ring` / `_output_ring` / `_input_tensor` / `_output_tensor` / `_engine` / `_provider`, the defaults `anira_processor_default_pre_process` / `_post_process`), every `ANIRA_STAGE_*` name `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_*` (`ANIRA_PROCESSOR_DESC_INIT`, `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST`, `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_HOOKS`; `ANIRA_STRUCT_STAGE_CTX` is `ANIRA_STRUCT_PROCESSOR_CTX`), and `anira_pipeline_add_stage` is `anira_pipeline_add_processor`; values, layouts and signatures are unchanged. The phases (`ANIRA_PHASE_*`) keep their names, and "stage" stays the generic word (the inference stage). The words anira writes follow: the consumer column of a processor's `anira_plan_ext` rows reads `"processor"`, and its records and messages say "the processor". In `anira/anira_em.h` the processor hook bits are `ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_*` (the engine hooks keep `ANIRA_EM_ENGINE_HOOK_*`), `anira_em_stage_desc_init` is `anira_em_processor_desc_init`, `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_stage` is `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_processor` (and its sibling `anira_em_pipeline_register_js_engine` is `anira_em_pipeline_add_js_engine`, as the pipeline's members say add), and the registry method the module calls is `processor` (was `stage`). No deprecation shims: `stage.h` came after 3.0.0-alpha.1. Migration: rename the identifiers (`s/anira_stage_/anira_processor_/`, `s/ANIRA_STAGE_/ANIRA_PROCESSOR_/`, `anira_pipeline_add_stage` → `anira_pipeline_add_processor`, `#include <anira/abi/processor.h>`).
- **Breaking (C++, `anira.hpp`; decision 10.18):** `anira::Stage` is `anira::Processor` (`Stage::Prepared` → `Processor::Prepared`, `StageContext` → `ProcessorContext`; `PrepareInfo` and `InitInfo` are the records processors and engines share and keep their names), and the pipeline has one way to be filled, one member per C entry named as the entry without `anira_pipeline_`: `Pipeline::add_inference(model, {candidates})` (and the variants overload), `Pipeline::add_processor(std::shared_ptr<Processor>)` and `Pipeline::add_engine(std::shared_ptr<Engine>)` (was `register_engine`), each returning the pipeline. The extras' `HybridNNStage` is `HybridNNProcessor` (`extras/models/hybrid-nn/HybridNNProcessor.h`), the compat shim's `anira::v2::LegacyProcessorStage` is `anira::v2::LegacyProcessor`. Migration: `anira::Pipeline p{anira::stage::Inference(model).engine(e), anira::stage::Custom(s)};` becomes `anira::Pipeline p; p.add_engine(e).add_inference(model).add_processor(s);`.
- **Breaking (Web, TypeScript; decision 10.18):** the TS layer follows: the custom stage is a processor, and the pipeline has one member per C entry. `defineStage` → `defineProcessor`, the interfaces `Stage` / `StageFactory` / `StageOptions` → `Processor` / `ProcessorFactory` / `ProcessorOptions` (`phases` and `flags` unchanged), `Registry.defineStage` → `Registry.defineProcessor` and the trampolines' target `Registry.stage` → `Registry.processor` (the C side calls `registry.processor`, `anira_em_js_processor`); `pipeline.addStage(name, options)` → `pipeline.addProcessor(name, options)`, `pipeline.registerEngine(id, options)` → `pipeline.addEngine(id, options)`, and `pipeline.inference(models)` → `pipeline.addInference(models)` (reverting 10.17's `inference`; `anira.hpp`'s `add_inference`); the bits are `ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_*` and `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_*` (values unchanged). The example `web/examples/stage-and-engine` is `web/examples/processor-and-engine` (`gain-stage.ts` → `gain-processor.ts`, `INPUT_GAIN_STAGE` → `INPUT_GAIN_PROCESSOR`), the browser test's `worklet-stage.js` / `load-stage.js` are `worklet-processor.js` / `load-processor.js`, and `build_web`, `run.mjs`, the docs' literalincludes and the web pages follow. Migration: `defineStage(name, f)` becomes `defineProcessor(name, f)`, `pipeline.addInference(model).registerEngine(id).addStage(name, { phases })` becomes `pipeline.addInference(model).addEngine(id).addProcessor(name, { phases })`.
- **Docs:** the guides describe the 3.x runtime only. The usage guide's sections 3 to 5 (the handler, prepare and the plan report, the Hard entries over tensors and their `_wait` twins, Static tensors, one-sided streaming, reset) are written on `anira::InferenceHandler` of `anira.hpp`, beside the C spelling of section 3.2; section 1.3 spells the required `Hard::budget`. The migration page says where `<anira/anira.h>` went, maps every 2.x handler call onto its `anira::InferenceHandler` member and C entry, and replaces the bridge section with "The removed bridge" (what each `anira::v3compat` call became). `benchmarking.rst` describes the fixture of `examples/benchmark/fixture/` and how to copy it. `architecture.rst`, `getting_started.rst`, `examples.rst` and the README follow, and the API pages are regenerated (`anira::Budget`, `anira::scheduler::InferenceThread`; the fixture's page is gone).
- **Breaking:** `<anira/anira.h>` is the C11 umbrella of the C ABI (every promised header of `anira/abi/`, generated from the registry's `umbrellas:` list), no longer the umbrella of the 2.x C++ classes. A 2.x program includes `<anira/compat/v2.hpp>` and spells `anira::v2::` (or `using namespace anira::v2;`); C++ against the 3.x API includes `<anira/anira.hpp>`; the 2.x headers that stay installed until a later pre-release are included one by one.
- **Breaking (C++):** `anira::Hard::budget` is required: it is an `anira::Budget`, which has no default constructor, so a `Hard` aggregate names it (`Hard{.block_max = 512, .rate = 48000, .budget = std::chrono::milliseconds(5)}`), as the web package's `budgetMs`. A `std::chrono` duration converts to an explicit budget; `Budget::measured()` is the measured one (refused at prepare in this pre-release). `Hard::budget_value` is gone (`budget.value()`, `budget.kind()`); `ContractHandle::hard()` reads the budget back as a `Budget`. Migration: replace `.budget = ANIRA_BUDGET_EXPLICIT, .budget_value = d` with `.budget = d`.
- `anira.hpp` refuses `ANIRA_CXX_MANUAL_INIT` and `ANIRA_NO_PROTOTYPES` for good (anira links its entry points; there is no `anira_get_proc_address`); `ANIRA_CXX_NO_EXCEPTIONS` stays unavailable in this pre-release.
- **Examples:** the JUCE plugin (variants 0-7), the CLAP plugin and the three benchmarks run on `anira.hpp`: a `Context`, a `Pipeline` of the model's inference stage (the bypass engine `org.anira.examples.bypass` behind the "BYPASS" setting, the clear engine behind the advanced benchmark's baseline) and, for GuitarLSTM, `HybridNNProcessor`, one `anira::InferenceHandler` prepared with the contract of the host's block; the backend parameter picks a plan of the `PlanReport` with `set_plan`; the audio is one planar tensor over the host's channel pointers. The CLAP plugin's `clap_init` checks `anira_check_abi`.
- **Tests:** the install consumers run on `anira.hpp` (`consumer`: a handler round trip over a custom engine; `consumer_engine`: a model config beside `onnxruntime_cxx_api.h`); `anira_header_isolation` compiles the C umbrella, `anira.hpp`, `compat/v2.hpp` and every 2.x header still installed; the coexistence gates put the 2.x headers (`test/abi/v2_headers.inc`) beside the C headers, `anira.hpp` and the shim in both orders; `anira_header_scheduler_coexist` compiles the library's scheduler classes beside `anira.hpp`; gate 4 compiles `<anira/anira.h>` as C11 and C++17; `test_InferenceHandler` runs `anira::InferenceHandler` with `HybridNNProcessor` and the bypass engine; `test_anira_hpp` covers the handler half (every contract form of `prepare`, the Hard forms and their `_wait` twins over a stereo stream, the Static pair, `set_plan` out of range, `InferenceThread` on a context without pool threads, `log_rt`). The 2.x float face's two helpers (`PlanarFloatAdapter.h`, `NonStreamableRouter.h`) left the library for `test/support/`, the white-box suites' `anira_test::V2Handler` and the copy-oracle rigs; both goldens are unchanged.
- **Examples:** the four minimal-inference examples that link anira (ONNX Runtime, LibTorch, LiteRT, TensorFlow Lite) read the model path and the tensor shapes their engine is fed from the bundled model files with the 3.x read-back (`examples/minimal-inference/minimal_model.h`: `anira::ModelConfig`, `SpecView`, the entry's layout), no longer through `anira/compat/v3_to_v2.h`, and hold their data in `std::vector` instead of anira's 2.x `BufferF` / `MemoryBlock`.
- **Breaking (2.x classes):** the scheduler thread class of the library is `anira::scheduler::InferenceThread` (`anira/scheduler/InferenceThread.h`, the same header; `anira::Core::make_inference_thread` returns it). The name `anira::InferenceThread` belongs to the `anira.hpp` class of the runtime cut-over: no class of namespace `anira` is defined both in libanira and in the header-only `anira.hpp`, on any link (an inline definition beside the library's would interpose on a shared library and resolve strong-over-weak on a static one). A host that drove the 2.x thread directly spells the namespace, or moves to `anira_inference_thread_*` / the `anira.hpp` class; the header becomes private in a later pre-release.
- **Breaking:** **Web (against #213 only; no alpha shipped it):** the TypeScript layer follows `anira.hpp` (decision 10.17): `web/src/` and `anira.hpp` are one layer in two languages, with the same class names, the same member words (camelCase for snake_case), the same return values and defaults; a difference exists only where the platform forces it (`Anira.create`, the worker module, `Context.ready`, the audio node, `measureRenderGeometry`, `fromUrl`, the MANUAL drain and log pump, the per-realm registry, `destroy()` instead of RAII, a thrown `AniraError` instead of a returned status), and each class's doc comment names it. The renames, old → new:

  | 2026-10 web layer | follows `anira.hpp` |
  | --- | --- |
  | `Handler` (`Handler.ts`) | `InferenceHandler` (`InferenceHandler.ts`) |
  | `handler.latency(slot)` | `handler.getLatency(slot)`, and the new `handler.getLatencies()` |
  | `Contract` (`Contract.ts`) | `ContractHandle` (`ContractHandle.ts`) |
  | `contract.setGeometry(min, max, rate)` | `contract.hardGeometry(min, max, rate)` |
  | `model.attachBytes(i, bytes)` | `model.setModelBytes(i, bytes)` (chains) |
  | `pipeline.addInference(model)` | `pipeline.inference(model)` |
  | `x.ptr` (Context, ModelConfig, Contract, Pipeline, Handler) | `x.native()` |
  | `AniraError.code` | `AniraError.status` |
  | `anira.setLogHook(fn)`, `scopeRegistry().logHook` | `anira.setLogSink(fn)`, `scopeRegistry().logSink` |
  | `context.threads` | `context.numInferenceThreads()` |
  | `ContractHandle.hard({ warmup })` | `ContractHandle.hard({ warmupIterations })` |
  | `ModelEntry { engine, engineId }` (`fetchModel`) | `ModelEntry { engine: EngineRef { kind, id } }` (`id` empty beside a built-in engine) |
  | `pipeline.addProcessor(name, { hooks })` | `pipeline.addProcessor(name, { phases })` |

  `InferenceHandler.prepare(contract)` and `audioNode.prepare(contract)` return the prepare's `PlanReport` (new: `numPlans()`, `plans()`, `slots(plan, inputs)`, `extensions(plan)`, `native()`, over `anira_plan_report_*`, as `anira::PlanReport`), so `InferenceHandler.create(...).prepare(contract)` no longer chains to the handler: create, then prepare; `planReport()` reads the last one. The thread-safe members get their twins: `rtError()` (the status returned, not thrown), `setPlan(plan)` / `getPlan()`, `numEntries()`. `setStaticInput` is dtype-generic on the handler and the audio node, in the spec's dtype and extents: a typed array of the spec's element type, any view of exactly the spec's bytes, or plain numbers converted to it (it was float32 and rank 1 on the handler, numbers as float32 on the node). The processor hook bits are one index space with the phases: `ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_*` is `1u << ANIRA_PHASE_*` (`ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_AFTER` 16, `= anira::Processor::k_after_inference`; `ANIRA_EM_PROCESSOR_HOOK_PREPARE` 32, `RELEASE` 64, `RESET` 128, `UNPREPARE` 256, `INIT` 512), checked by a `static_assert` in `em_hooks.cpp`, and `ANIRA_EM_HOOKS_VERSION` is 2; a C caller that spelled the bits by name recompiles, one that spelled numbers changes them. The `threads: 0` sentence ("a host that drives inference itself") is gone from `Context`: the web has no inference thread a host drives. The web tests, the examples (whose model-file check now reads the plan report: one plan, the ONNX Runtime engine, the contract's 5 ms budget), `web/README.md` and the web pages of `docs/sphinx` use the new names; the reference pages are `InferenceHandler`, `ContractHandle` and the new `PlanReport`.
- **Compat (docs):** `anira/compat/v2.hpp`'s `InferenceHandler::set_inference_backend` and `get_inference_backend` are documented as callable from any thread while audio runs, but **not while `prepare()` or the destructor runs** on another thread, the rule `anira_handler_set_plan` already states. `prepare` rebuilds the plan table and may replace the C handler they read, so a concurrent call races it (#202). 2.x promised the switch while audio runs, never alongside `prepare`; a host that switches from a UI thread and re-prepares from another serialises the two. The class comment, both members and the compat page of `migration.rst` say so; no code changes.
- **Web (docs):** `audio_nodes.rst` notes resampled contexts: a context of 44.1 kHz on a 48 kHz device renders 2048 × 44100 / 48000 = 1881.6 context frames per callback (runs of 14 and 15 quanta of 128, a burst of 1920), which the probe measures (it times the callbacks and counts at the context's rate) and Chromium's `baseLatency × sampleRate` (2048) overstates.
- **Web:** the module's memory grows. AniraWeb links `-sALLOW_MEMORY_GROWTH=1 -sINITIAL_MEMORY=268435456 -sMAXIMUM_MEMORY=2147483648` (it was a fixed 512 MiB), and `Anira.create` makes a shared memory of 256 MiB growing to 2 GiB; `Anira.create({ memory: { initial, maximum } })` sizes it in bytes, `initial === maximum` fixes it (the opt-out). **Breaking** (against the earlier pre-release): the `memory` option was a pre-allocated `WebAssembly.Memory`; pass its sizes instead. Sizes the module cannot run on (below 256 MiB, a maximum below the initial size or above 2 GiB) are refused with an `AniraError` (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`), and a memory the browser cannot reserve with one (`ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY`) naming the sizes and the way to a smaller maximum. A shared memory's maximum is reserved as address space at creation and committed as it is touched: free on 64-bit browsers (V8 reserves 8 GiB per wasm32 memory whatever the maximum, SpiderMonkey 4 GiB plus a guard), and capped at 2 GiB for 32-bit and constrained devices; Chromium 156 and Firefox 157 accept up to 65536 pages (4 GiB). The memory never shrinks. A growth gives the memory a new buffer, so every heap access of the package goes through one helper, `heap(module)` of the generated bindings (exported from the scope entry for processors and engines), which rebuilds the realm's views once after a growth; the processor and engine trampolines call it before every call, and anira's boot gives each inference Worker's and the worklet's `Module` its `wasmMemory`, which AniraWeb lists in `EXPORTED_RUNTIME_METHODS`: unexported, it is a getter-only property of `Module` under `ASSERTIONS`, and in a Debug build (`wasm-debug`) the boot's assignment threw in strict mode (`TypeError: setting getter-only property "wasmMemory"`), so no inference Worker entered its loop and the worklet's registry never booted (the ABI gates' glue is a sloppy-mode script, where the assignment fails silently). The C audio path still allocates nothing, so the audio thread never grows the memory; a JavaScript processor's realm rebuilds its views at its first call after a growth (a small JavaScript allocation). The cost of the glue's own checks with `-pthread` (Emscripten's `pthreads-mem-growth`), headless, medians of 5, growing against fixed: the worklet's work per quantum (60 s offline, not waiting) 59.5 against 56.3 ms in Chromium 156 (+6 %) and 48.9 against 46.7 ms in Firefox 157 (+5 %); a render waiting for a JavaScript engine on two Workers 468 against 469 ms; a binding call 127-132 ns in both. `growth.html` grows the memory from 256 to 531 MiB through 56 handlers and renders exact through the Workers and the worklet with a node from before the growth and a handler from after it, grows and re-prepares a node mid-stream (offline: suspended at the half in Chromium, from a timer in Firefox; live in Chromium) with no wrong sample, and checks the refused sizes; `heap.test.ts` checks the rebuild; `oom.html` creates its module with a fixed 512 MiB, which fills in moments, and `anira_em_oom` keeps its own fixed memory. The defaults stay AUTO: an AUTO pool of 16 Workers with two RAVE models (about 530 MiB of instances beside the core's 169 MiB) did not fit the fixed 512 MiB (#218) and fits now.
- **Web:** the render geometry follows the Web Audio API and reads no `baseLatency` (`web/src/renderGeometry.ts`). `measureRenderGeometry` always measures the burst (it returned Chromium's `baseLatency` at once, a value no other browser gives and Chromium keeps from the first device after `setSinkId`); its `probe` and `probeMs` options are gone, and `hold` holds the context's running nodes while it measures (`AniraAudioNode.hold`, `anira_em_audio_node_hold`), which fixed a probe that measured 512 of 2048 frames beside a node with a heavy JavaScript processor. `Handler.createAudioNode` no longer warns from `baseLatency`, and `Handler.blockMax`, which only fed that warning, is removed. The browser test runs on Playwright 1.64.1 (Chromium 156, Firefox 157; it was 1.49.1), whose Chromium has `renderSizeHint`.
- **Web:** `Context.create` without `threads` keeps the context config's default, `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO` (half the cores, at least one), the native default, instead of overriding it with 1: one config for native and web. Pass `threads` for an exact count; the browser tests that need one do. The examples, `basic_usage` and the web README use the default.
- **Web:** a JavaScript engine gets the model of its entry, and the model is fetched once. A JavaScript engine's descriptor now has C `load` / `prepare` trampolines that do not cross into JavaScript (the engine runs in the Workers' scopes, not the main thread's, where the C load runs): the load reads the row of `anira_engine_load_info` as a C engine's would (`anira_model_config_model_path`, `_model_bytes`) into a record the loaded model owns (the bytes borrowed for its lifetime, the path copied), and the prepare hands that record to every `process` and `reset` call as `prepared`. `engineModel(env, prepared)` reads it (`row`, `bytes` as a `Uint8Array` view of the shared memory, `path`), and an engine's new optional `load(model)` hook runs on each Worker before the first call on a loaded model. No ABI surface is added (the record is internal to `em_hooks.cpp` and `registry.ts`). `onnxRuntimeWeb()` takes `model` optionally: without it, the engine creates its session in `load` from the entry's bytes, which `ModelConfig.fromUrl` attached on the main thread (copied into onnxruntime-web's own heap), or from a synchronous fetch of the entry's path when it carries no bytes; with it, as before. So a config naming `org.anira.onnxruntime-web` states the model once and it is requested once, where before every Worker fetched its own copy besides the main thread's unused one. `installation_building` adds onnxruntime-web to the Vite `optimizeDeps.exclude` advice (a dev-server dependency re-optimisation reloads the page mid-fetch). The browser test's `ort.html` runs the config `ort-gain.model.json` on a pool of two Workers and counts the requests (`/__requests` of `run.mjs`): the `.onnx` once, the output exact; `npm test` checks that `load` sees the entry's bytes once per loaded model.
- **ABI (unstable):** `ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_NEEDS_NO_MODEL` is consumed: a model entry of a custom engine that declares it needs no source. A JSON entry may leave out `"path"`, `anira_model_config_add_model_path` takes a NULL path for such an entry (an empty one is still refused), and C++ gains `ModelConfig::add_model(engine_id)`; `anira_handler_create` refuses a source-less entry of any other engine with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, naming the flag. The engine's load reads no path and no bytes (`anira_model_config_model_path` is NULL). The 2.x bridge hands the CUSTOM backend a stand-in source it never opens. Before, every entry needed a path, so a computing engine (a JavaScript passthrough, a test double) carried a placeholder such as `"never-opened.bin"` and, on the web, `fetchModel: () => false` to keep `ModelConfig.fromUrl` from fetching it; the examples, tests and guides drop both. The 2.x JSON loader's `model_path` stays required.
- **Web:** `ONNXRUNTIME_WEB_ENGINE_ID` lives in a module of its own (`web/src/engines/ids.ts`), re-exported by the root entry, the scope entry and the engine entry: a page that only names the ONNX Runtime Web engine (registers it, names it in a model entry) no longer bundles onnxruntime-web's glue (~25 KB) to get the id, since only the engine's own entry imports onnxruntime-web. `npm run check:bundle` (`web/test/bundle-check.mjs`, run by `build_web` after the examples) follows every static import of `dist/index.js` and `dist/scope.js` and fails on a module carrying onnxruntime-web's code, on a root entry without the id, and on an example chunk carrying it.
- **Docs (Web):** what an inference Worker and the worklet can see (`web-api/architecture.rst`, linked from the JavaScript engines page): every Worker and the AudioWorkletGlobalScope is its own realm, only the WebAssembly memory is shared, so processors and engines are defined in the realm that runs them and a pool of N threads builds N engine instances; once the engines' `init` resolved, a Worker's C loop blocks (`memory.atomic.wait32`) and its event loop never runs again until the stop, so `process` is synchronous and `init` may be async; the channels after init are the Static inputs, a processor's `prepared` pointer and an app block in the heap with `Atomics`, never a `postMessage`; the worklet realm has no `fetch` and no `performance`. All of it unchanged since 2.x. Asynchronous engines (onnxruntime-web `session.run()`, a WebGPU `mapAsync` readback, tfjs `data()`) are not supported; they are recorded for M3 in `docs/anira-v3-architecture.md` (a Worker that leaves its blocking loop with `Atomics.waitAsync`, completing the job's ticket later). The `anira/anira_em.h` header doc and the guide say a js_key is the 32-bit FNV-1a hash of the name and a registry refuses a second name with a taken key (`npm test` forces a collision pair). The Doxygen of `CoreConfig` (the thread default is half the cores on WebAssembly too, the true reasons `Blocking` and the drain thread stay coerced there: the queue has no semaphore and an idle Wasm Worker parks on the wake word, the JavaScript log sink runs on the main thread), of `InferenceThread`, `InferenceQueue`, `set_non_realtime` and `v2::ContextConfig`, the usage guide and the coercion warnings no longer describe the pre-Worker runtime.
- **Docs (Web):** the Anira Web guides describe the typed layer: `index` (the threads anira starts, configs once, the worker module as the one web-only context field), `installation_building` (the three entry points, what a page needs: cross-origin isolation, self-contained worker and worklet modules, `optimizeDeps.exclude`, the dev server; the example's `vite.config.ts` included), `basic_usage` (a bundled config on the built-in ONNX Runtime engine through the audio node, step by step from `web/examples/model-file/main.ts`, raw calls through `anira.abi`; the processor-and-engine example's `main.ts` included), `architecture` (the main thread, the pool of Wasm Workers, the audio thread, the scope registry, where each phase runs, the bindings), `audio_nodes` (in place of `custom_audio_worklets`: the C render callback, its options, the worklet scope), `custom_pre_post_processing` (`defineProcessor`, `addProcessor`; the example's `gain-processor.ts`), `custom_inference_backends` (the worker module, `defineEngine`, `addEngine`, ONNX Runtime Web; the example's `double-engine.ts`); every included file is type-checked by `npm run examples` and run by the browser test. The API reference lists the new classes and functions. The migration page maps the 2.x classes and the 3.0.0-alpha.2 runtime to the typed layer; `web/README.md` and the README follow. `Fetcher` names the fetch the loaders take (sphinx-js cannot cross-reference `typeof fetch`).
- **WebAssembly:** `num_threads` and `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO` are no longer coerced to 0, by the core nor by the 2.x `JsonConfigLoader` (the pool's threads are Wasm Workers); `ANIRA_WAIT_BLOCKING` still becomes `ANIRA_WAIT_SPIN_BACKOFF` (an idle Worker parks on the wake word) and `ANIRA_LOG_DRAIN_THREAD` `ANIRA_LOG_DRAIN_MANUAL`. A context config that leaves the threads at AUTO now gets a pool of Workers on the web: a host that drives inference itself (the 3.0.0-alpha.2 `spinUpInferenceWorker`) sets `num_threads` 0.
- **Build/CI (WebAssembly):** the module links `-pthread -sWASM_WORKERS -sAUDIO_WORKLET` (Emscripten's hybrid mode; `cmake/build-wasm.cmake`), names `-sINCOMING_MODULE_JS_API=wasmMemory,wasmBinary,locateFile,instantiateWasm,print,printErr`, builds the shipped Release module with `-sASSERTIONS=0` (`wasm-debug` keeps 1: emsdk's Web Audio library asserts `instanceof window.AudioContext`, which an `OfflineAudioContext` fails) and exports `emscriptenRegisterAudioObject`; the environment is `web,worker,worklet`. Every C and C++ object of the Wasm build, the fetched dependencies' included, compiles with `-pthread` (`CMakeLists.txt`, in place of `-matomics -mbulk-memory`), as do the node gates of `test/abi`. The emsdk stays at 4.0.23, the shared tanh-lab pin: the prebuilt ONNX Runtime WASM archive is LTO bitcode of that toolchain, and on emsdk 6.0.13 (libc++ 22) every model load of the built-in engine fails (`ONNX Schema NhwcMaxPool: failed validating the check`); the move waits for a matching archive (anira-project/backends#24). The built-in ONNX Runtime engine creates no thread on WebAssembly: its global thread pools are one thread each, the calling one, inter-op included (a pthread started from the browser's main thread runs only once that thread yields, so the join at the environment's release never returned). The 3.0.0-alpha.2 package keeps working on the new module: its worklet instantiates the glue as a plain instance (the glue is shown a window, not the worklet scope, while it starts), gets a one-core `navigator`, and `createAniraWasm`'s `locateFile` answers in a scope without `URL`.
- A stateless model that sets no `max_instances` gets as many shared instances as the context's inference pool has threads (at least 1; 1 when the host brings its own threads), 2.x's default (`num_parallel_processors` defaulted to half the hardware threads, the default pool's size) instead of 3.x's 1, so a pool's threads can run one model's inferences at once. The default is the new registry constant `ANIRA_INSTANCES_AUTO` (`0xffffffffu`, beside `ANIRA_THREADS_AUTO`): what `anira_model_config_create` stores and `anira_model_config_max_instances` / `ModelConfig::max_instances()` read back unresolved, what `anira_model_config_set_max_instances` accepts besides a count of at least 1 (0 is still refused), and the `max_instances` key left out of a model document (`to_json` leaves it out too). `Core::create_session` resolves it, silently, when the handler is prepared, before the session is built and before the plans meet the pool's keys, so two handlers that leave the count to the pool share one loaded model; an explicit count wins, clamped to the pool as before; a stateful model or one with a declared State pair is unchanged (no shared instance, one executor per handler). The upgrade of a 2.x model document without `num_parallel_processors` keeps `ANIRA_INSTANCES_AUTO` instead of writing half the hardware threads, and the shim's (`anira/compat/v2.hpp`) `InferenceConfig` constructors default their count to it (a document without the key; the shim reads it back as `Defaults::num_parallel_processors()`); `anira::v3compat::to_inference_config` carries the sentinel into `num_parallel_processors`, which the core resolves. Memory: every instance is a full copy of the model (one engine executor over the loaded model), so a model with no `max_instances` now takes up to the pool's thread count of copies; set `max_instances(1)` to keep one. Latency: the latency calculation counts on the instances (`n`), so a model whose inference takes longer than a hop gets a lower computed latency with the default than with 1. The documented default changes from 1 (`anira_model_config_create`, `anira_model_config_set_max_instances`, `usage.rst`); no signature changes. Tests: `AbiInstanceDispatch.MaxInstancesLeftToThePoolIsThePoolsThreadCount`, `.TwoHandlersLeftToThePoolShareOneLoadedModel`, `.AnExplicitCountWinsAndAStatefulModelHasNoSharedInstance`, `.MaxInstancesLeftToThePoolIsOneWithoutAPool`; the read-back, JSON upgrade, fixture, translate and engine-record tests expect the sentinel or the pool's count.
- **Build/CI:** test parallelism is no longer pinned in `CMakePresets.json`. Every test preset lost its `execution.jobs: 4`, and the shared `cmake-test` action resolves the job count from the runner instead (its new `CTEST_JOBS` input, default `auto`); the ci-actions pin moves to `v0.3.15`. A job count in a preset is a property of whichever machine happens to run it: it capped a 32-core workstation running `desktop-tests-rtsan` / `-asan` / `-tsan` by hand, and oversubscribed the 3-vCPU `macos-latest` runners by a third. CI parallelism is unchanged on the 4-vCPU Linux and Windows runners and now matches the hardware on macOS. Locally, `ctest --preset ...` follows `CTEST_PARALLEL_LEVEL` or an explicit `-j`; note that ctest ignores that variable whenever any job count reaches the command line, which is why the presets had to give the number up for it to work. The `timeout` values are unchanged.
- **Build/CI:** build parallelism is no longer pinned either. The shared `cmake-build` action defaults `CMAKE_BUILD_PARALLEL_LEVEL` to `auto` (ci-actions `v0.3.15`), and the six remaining `--parallel 4` call sites in the workflows and in `.github/scripts/build_ios_xcframework.sh` detect the core count instead. Those were the Android install legs, the four iOS builds and the install-consumer build; on the 3-vCPU `macos-latest` runners the fixed 4 was oversubscribing. Nothing pins a job count anywhere now, with one deliberate exception: the benchmark leg keeps `-j 1` in its `CTEST_ARGS`, since running benchmarks concurrently would corrupt their timings, and the action appends `CTEST_ARGS` last precisely so an explicit override still wins.
- **Build/CI:** `.vscode/settings.json` is now tracked (the folder stays ignored otherwise). It enables `cmake.ctest.allowParallelJobs`, without which the CMake Tools Test Explorer runs one `ctest` process per test, sequentially, ignoring any job count. Since anira registers one CTest entry per gtest case, a full local run took 126 s that way against 16 s for a single parallel `ctest` on a 32-core machine.
- **Build/CI:** every configure preset exports the compile database. `CMAKE_EXPORT_COMPILE_COMMANDS` is `ON` on the preset roots (`desktop-base`, `ci-base`, `wasm-debug`, `wasm-release`, `docs`), so all 34 visible configure presets inherit it and the `clang-tidy` preset drops its own copy. Only `clang-tidy` set it before; a desktop or ci tree's `compile_commands.json` held nothing but the fetched tanh-lib's five entries (tanh-lib sets the variable in its own directory scope), so clangd and a local clang-tidy saw none of anira's sources. A fresh `desktop-tests-debug` or `ci-tests-static` tree now lists 159 entries (37 under `src/`, 83 under `test/`). The Visual Studio generator ignores the variable.
- **Build/CI:** clangd follows the tree configured last. tanh-tooling v0.3.0 on both sides: anira's `.clangd`, `cmake/tanh/` and `lint.yml`, and tanh-lib pinned at `7ae7c69` (tanh-lab/tanh-lib#73: v0.4.0's core plus the same tooling tag). `.clangd` reads `build/compile_commands.json` instead of `build/desktop/Debug/`, and the new option `ANIRA_LINK_COMPILE_COMMANDS` (default OFF, top-level builds only) links it to the configuring tree's database (`tanh_link_compile_commands`, `cmake/tanh/compile-commands.cmake`). Every `desktop-*` preset and `wasm-debug` / `wasm-release` turn it on; `clang-tidy` turns it off, and the `docs`, `ci-*`, sanitizer and mobile presets never set it, so a tidy sweep or a sanitizer run leaves the link where it was. Configuring `desktop-tests-debug` shows clangd the tests, `wasm-debug` the Emscripten branches. Reconfigure a development preset once to create the link; for the wasm trees, start clangd with `--query-driver=**/em++` (`docs/sphinx/contributing.rst`).
- **Build/CI:** every Linux job runs on `ubuntu-24.04` instead of `ubuntu-latest` (the six Linux legs of `.github/build_test_matrix.json`, the benchmark, examples and install matrices, and the workflows' own jobs). GitHub moved `ubuntu-latest` to Ubuntu 26.04 (resolute), for which apt.llvm.org builds LLVM 21 and later but no 20, so the LLVM 20 that the tooling is pinned to could not be installed and every Linux leg failed in `setup-cpp-build-tools` (`apt-get update`: `llvm-toolchain-resolute-20` has no Release file, exit 100). The tidy job was already on `ubuntu-24.04`, and so are ci-actions' sanitizer legs; the coverage legs run inside ci-actions' reusable workflow, whose runner was hard-coded to `ubuntu-latest`, so the ci-actions pin moves to `v0.3.16` (tanh-lab/ci-actions#15: a `runs_on` input defaulting to `ubuntu-24.04`, and `setup-cpp-build-tools` failing with a message that names the missing LLVM release). Moving to LLVM 21 instead would change the clang-format and clang-tidy output against the tanh-tooling pin.
- Docs: `docs/sphinx/contributing.rst` gains "Running the format and tidy checks locally": LLVM 20 from the PyPI wheels, clang-format's check mode over the tracked sources and a fix over the branch's changed files only (never `-i` over the tree), the `clang-tidy` preset's tree, a pull-request run over the changed `.cpp` files and the merge queue's sweep over the workflow's `SOURCES`, what the sweep costs and why (the test files, 80 to 125 s each), and clangd over any tree.
- **Unload (ELF and Mach-O):** a library unloaded while an inference is still running aborts with a message instead of hanging. The library-unload hook ran `Core::shutdown()`, which joins the pool, from inside `dlclose`, where the unloading thread holds the loader lock (glibc's `dl_load_lock`) for the whole close.
