A pool thread that needed that lock mid-inference could never finish, so the join hung the host for good: LibTorch's first inference on a thread registers its `thread_local` destructors through `__cxa_thread_atexit`, which takes the lock, and a runtime's lazy `dlopen` or a custom engine can do the same. Returning without the thread would unmap the code under it. The hook now tells every pool thread to stop (`InferenceThread::request_stop`), waits up to two seconds for every pool thread to leave its loop and for the inferences in flight (`InferenceThread::get_num_in_flight()`, a process-wide atomic every thread that runs jobs keeps from the dequeue of a job to its end; idle threads count nothing), and joins as before once both hold. Waiting for the loops matters because a thread checks its stop flag and then dequeues: one that passed the check just before the stop takes one more job, counted only from its dequeue, possibly after the hook's first look (a queue run on a 4-vCPU runner hung that way in `ABuiltInInferenceInFlightAtUnloadNeverHangs`). When a thread or an inference is still running at the bound it writes `anira: the library is being unloaded while an inference is still running (N in flight, M inference threads not stopped); destroy every anira handler, or call anira_shutdown(), before unloading. Aborting instead of hanging.` to stderr with `write(2)` (not through anira's log queue or sinks, which may be gone or be what is stuck) and calls `std::abort()`. A correct host (every handler destroyed before the unload) sees no change: the pool is gone before the hook runs. At process exit the hook runs as well (`_dl_fini`), but no loader lock is held there, so it keeps waiting without a bound: an exit handler registered when the core is first put to work tells the hook that the process is exiting (a core first put to work before `main` is not told). Exit behaviour, established with the tests below against the old hook: a program that exits with an inference in flight exited normally before (the join waits, a 3 s inference included; LibTorch mid-first-inference exited 0 in 20 of 20 runs) and still does; with the bounded wait applied at exit too the 3 s case would abort, which the exit handler prevents. Windows is unchanged: the CRT runs the static destructor from `DllMain` under the loader lock, where nothing may be waited for, and it also runs at exit after `ExitProcess` has ended every other thread, so an in-flight count would be stale there. macOS: the order of an image's exit handlers and its terminators at `dlclose` under dyld is not verified; if dyld runs the handlers first, the hook takes the unload for an exit and joins without a bound, today's behaviour. Also fixed: the hook's comment claimed that pool threads never take the loader lock. Tests (`test/contracts/unload`): `LibraryUnloadDeathTest.AnInferenceStillRunningAtUnloadAborts` (a module engine that never returns), `AnInferenceWaitingForTheLoaderLockAtUnloadAborts` (glibc: a module engine that takes `dl_load_lock` through `dlopen`, the investigation's hang made deterministic), `ABuiltInInferenceInFlightAtUnloadNeverHangs` (the investigation's case: the built-in engine with blocks in flight either unloads cleanly or aborts with the message), `ExitWithASlowInferenceInFlightExitsNormally` and `ExitWithABuiltInInferenceInFlightExitsNormally`, and `LibraryUnload.AnInferenceThatFinishesWithinTheWaitUnloadsCleanly` (a 300 ms inference in flight: the hook waits, joins and the library unmaps). Against the old hook the three unload death tests hang until their watchdog; skipped on Windows and, for the unload cases, on Apple. The module gains `unloadtest_create_engine` and `unloadtest_engine_entered`.
- Docs: the teardown rule is stated where hosts read it: a plugin destroys its handlers, then its contexts, before its host unloads it, from its instance teardown or module-exit entry point, and never from its own static destructors or `DllMain`. `docs/sphinx/usage.rst` gains section 6 "Teardown and unloading"; `troubleshooting.rst` ("Host crashes when unloading the plugin"), `migration.rst`, the docs of `anira_shutdown`, `anira_release_core_if_idle`, `anira_context_destroy` and `anira_handler_destroy` (`abi/anira.yml`, regenerated; doc-only, no symbol changes), `anira::Context` and `anira::shutdown()` in `anira.hpp`, `anira::v2::InferenceHandler`'s destructor, and `anira::Core` (the pool-lifetime section, `shutdown()`, `make_inference_thread()`) say so.

- The built-in engines have the init level a custom engine has. The core hands out one engine object per compiled-in engine while a loaded model of the engine holds it (`Core::builtin_engine`, `engine::BuiltinEngine`, the twin of a custom engine's carrier; the last holder frees it on its own thread, and the next load makes a new object) and runs its init once per object, with the same `anira_init_info`, under the lifecycle lock, right before the engine's first load; what an engine keeps per process moved there from every load: ONNX Runtime's environment (one per process, its logger at the level in effect at init; every session of every loaded model is created over it), LiteRT's environment (likewise, with the accelerators its registration finds, which a load's record is checked against), ExecuTorch's `runtime_init()`, LibTorch's intra-op thread count and c10 log level; TensorFlow Lite keeps nothing per process. Every loaded model of the engine holds the object and the last to let go frees it (a failed load leaves nothing behind); the context's probe asks it for the engine's providers (`BuiltinEngine::providers`, the runtime asked at every probe, without the lifecycle lock). A load before the engine's init is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`, for a built-in and a custom engine alike. Internal: `make_builtin_loaded` takes the engine object (`make_builtin_engine` makes one), `builtin_providers(engine)` and `litert_providers()` take no level, and `engine::Model` carries no log level.
- **Breaking (ABI, unstable):** `anira_processor_phase`, now `anira_phase`, is renumbered so the three inference-thread phases count in the order they run: `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE` takes 3, `ANIRA_PHASE_AFTER_INFERENCE` moves 3 -> 4, `ANIRA_PHASE_PREPARE` 4 -> 5, `ANIRA_PHASE_RELEASE` 5 -> 6 (`PRE_PROCESS` 0, `POST_PROCESS` 1 and `BEFORE_INFERENCE` 2 stay). Migration: recompile against the new `anira/abi/enums.h` (and take `web/src/abi/enums.ts` with it); use the names, never the numbers. No entry point consumes the enum yet, so no stored value exists.
- **Design (docs/anira-v3-architecture.md, `anira/anira.hpp`):** a custom engine belongs to the pipeline, never a stage of its own. It is one more implementation the inference stage's candidates resolve to (`anira_pipeline_add_engine`, legal before or after `anira_pipeline_add_inference`), it takes no place beside the pipeline's processor, and its `process` runs in `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE`. The planned C++ `stage::CustomBackend` is dropped; the spelling is `Pipeline::add_engine(impl)`, the id being the engine object's own. The JS hook object's phase table is stated by phase name. The doc of `anira_pipeline_add_inference` says the same.
- **Breaking (rename):** the 3.x handle over the core is the *context*, the C convention for the object created first, passed to everything else and possibly instantiated more than once (`libusb_context`, `zmq_ctx`, `SSL_CTX`, the GL and CUDA contexts). `anira/abi/machine.h` is `anira/abi/context.h`; `anira_machine` / `anira_machine_config` are `anira_context` / `anira_context_config`, and every entry moves with them (`anira_context_create` / `destroy` / `probe` / `capabilities` / `byte_image_bytes`, the 17 `anira_context_config_*` entries of `anira/abi/config.h` that v3.0.0-alpha.1 shipped as `anira_machine_config_*`); `anira_inference_thread_create` takes an `anira_context*`. In `anira/anira.hpp`, `MachineConfig` is `ContextConfig` and `Machine` is `Context`; the JSON *machine file* is the *context file* (its keys are unchanged, and a 2.x document's `context_config` root still upgrades into it). Migration: replace `machine` with `context` in every 3.x identifier; the registry diff against alpha.1 lists the removed names one by one.
- **Breaking (overlap):** the elements a streamed tensor keeps from the previous window are its *overlap*, the DSP word beside *hop* (window minus overlap), where alpha.1 said *context*: `anira_tensor_spec_set_window(spec, window_min, window_max, overlap)`, `anira::TensorSpec::window(min, max, overlap)`, and the model file key `"overlap"` (the bundled model files are rewritten). Migration: rename the key and the argument; nothing else moves.
- **Breaking (the core's C++ face):** `anira::Core` is all static: both `get_instance` overloads, the staged configuration behind the deprecated one and the three-argument `create_session` are gone, with `InferenceManager::get_core()`. Migration: `Core::create_session(pp, config, custom, core_config)` and the other statics; a handler needs no reference to the core.
- `anira/abi/core.h` holds the steady clock (`anira_now_ms`, `anira_now_ns`) and the shutdown family (`anira_shutdown`, `anira_release_core_if_idle`, `anira_has_core`), moved out of `anira/abi/context.h` while ABI 0.2 is untagged. The per-context twins `anira_context_drain_log` and `anira_context_num_inference_threads` of the first draft are gone, since the queue and the pool are the core's: `anira_drain_log` (`anira/abi/log.h`) and `anira_num_inference_threads` (`anira/abi/thread.h`) are the entries, with `anira::drain_log()` and `anira::num_inference_threads()` in `anira/anira.hpp`.
- A version 2 document whose root also carries a 3.x key (`models` beside `inference_config`, `num_threads` beside `context_config`, ...) is refused as `ANIRA_ERROR_JSON` naming the key, instead of being read as 2.x with the 3.x key ignored.
- **Breaking (2.x classes):** the process-wide singleton the 2.x handler registers with is the *core*: `anira::Context` is `anira::Core` (`anira/scheduler/Core.h`; its private state struct is `Core::State`), `anira::ContextConfig` is `anira::CoreConfig` (`anira/CoreConfig.h`, with `LogConfig` / `LogDrain` / `WaitStrategy` / `LogLevel` unchanged), `Context::register_machine` is `Core::register_context`, `InferenceManager::get_context()` is `get_core()`, `JsonConfigLoader::get_context_config()` is `get_core_config()`, the bridge's `to_context_config` is `to_core_config`, and the log group `anira.context` is `anira.core`. The 2.x JSON key `context_config` is unchanged (it is the document format). The compat namespace of the 3.x handler will spell the 2.x struct `anira::v2::ContextConfig`; `CoreConfig` is this pre-release's spelling. Migration: the 2.x handler used to take a `ContextConfig` and takes a `CoreConfig`, the same struct under its truthful name; the C handler of `anira/abi/handler.h` takes an `anira_context*` built from an `anira_context_config`. `Core::shutdown()` and `Core::make_inference_thread()` are the two members the guides showed; `anira_shutdown` and `anira_inference_thread_create` are their 3.x forms.
- **Breaking:** `anira_shutdown` (and the CLAP example's `clap_deinit`, which now calls it) is effective only when no context and no handler exist in this copy of anira; otherwise nothing happens and it returns `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`, so one client of a shared `libanira` cannot silence another's sessions. `anira::Core::shutdown()`'s unconditional form stays internal for the library-unload hook. Migration: destroy every context and handler before the module-exit entry point runs, which a plugin does anyway.
- **Breaking:** `anira::CoreConfig::m_anira_version` and `m_enabled_backends` are gone, with the cross-session version and enabled-backends compares of the context (inside one copy every session shares one header; across two copies nothing can see the other). What the build compiled in is `anira_enabled_engines`; what is usable here is `anira_capabilities_backends` on a probed context. Migration: nothing to write; code that read the two fields uses the two entries.
- The core owns the real-time log drain thread: a low-priority `thl::core::Thread` named `anira-log` of anira's own (`LogDrainLoop`, `src/scheduler/LogDrainLoop.h`) runs `Queue::drain()` every drain interval while a session or a context exists (tanh-lib's `DrainThread` is no longer constructed), and the last user's release, `anira_context_destroy` or `anira_shutdown` stops it and flushes the queue through the sinks on the calling thread. `ANIRA_LOG_FLAG_TRACE_FAILURES` is admitted by `anira_context_config_set_log_flags` and the `flags` of `anira_log_desc`.
- Every input and output ring of the pipeline is an instantiation of `RingBufferT<T>` with `T` the element type of the slot's ring dtype: `anira_ring` (`anira/utils/RingBuffer.h`, the type `anira::RingBuffer` now names) holds one instantiation per scalar dtype the ABI pins (float32, float64, float16, bfloat16, int8, uint8, bool8, int16, int32, int64, with `T` the dtype's C type and the three that have none stored as their bits; no two dtypes share a ring; float32 unless a slot says otherwise), chosen at prepare: `SessionElement::prepare` and `Core::prepare_session` take the per-slot `RingDtypes` and the caller's `CustomLatencies` (the former `std::vector<long>`, named), which the 2.x `InferenceManager::prepare` fills from its arguments (a new overload carries both for the 3.x prepare). The ring's block API (`push_block`, `pop_block`, `peek_past_block`, `push_fill`, `push_zeros`, `discard`, `pop_windows`, which now holds the batched sliding-window pop of `PrePostProcessor::pop_samples_from_buffer`) takes the caller's dtype, returns 0 and writes nothing on a disagreement, and never converts; the 2.x float API stays as the float32 face, so every 2.x pre/post processor compiles unchanged. The typed Hard entries of `anira/abi/handler.h` are what reaches these rings across the ABI; nothing converts, and a ring dtype that differs from its spec's dtype is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at `anira_handler_prepare`.
- **Breaking (the core's C++ face):** the core reads the context config itself: `anira::Core::register_context` and `Core::create_session` take a `const anira_context_config&` (the struct behind `anira_context_config_create`), so the C enums `anira_wait_strategy`, `anira_log_level` and `anira_log_drain` reach `InferenceThread`, the log queue and the drain unconverted; `InferenceThread` takes an `anira_wait_strategy`; `create_session` and the `SessionElement` constructor take an optional `anira::RtLatch*` (the handler's real-time latch, the session's own when absent); the consumed-or-fail walk over a context's extensions runs once, at `anira_context_create`. A 3.x handler is a user of the core (`Core::register_handler` / `unregister_handler` / `get_num_handlers`), so `anira_shutdown` and `release_core_if_idle` refuse while one lives; `Core::get_wait_strategy` reports the strategy the pool runs. The 2.x `InferenceManager` constructor keeps its `CoreConfig` and maps it onto the C struct (`anira::capi::make_context_config`, gone with the 2.x classes at the cut-over); the bridge's `to_core_config` is unchanged. The three reconciliation warnings say "log level mismatch", "log drain mismatch", "wait strategy mismatch" ("a new context or session requested ...") instead of naming `CoreConfig`. Migration: a direct caller of `Core::create_session` builds an `anira_context_config` (in-tree white-box tests include `src/capi/handles.h`); everything through `anira::InferenceHandler` is unchanged.
- **Breaking (2.x classes):** `ANIRA_REALTIME` is gone. The eight real-time declarations of `anira::InferenceHandler` (`process`, `push_data`, `pop_data`, `reset`) carry `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` of `anira/abi/export.h` — clang's `nonblocking` attribute wherever clang has it, no longer gated on `ANIRA_WITH_RTSAN` — and `anira/utils/RealtimeSanitizer.h` keeps `ANIRA_BLOCKING` alone. Target `anira` compiles with `-Wno-function-effects` under clang until the internal chain is annotated; consumer-shaped targets take `-Werror=function-effects` from the tensor PR on. Migration: spell `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` and include `anira/abi/export.h`.
- The three miss policies are implemented in the scheduler's starvation path, so the `ANIRA_MISS_BYPASS` default of `anira_contract_create_hard`, of `anira::Hard{}` and of the bundled contract files is truthful from here: `ANIRA_MISS_ZEROS` delivers zeros (the 2.x behaviour), `ANIRA_MISS_HOLD_LAST` repeats the last delivered block of every streamed output (one block-sized buffer per output channel, allocated at prepare) and the latest value of a Static output, `ANIRA_MISS_BYPASS` copies the block the call pushed into the anchored input into the output on the process forms (the smaller of the input's and the output's sample count, zeros past the input; a call that pushed another slot, and a pop, deliver zeros). A missed block returns `ANIRA_MISSED` under every policy, with every delivered count 0 (the policy governs the buffer's content, the status says the block was missed), the stream stays time-aligned, and an output whose request is 0 is left untouched. BYPASS is refused at prepare with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming `on_miss` when the anchor is an output (no anchored input to pass through: a generator, or a named output anchor) and when a streamed output's channel count differs from the anchored input's; hence `rave_funk_drum_encoder.contract.json` and `rave_funk_drum_decoder.contract.json` spell `"on_miss": "zeros"` (1 audio channel against 4 latents, and the decoder's output anchor), every other bundled file keeps the default, and the Hard contract a version 2 document is upgraded to carries `ANIRA_MISS_ZEROS` (what the 2.x runtime delivered on a miss).
- The bridge to the 2.x runtime (`<anira/compat/v3_to_v2.h>`) no longer refuses a miss policy other than BYPASS; a ring dtype that differs from its spec's dtype is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` there, and at `anira_handler_prepare` unless the pipeline's processor fills the phase that moves that ring (a name that is not a Streamed tensor likewise, everywhere). The registry doc of `anira_contract_hard_set_ring_dtype` (`anira/abi/config.h`) and `anira::ContractHandle::hard_ring_dtype` state the no-conversion rule: the ring dtype is the element type the typed Hard entries carry and the ring holds as is; nothing in anira converts; the "data only in this pre-release" sentence is gone. The empty candidate set of a pipeline (`none of the N model entries names a candidate engine`) is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, not `NOT_SUPPORTED`. The candidate filter of the bridge and of the extension walk takes `anira_backend_id` rows; the `std::span<const anira::EngineKind>` overloads map every engine onto `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU`.
- Real-time failures are latched (`anira/utils/RtLatch.h`): a refused `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` or `_wait` entry records its status in `anira_handler_rt_error` and logs one Error record per kind through the real-time queue, flagged `ANIRA_LOG_RECORD_CONTRACT_VIOLATION` (`"<entry>: <status text>"`), later occurrences count; prepare and reset re-arm and log the count. The fourteen operational real-time sites of the scheduler and the five engines (missing samples, an output nobody pops, a dropped inference, an engine without a model, a failed inference) log their first occurrence since the last prepare and count the rest; the drain (the `anira-log` thread, or `anira_drain_log` under `ANIRA_LOG_DRAIN_MANUAL` and on WebAssembly) reports a condition whose count grew at most every 10 seconds ("still failing, N more suppressed") without re-arming it. A failed inference — an engine exception, or a custom processor or a `before_inference` / `after_inference` hook that throws — zero-fills its output, sets `rt_error` to `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` and emits one latched record; every inference-thread body runs under a catch, so no exception ends a pool or user thread through tanh-lib's top-level handler, and the four engines without one get the RAII busy-flag guard LibTorch had, so a throwing instance is never left busy.
- Docs: `docs/sphinx/usage.rst` gains section 3.2 "The C handler" (the entries of `anira/abi/handler.h` in the reference style of 1.6 and 3.1) and the on_miss, wait and ring-dtype semantics under 1.3; `logging.rst`'s real-time section describes the shipped `rt_error`, latch and summary (`ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` included) instead of announcing them; `latency.rst` names `wait_ratio` and the `_wait` twins where it said `blocking_ratio`; `migration.rst` says which handler is which; the architecture document's section 4 states that the core reads the context config directly.
- CI: a pull request's `clang_tidy` run checks only the `.cpp` files the pull request changes (`CHANGED_FILES_ONLY` of `tanh-lab/ci-actions`' `clang-tidy-check`, which reads the list from git, no token), so the job is the three-minute configure and build plus those files, instead of the 20-minute sweep that any header change used to force. The merge queue and a manual run (`workflow_dispatch`) still sweep every source, and so does a pull request that changes `.clang-tidy` or the workflow itself (the two files that change tidy's own verdicts). A header or build-file change no longer widens the set: what it breaks in an untouched file fails in the merge queue, not on the pull request. Every workflow moves from `tanh-lab/ci-actions` v0.3.10 to v0.3.14 with it (the `build_test` gate allows one version repo-wide): beside the tidy inputs (v0.3.13) that range adds the libcurl headers to the Linux tool setup, and a coverage export that tolerates one invalid `.profraw` and reports it (v0.3.14), after the `static-executorch` coverage leg lost a race on the 248-byte profile of `anira_abi_layout` three runs out of five.
- CI: `clang_tidy result` reports on every pull request, so it can be a required check and the merge queue's full sweep gates a merge (as a path-filtered, unrequired check its failure in the queue did not block one). The workflow's `paths:` filter is gone; a pull request that changes no `.cpp` file and neither `.clang-tidy` nor the workflow skips the fixture restore, the tool setup and the build, so its job is a checkout and one script (`tanh-lab/ci-actions/changed-files` run first, with the same sweep pattern the tidy action uses).
- `tools/abi/gen.py` models what the tensor records need: a `union` entity and a named union member of a Tier-1 struct, their arms being leaves or unnamed struct types with a member name (never a truly anonymous struct, which ISO C++ does not have); one layout tree behind `abi/layout-<major>.txt`, `test/abi/generated/test_layout.c` and `web/src/abi/layout.ts`, with one row per member and, inside an unnamed struct or union, one per member of it, written as the C designator (`field anira_sync_token.u.vk.value offset 16 size 8`; a union block starts with `union` instead of `struct`); `function_type: true` on a callback (`typedef R (ANIRA_CALL name)(...)`, so `ANIRA_PTR(name, slot)` is a function pointer) with `forward: true` on the struct it names (the `typedef struct X X;` is emitted ahead of the first entity that names `X`); a header-level `draft: true`, which belongs to a file under `draft/` holding `status: draft` functions only (guard `ANIRA_ABI_DRAFT_*`, names in `abi/symbols-draft.txt`); `struct_id:` linking a record to its `anira_struct_id` constant, required on every Tier-1 record, from which `src/capi/generated/struct_sizes.inc` (the table behind `anira_sizeof`) is generated; and the refusal of a member name repeated within one C scope, an `ANIRA_PTR` slot taking both `name` and `name_bits` (`--check` exits 1: `config.h:anira_vulkan_desc: duplicate field device in anira_vulkan_desc`, the run recorded in the pull request). `--diff-against` classifies a changed union arm (an appended one included) as a Tier-1 layout change, a callback turned function type as a signature change and a removed `callback_safe` as a break, and admits whatever happens to a `status: draft` function (changed, removed, promoted) as outside the promise. `padding_free: true` on a Tier-2 struct asserts in `test_layout.c` that `sizeof` equals the sum of the member sizes on every target; an array extent below 1, a function type as a parameter and a malformed member list are registry errors, and `layout.ts` refuses a generated line over 90 columns. `--check` covers 58 files.
- `cmake/abi-layout-check.cmake` reads the ABI major from the name of the table it is given and says which rule was broken: while the major is 0 the table grows with the registry (run `gen.py --write`, commit `abi/layout-0.txt` with the headers; if `gen.py --check` is clean the compiler disagrees with the generator's model), from the freeze on a Tier-1 layout changes only with `ANIRA_ABI_MAJOR`.
- The `ANIRA_DTYPE_F32` doc names the accessor that now exists: float32 is the only type `anira_tensor_data_f32` returns.
- Interleaved and planar host blocks reach the rings through the C entries now: `anira_handler_process` and its siblings copy every description of a host tensor straight between the host memory and the ring, one strided ring call per channel, for any channel count; a host with an interleaved device callback no longer de-interleaves into scratch buffers before it calls anira.
- Docs: `docs/sphinx/usage.rst` 3.2 describes the Hard entries over host tensors first (one tensor per slot, the three descriptions of memory with a planar example over JUCE's channel pointers that needs no cast and an interleaved example that assigns `strides = {1, channels}` after `anira_tensor_init_host`, in place, the empty tensor, the refusals and their order, `delivered`), then a host with channel pointers (one planar tensor built once over the pointer array, `shape[1]` stored before each call) and the status and counts of every entry; 1.3 and the contract-file section describe `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK`, the two-step for a parsed `"callback"` and the `ANIRA_NONBLOCKING` declaration a backup function needs under clang; 3.3 no longer says that no entry takes a tensor. The quick start of 3.1 and sections 1.3 and 5 call the tensor entries. `logging.rst` names the refusals of a host tensor; `migration.rst` says that a host of the C ABI presents its channel pointers itself; `contributing.rst` names what `anira_abi_rt_contract` compiles now.
- Docs: `docs/sphinx/usage.rst` section 2 is "Processors", the C descriptor as the guide describes it (the one processor per pipeline, the phases of one chunk with the edge directly before and after the engine and the thread each phase runs on, NULL against filled slots and the explicit "call super", the context accessors per slot with the `entry` index and `anira_handler_num_entries`, the ring accessors with the no-conversion rule and the ring-dtype exception, the hop rule, the failure rules, the Static snapshot and the write order of a chunk, what a State slot answers in each phase, the flags against the placement, prepare and release, an int16-converting processor in C and its `anira::Processor` twin), section 1.3 gains the host domain beside the ring dtype and 1.5 the `host_domains` key, the State role of 1.1 points to the new section 3.4 "State tensors" (the declared pair with the bundled StatefulAccumulatorNetwork's file, feed and capture, a failed inference, reset, plan switches, what the host and a processor see, the validation rules, the engine rows the fixture names and why), 3.2 describes the one processor and what prepare checks for it, and 5.3 and 5.4 show the Static entries beside the 2.x calls. `custom_preprocessing.rst` is rewritten onto the processor (when a processor is needed, the rules, a normalising processor, a spectrogram processor whose transform runs in `pre_process` under `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST`, the split over the entry scratch that moves a heavy transform to `before_inference` / `after_inference` in C, Static and State tensors in a processor, the integration in C and C++), with one note pointing 2.x readers to `migration.rst`, which gains the mapping of the 2.x handler calls onto the C entries (the slots, the `_wait` twins, the Static entries) and of every `PrePostProcessor` virtual and helper onto the processor. `contributing.rst` states the dual tag's rule for entries against callback typedefs; `logging.rst` names what a processor latches; `latency.rst` and `getting_started.rst` say what a slot of the latency vector and a 2.x non-streamable block are. The API reference leaves the `anira::detail` namespace out (`EXCLUDE_SYMBOLS` of `docs/doxygen/Doxyfile.in`), so the pages of the `anira.hpp` helpers (`ExtTraits`, `KeptExt`, `ProcessorTrampolines`) go, and the six stale pages whose compounds Doxygen no longer emits (`anira::Buffer`, `MemoryBlock`, `RingBuffer`, the three `Processor::Instance` structs) go with them.
- Docs: `docs/sphinx/usage.rst` gains section 3.3 "Runtime tensors" (the factories and their refusals, the NULL rules of the accessors, planar host memory, release and ownership, sync tokens, the draft folder and its promotion rule, the MSVC `/wd4201` note), and 1.3 and 3.2 say that the tensor forms of the Hard entries are reserved for a later pre-release; `contributing.rst` describes the new entity kinds and keys of the registry, the duplicate-member rule, the closed allowlist of six and the pre-freeze growth of the layout table, and under "Sanitizers" names `anira_abi_rt_contract` as the compile-time half of the RTSan contract and the function sanitizer behind the `no_sanitize("function")` of the DLPack trampoline. The API reference gains `anira_tensor`, `anira_sync_token`, `anira::Tensor`, `anira::SyncToken` and, through a new union list (`breathe-apidoc -g class,struct,union`), `anira_memory_handle`; `ANIRA_PTR(T,name)=T* name` in the Doxygen `PREDEFINED` list makes every pointer slot a member instead of a function named `ANIRA_PTR`, which also repairs the page of `anira_log_record`.
- tanh-lib v0.4.0 pinned for the strided ring calls (tanh-lab/tanh-lib#50: `thl::core::RingBuffer<T>::push_block`, `pop_block` and `peek_past_block` take a `stride`, counted in elements, so one channel of an interleaved block moves between the host's memory and a ring in one copy, without a de-interleaved block in between). `anira_ring` (`anira/utils/RingBuffer.h`) forwards it: the dtype-checked `push_block`, `pop_block` and `peek_past_block` gain `size_t stride = 1` as their last parameter (1 or more; 1 is the packed run and the unit-stride path of the storage; a disagreeing dtype still returns 0 with nothing written; a push larger than the capacity keeps its tail and a pop beyond the available count value-initialises the strided tail and nothing between), and `element_size()` reports `sizeof(T)` of the instantiation behind the ring's dtype. The float face and `pop_windows` are unchanged, and nothing in the scheduler uses the stride yet. tanh-tooling stays v0.2.8 on both sides.
- **One host<->ring copy path, over `anira_tensor`.** `InferenceManager`'s `process_input`, `process_output` and the three miss policies (`hold_output`, `bypass_output`, `clear_output`) are written once over `const anira_tensor*`, one tensor per slot in slot order, and the float face of the library, `anira::InferenceHandler`, runs on it: it keeps its signatures, its in/out count protocol and its return values and presents the caller's channel pointers as planar float32 tensors that prepare builds once (a call stores `shape[1]` and one pointer per channel; nothing is allocated on the driver thread). Every block it delivered before it delivers bit for bit: the float core was recorded first (`test_scheduler` suite `CopyPathOracle`, `test_abi` suite `AbiHandlerCopyOracle`: every float a call writes and every float it leaves alone, under the three miss policies) and the rewritten core reproduces the recording. New on `InferenceManager`, as the C++ face the tensor entries call: `process_nowait(inputs, outputs)`, `process_wait(inputs, outputs, budget, outcome)`, `push_data(inputs)`, `pop_data(outputs)`, `pop_data_wait(outputs, budget, outcome)` and `contract_wait_budget(inputs, outputs)` over `const anira_tensor*` arrays, returning the manager's own array of delivered counts, and the two 2.x behaviours in the same form: `process(inputs, outputs)`, the dispatcher on `blocking_ratio`, and `pop_data(outputs, wait_until)`, the deadline pop. A slot's tensor is a host block of the logical shape `[channels, samples]` (`[1, values]` for a non-streamable slot) in any of three descriptions, for any channel count: planar (`ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR`, `byte_offset` and `strides[1]` inside each plane), one block read by `strides` (interleaved is `{1, channels}`, contiguous `{samples, 1}`), or one block with all-zero strides (packed). Each channel moves between host memory and the ring in one strided ring call, with no intermediate copy; a unit stride is the ring's plain block copy, as before. The request is `shape[1]`; a slot whose `shape[1]` is 0 is not touched and its memory is not read; a descriptor is never written; in place is the same tensor on both sides. The copy is dtype-generic: the element type is the tensor's, which must be the slot's ring dtype (float32 for a non-streamable slot), nothing converts, and `HOLD_LAST` holds bytes of the ring's element type. The stems trust their descriptors (the caller validates them; `src/scheduler/TensorRun.h` carries that check, `check_host_tensor`, beside the run helper) and check the dtype only: a mismatched input is not pushed, a mismatched output is zero-filled and reports 0 samples, logged once through the real-time queue under the new site `RtSite::TensorDtypeMismatch`. That also ends a silent failure of a float32 call on a session prepared with a non-float32 `RingDtypes` entry, which pushed and popped nothing and still reported a delivered block over memory it had not written. On a `BYPASS` miss, input and output memory that overlap under two different descriptions are undefined; the identical run (in place) is left where it is.
- **Breaking (pre-release ABI 0.x, the Hard entries):** the Hard entries carry Streamed tensors, and a Static tensor travels whole. Against the entries of the previous pre-release commits (#189, #190; neither shipped in a release, `v3.0.0-alpha.1` has no handler entry, so the Breaking lines of this cycle about handler entries concern builds from `v3` snapshots alone): (1) the flat `[1, values]` description of a Static slot, its clamp and the clamped count are gone: the element of a Static slot in a `_multi` call is the whole tensor in the spec's shape and dtype, exactly what `anira_handler_set_static_input` / `anira_handler_get_static_output` take, or an empty tensor; another rank or extent is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, another dtype `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, `ANIRA_TENSOR_PLANAR` `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`; there is no partial Static tensor and no count. (2) A `_multi` call is *defined* as the sequence it abbreviates, over the same functions: `set_static_input` for every non-empty Static element of `inputs`, the streamed call over the Streamed elements, `get_static_output` for every non-empty Static element of `outputs`; every element is validated before anything is set or pushed. (3) The empty element generalises from `shape[1] == 0` to a rank of 1 or more with any extent 0; nothing else of it is read. (4) A single form (`anira_handler_process`, `push_data`, `pop_data` and the `_wait` twins) whose slot names a Static tensor on either side is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`: the single form of a Static slot is the pair of Static entries, so a generator with Static input 0 calls `anira_handler_set_static_input(h, 0, ..)` and `anira_handler_pop_data(h, .., 0, ..)`. (5) `ANIRA_MISS_ZEROS`, `ANIRA_MISS_HOLD_LAST` and `ANIRA_MISS_BYPASS` define nothing for a Static output: on a missed block a carried Static output holds the stored value (the latest the model produced), where it used to be zeroed or repeated per policy, and its `delivered` count is its element count on `ANIRA_OK` and on `ANIRA_MISSED` alike (0 for an element left out). (6) Under `ANIRA_MISS_CALLBACK` the handler fills the non-empty Static output elements of a missed block with the stored value *first* and then calls `anira_miss_fn` with the caller's arrays; the function may leave or overwrite them and has the last word on every output of the block, the get step behind the streamed call is skipped for that block, and what the function writes never enters the store. Migration: build the Static tensor once with `anira_tensor_init_host` over the spec's extents (`[1]` for the bundled gain models, where the flat form was `[1, 1]`), hand it to the two Static entries or use it as the `_multi` element, request the whole tensor instead of a count, and replace a single-form call on a Static slot by the Static entry. The 2.x `anira::InferenceHandler` keeps its behaviour (the count of values inside the `float***` block, the clamp, the miss rules). The recorded C-face oracle (`test/abi/handler_copy_oracle_golden.h`) was recorded again for its Static lines alone, in the five transcripts that have a Static slot: a call block differs from the float core's recording only where its call carried a non-empty Static element or named a Static slot, and only in the call label, the Static slot's request and `delivered` / `num_out` word and the Static slot's float row; every status, `rt_error=`, ring level, `settled:` line and row of a Streamed slot is byte-identical, which a masked comparison of the two files showed. The manager-face oracle (`test/scheduler/copy_path_oracle_golden.h`) is untouched.
- **Breaking (the manager's C++ face):** `anira::InferenceManager` takes host tensors only. Its seven `float***` overloads are gone from the installed header `anira/scheduler/InferenceManager.h`: `process`, `process_nowait`, `process_wait`, `push_data`, `pop_data`, the deadline form of `pop_data` and `pop_data_wait` over `const float* const* const*` / `float* const* const*` beside `size_t*` count arrays, and with them the count-array form of `contract_wait_budget` (the tensor form measures the block by `shape[1]` of the anchored tensor). The float adapter they shared with the rest of the library moved out of the manager into one private helper, `anira::PlanarFloatAdapter` (`src/scheduler/PlanarFloatAdapter.h`, not installed), which `anira::InferenceHandler` owns, so nothing changes for its users: every `process`, `push_data` and `pop_data` overload keeps its signature, its count protocol and every float it delivers (the recorded copy-path suites `CopyPathOracle` and `AbiHandlerCopyOracle` pass with their transcripts untouched). This change touches no C entry and no registry line; the C ABI change of the same cycle is under Removed. Migration, for code that drives an `InferenceManager` directly: present one `anira_tensor` per slot to the tensor form of the same name. For channel pointers that is `anira_tensor_init_host_planar(&t, channels, num_channels, ANIRA_DTYPE_F32, 2, shape)` with `shape = {num_channels, num_samples}` (`{1, num_values}` for a non-streamable slot), a slot the call leaves out is an empty tensor (`shape[1] == 0`, memory may be NULL), in place is the same tensor on both sides, and the returned `const size_t*` is the manager's own array of delivered counts (0 for every slot of a missed block), so the caller's request array is no longer overwritten.
- A Static or a Buffer tensor spec may carry a Channel axis of any extent: `anira_handler_create`, `anira_handler_prepare` and the 2.x bridge no longer refuse such a spec with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` ("a Static tensor's Channel axis must have extent 1"). The Channel tag maps onto ring channels on a Streamed tensor only; a non-Streamed tensor has the spec's shape, the tagged axis being one of its axes, and moves as the product of its extents (the 2.x config the translator builds carries it as one channel with the full dims in every shape). At most one Channel axis per spec stays the rule for every role. The rule is about the spec: a Buffer spec with such an axis is valid in a model config and a model file, while `anira_handler_prepare` refuses any Buffer spec under a Hard contract (see the Static entries under Added).
- The session of a C-created handler runs one processor, the processor runner (`ProcessorRunner`, `src/capi/processor.cpp`), whether its pipeline has a processor or not: without one it runs the two default bodies, which are the streamed branches of the 2.x default `PrePostProcessor`, and both recorded copy-path oracles stay byte-identical. Static tensors travel through the handler's Static store (the float atomics the processor inherits from the 2.x processor are not used). `anira_ring` (`anira/utils/RingBuffer.h`) gains `set_owner` / `hop` / `owner` (the hop of its slot and the owning session's `anira::RingOwner`: the latch a refused accessor records into and the processor running on the driving thread), set by `SessionElement::prepare`; `SessionElement::ThreadSafeStruct` gains `m_processor_status`, which `Core::pre_process` and `InferenceThread::do_inference` read after the processor's hooks (a 2.x processor never writes it); `anira::RtLatch` gains `record_any` and `RtKind::Other`, so that a processor's status without a kind bit of its own still reaches `rt_error`; `InferenceManager::session()` hands the processor runner the session it binds to.
- The 2.x `anira::InferenceHandler` routes the non-streamable tensors of its `float***` blocks itself. `anira::NonStreamableRouter` (`src/scheduler/NonStreamableRouter.h`, private to the library like `PlanarFloatAdapter`, which it stands beside) wraps every tensor stem of `InferenceManager`: it stores the carried non-streamable inputs in the session's `PrePostProcessor` (the count clamped to the tensor's size), hands the stem views of the two tensor arrays in which every non-streamable slot is left out, and reads the requested non-streamable outputs back afterwards (the clamped count on a delivered block; on a missed block `HOLD_LAST` writes `min(request, size)` values and every other policy zeroes the request as it came, the count 0). It is the copy core's non-streamed code moved as it was, over tensor arrays, so `InferenceHandler`, the `CopyPathOracle` rig and the `TensorStems` replays (which present the Static tensors under every memory description) go through it and both recorded oracles stay byte-identical; nothing changes for a 2.x caller. The copy core itself moves streamed slots only: `InferenceManager`'s stems never read the tensor of a non-streamable slot (whatever it holds: the whole Static tensor of a C `_multi` call, of any rank), report 0 for it and leave its memory alone on a miss; its non-streamed branches (`process_input`, `process_output`, the `HOLD_LAST` and the unclamped zeroing arms of a miss) are gone, and with them the manager's reference to the `PrePostProcessor`. `InferenceManager::miss_policy()` reads back what `set_miss_policy()` stored.
- **One slot space.** A slot is the tensor's position in the model config's input list or output list, State tensors included, and that one number is used everywhere: `slot`, `in_slot` and `out_slot` of every `anira_handler_*` entry (every single form names its index `slot`: `anira_handler_push_data`, `pop_data`, `pop_data_wait`, `get_latency` and `get_available_samples`), the positions of the arrays and `delivered` counts of the `_multi` forms, the arrays `anira_miss_fn` receives, the vector of `anira_handler_get_latencies`, the plan report's slot rows, and the `slot` of a processor's context accessors (`anira_processor_input_role`, `anira_processor_input_ring`, `anira_processor_input_tensor` and their output twins). The role of the tensor decides which entries take its slot: a Streamed one the block calls (`process`, `push_data`, `pop_data`, their `_multi` and `_wait` forms), a Static one `anira_handler_set_static_input` / `anira_handler_get_static_output` or an element of a `_multi` form, a State one no Hard entry. A single form that names a Static or a State slot is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`. The arrays of the `_multi` forms are as long as the model config's lists (`num_inputs` and `num_outputs` must equal those lengths), and the position of a State tensor must hold the empty tensor (a rank of 1 or more with an extent of 0; a zeroed record has rank 0 and is refused), checked in the same validate-everything-first walk as the Static elements, so a refusal sets and pushes nothing; its `delivered` count is always 0. `anira_handler_get_latency` and `anira_handler_get_latencies` have one entry per output tensor, 0 for one that is not Streamed, and `anira_handler_get_available_samples` reads 0 there. The caller's arrays reach the copy path as they are, and `anira_miss_fn` receives the caller's own arrays. A host that does not want to hard-code a slot resolves it by the tensor's canonical name at setup, and the plan report says what a slot is: `anira_plan_slot.role`, the last field of the row, is the `anira_role` of the slot's spec (the same in every plan), which a processor reads in its `prepare` through the report it receives. The slot refusals of the Hard and the Static entries name the tensor's canonical name beside the slot number in their log record.
- **Breaking (pre-release ABI 0.x):** `anira_handler_process` and `anira_handler_process_wait` take one slot per side: `anira_handler_process(handler, in, in_slot, out, out_slot, delivered)` and `anira_handler_process_wait(handler, in, in_slot, out, out_slot, delivered, timeout_ms)`. One index shared by two unrelated lists only ever worked for a model whose stream stands at the same position on both sides; a model with `data` at input 1 and `processed_data` at output 0 (declared state first in, last out) calls `anira_handler_process(h, &in, 1, &out, 0, &delivered)`. In place stays the same tensor on both sides. Migration: a call `anira_handler_process(h, &in, &out, 0, &delivered)` becomes `anira_handler_process(h, &in, 0, &out, 0, &delivered)`, and `anira_handler_process_wait(h, &in, &out, timeout_ms, 0, &delivered)` becomes `anira_handler_process_wait(h, &in, 0, &out, 0, &delivered, timeout_ms)`, and `anira_handler_pop_data_wait(h, &out, timeout_ms, 0, &delivered)` becomes `anira_handler_pop_data_wait(h, &out, 0, &delivered, timeout_ms)` (one rule for the four `_wait` twins: the bare form, then `timeout_ms`). Neither form shipped in a release: `v3.0.0-alpha.1` has no tensor entries.
- `anira_handler_reset` and `anira_handler_prepare` re-initialise declared state to zeros, and `anira_handler_reset` reaches the processor's own state and the engine's own state through the two reset slots. The reset itself stays one generation bump: the state is zeroed on the inference thread, at the first inference of the new stream (keyed on the chunk's dispatch generation stamp), so an inference still in flight across the reset cannot seed the new stream with its capture; the processor's `reset` slot runs for the first chunk of the new stream, before that chunk's `pre_process` and on its thread, and so it does for the first chunk after `anira_handler_prepare` (`ANIRA_PHASE_RESET`), the processor runner telling that chunk by its dispatch stamp, never by the session's atomic, which is why `Core::pre_process` stamps the chunk's generation ahead of the `pre_process` virtual, on the same thread, instead of behind it; the engine's `reset` slot of `anira/abi/engine.h` is the same boundary on the inference thread for an exclusive handler's prepared handle (`ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL`, a declared pair), run with the first inference's context right before its `process` (TensorFlow Lite resets the variable tensors of the handler's own interpreter there, a registered engine whatever it keeps; a handler whose model is stateless is never reset). State a 2.x `BackendBase` keeps inside itself stays untouched, as before.
- A model with a declared state pair runs as `ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL` (an exclusive handler, one inference at a time and in order) whatever its `state` says.
- The 2.x bridge `anira::v3compat::to_inference_config` refuses a model with a State spec with `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, naming the tensor: a 2.x `InferenceHandler` feeds no state back, and the model would run on a zero state without a word. The 2.x path is otherwise untouched.
- **Breaking (rename, pre-release API):** the enum alias of `anira_engine` in `anira/anira.hpp` is `anira::EngineKind` (was `anira::Engine`, the name the class of a custom engine takes now): `ModelConfig::add_model_path(EngineKind, path)`, `add_model_bytes(EngineKind, ...)`, `model_engine(i)`, `default_engine(EngineKind)`, `ProcessorContext::engine()` and the `std::span<const anira::EngineKind>` candidate overloads of `<anira/compat/v3_to_v2.h>` spell it; a consumer that wrote `anira::Engine` for the enum renames.
- **Breaking (announced, effective with the runtime cut-over):** subclassing `anira::BackendBase` goes with the 2.x runtime and gets no shim: a 2.x custom backend is rewritten as an `anira::Engine` (or an `anira_engine_desc`) registered on the pipeline under its id, which `docs/sphinx/migration.rst` maps virtual by virtual; and a name in a `tensors` record on a side an engine binds by position is refused (`ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED`, above), not accepted and ignored.
- Docs: `docs/sphinx/usage.rst` section 2 describes the shared lifecycle of the processor (the prepare record, the per-handler `prepared` pointer, `reset`, `unprepare`, `release`, the `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_*` names; the C example keeps its scratch behind `prepared` and frees it in `unprepare`, the C++ example is an `anira::Processor` whose `prepare` returns the `Processor::Prepared` with the scratch), `docs/sphinx/custom_preprocessing.rst` is rewritten onto `Processor` / `Processor::Prepared` (one registration, one prepared object per handler with its scratch, the phases and `reset` on it, why `unique_ptr`; the C spectral example over `prepared`, `reset` and `unprepare`), and `docs/sphinx/migration.rst`'s processor rows follow. `docs/sphinx/custom_engines.rst` is "Custom Engines", rewritten around `anira_engine_desc` and `anira::Engine` (what an engine is and where it sits, the registration and its refusals, the descriptor's slots, the lifecycle with its records and the context, the adapter rule, the flags and where a registered engine appears in the reports, declared state over the two buffers and the dtype rule, pooling and lifetime, a gain engine in C and in C++, the version-neutral section on driving a bundled engine from a custom one kept with its example as an `Engine::Prepared` that owns the engine's objects, and a note sending `BackendBase` readers to the migration guide); `usage.rst` 1.2 names the registered id and the refusal of an unregistered one, 2 says what the phases bind and flip and that a State pair takes any dtype the engine accepts, 3.2 describes the registration in place of the placeholder (the entry, the slots, the context, the refusals, the pool rule, the lifecycle-lock rule, `engine_flags` and `binding` in the report), 4.3 says that the C handler selects plans and that a custom backend is a registered engine, and 5.5 says what a reset re-initialises (declared state, the engine's own state, the processor's own state); `migration.rst` gains the registration entries, a `BackendBase` -> `anira::Engine` table beside the processor table and the C handler's refusal of an unregistered id; `architecture.rst`, `benchmarking.rst`, `getting_started.rst` and `troubleshooting.rst` say in one sentence each where a custom engine lives now; `contributing.rst` states that an `[inference-thread]` callback typedef carries no `nonblocking` (the promise is the flag), the naming scheme of the two descriptors (`processor` / `engine` on every level, `FLAG` in the flag defines, `Prepared` for what `prepare` hands back) and the callback shape `(ctx, prepared, user_data)` as the convention every later descriptor keeps. The C++ examples of `usage.rst` section 2 and `custom_preprocessing.rst` define `prepare` after the nested `Prepared` class it allocates (an inline body allocated an incomplete nested class and did not compile). The API reference gains the pages of `anira::Engine`, `EngineContext` and `EngineLoadInfo`, the nested `Engine::Prepared` and the internal `SessionElement::PlanSlot` stay out of it like `Processor::Prepared` and `ThreadSafeStruct`, and the five processor pages are gone.
- The scheduler runs every engine through one interface, the engine room's adapter (`src/engines/Adapter.h`, the shape of the C descriptor of `anira/abi/engine.h`): a session's plan table (`SessionElement::m_plans`, one `PlanSlot` per plan with the two pointers of the C lifecycle, the loaded model it runs on as a shared `anira::engine::Loaded` and the session's own `anira::engine::Prepared` over it, declared after the `Loaded` so that it dies first, its engine, provider and engine id, its 2.x backend and whether a 2.x row has no model) replaces the five `#ifdef` processor pools of the core, the five typed processor members and the custom-processor pointer of the session and the five-way engine switch of the inference thread. `Core::create_session` takes the plans as `engine::PlanRequest`s (the 2.x constructors ask for the table the core always built through `engine::legacy_plan_requests`: one plan per configured model, then every other backend of the build, CUSTOM last; a C-created handler asks for exactly its plans through the new `InferenceManager` constructor over requests, and `InferenceManager::set_plan_backends` is gone: the table is the session's from create), pools loaded models by the record of the model (`engine::Model`: engine, id, path or bytes identity, entry, per-slot names, dims and dtype, the shared slots, warm-up, and a custom engine's variant as canonical JSON; whether a session runs the model exclusively is the session's property, `engine::PrepareRequest`, not the record's) and the carrier of a registered engine instead of by `InferenceConfig::operator==` (two handlers with different resolved windows never share; budget, blocking ratio and processing spec no longer keep equal models apart), never pools a 2.x custom backend or the roundtrip, and releases a pool entry by pointer identity when the plan table of the last registered session lets go. The engine room is three classes, the C lifecycle's levels: `Loaded` (one loaded model, the C `loaded` pointer: `init(const anira_init_info&)`, the engine's init once per object with the facts of the core, which the core calls right before the first `load`; `load(const Model&)`, once, under the lifecycle lock, which keeps a copy of the record, loads the model (`do_load`) and sizes the shared call slots to the record's `instances`; `prepare(const PrepareRequest&)`, once per session that runs the model, whose result the session's plan slot holds; it holds the shared slots and, once instead of five times, the spin-and-claim loop the 2.x processors each carried, `claim_and_run`: a shared call claims the first slot whose busy flag was clear and the claimed index travels in the context, `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` and no engine call on a model without a shared slot), `Prepared` (one session's handle over a loaded model, the C `prepared` pointer: `run(ctx, chunk, reset_first)`, what the inference thread calls, copies the context, writes the loaded pointer into it and, for an exclusive session, flags the call `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE` with `instance` 0, resets first when asked and runs it on what the session keeps; a shared session's call goes through the claim loop) and `Executor` (one call slot of a loaded model, the engine's executor, what one process call runs on and never two at once: `process`, `reset`, `warm_up`), with `ExecutorLoaded` / `ExecutorPrepared` as the shape of the five built-in engines: `do_load` loads the engine's shared object, makes one executor over it (the probe, whose view of the model the binding rule runs over) and hands it to `adopt`, which makes the executors of the shared slots with `make_executor` (the probe is slot 0) or keeps the probe as the spare the first exclusive session takes when the record has no shared slot, and warms every one of them up with the record's FIXED warm-up; an exclusive session's prepare takes the spare or one more `make_executor`, warmed up there, and its `Prepared` owns it. `InferenceThread::inference` builds an `anira_engine_ctx` per chunk over the struct's descriptors (`ThreadSafeStruct::m_input_tensors` / `m_output_tensors`, built once at prepare over the buffers and re-pointed at every claim), runs the plan slot's `Prepared::run` with it, telling it to reset first at the first inference of a new stream of an exclusive session (the chunk's dispatch stamp against `SessionElement::m_engine_generation`, adopted there), and treats an engine status other than `ANIRA_OK` as a failed chunk: zeros at its stream position, `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` latched with one log line naming the status, and no `after_inference` (the rule of a failed hook; a 2.x `PrePostProcessor::after_inference` override no longer runs behind a failed engine). The five built-in engines are adapters of the descriptor shape (below), a registered engine runs behind `DescriptorAdapter`, and a caller's 2.x `BackendBase` (the 2.x custom constructor, the Emscripten wrapper's `JSProcessor`, the extras and benchmark processors, the test mocks) rides behind `LegacyAdapter` until the runtime cut-over, called as concurrently as the scheduler dispatches, as before, with its failure logged unlatched beside the scheduler's latched record. The processor context's tensor accessors hand out a copy of the chunk's descriptor instead of rebuilding one per question, and `ProcessorRunner::bind` reads the engine and the provider of a plan from the session's table. The five real-time sites `NoLibTorchModel` .. `NoExecuTorchModel` are one site, `RtSite::NoModelForBackend` (`k_rt_site_names` shortened with it).
- Every engine of the C handler binds its tensors by name where the engine's side has names and by position otherwise, checked at prepare and reported per slot, on every built-in engine: a slot whose entry's `tensors` record names it binds to the engine tensor of that name (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` at prepare, listing the file's names, when the engine has none of that name); a slot without a record binds to the engine tensor of its canonical name where the side has one and to the tensor at the slot's position otherwise (ONNX Runtime's graph order, the signature's key order on TFLite and LiteRT, the method's argument order on LibTorch, the method's order on ExecuTorch); every engine tensor of a side ends up bound exactly once (a duplicate or an unbound engine tensor is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming it); whichever way a slot was bound, the engine tensor's element type, rank and every static extent are checked against the spec's engine dims (the entry's layout applied; a dynamic extent of the file matches anything), a mismatch being `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the slot, the engine tensor and both shapes; `anira_plan_slot.binding` reports `ANIRA_BINDING_NAME` / `ANIRA_BINDING_POSITION` per slot of every plan (the 2.x custom plan reports position) and the prepare log's slot line says `bound by name` / `bound by position`. The rule is one shared piece of the engine room (`anira::engine::bind_slots`, `check_engine_tensor`, `bind_side` in `src/engines/Adapter.h`) that every built-in adapter calls at prepare; the 2.x path binds by position as before. A name in a `tensors` record on a side its engine binds by position (every ExecuTorch tensor, a LibTorch output) is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` at `anira_handler_create` ("binds by position; drop the name, keep the layout") instead of being accepted and ignored.
- ONNX Runtime runs as an adapter over `anira_tensor` (`src/engines/OnnxRuntimeAdapter.cpp`, file-local over the engine room's `Model`, replacing `OnnxRuntimeProcessor`): what one load shares between its executors is one environment handle (anira's log level on it; ONNX Runtime keeps one environment per process behind every handle) and one session-options object (one intra-op thread), immutable after load and freed with the last of them, and each executor owns one `Ort::Session` over them, kept per executor so that no two executors share a session's allocator (the web build creates the environment with a global thread pool of one intra-op thread through `Ort::ThreadingOptions`, since a WebAssembly session cannot spawn its own; the environment copies the options, which die with the load); every input of a call an `Ort::Value` created over the descriptor's memory and every output a value pre-created over the output descriptor's memory and handed to `Session::Run`, so no result is copied and a declared State pair will bind in place (the copied form is gone; a shape ONNX Runtime cannot honour fails the FIXED warm-up at prepare with its text); the float32 rule (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the engine and the first tensor of another dtype, before the file is opened); a descriptor the adapter cannot hand the engine fails the chunk with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` and a failing run with `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE`, both latched by the scheduler with the engine's text logged unlatched, nothing cleared or recorded inside the adapter; the FIXED warm-up runs through the same process path over the instance's own scratch.
- LibTorch runs as an adapter over `anira_tensor` (`src/engines/LibTorchAdapter.cpp`, replacing `LibtorchProcessor`): one TorchScript module per executor (a module is not shareable between threads, so every executor loads its own, with its own copy of the weights), what one load shares being the record and the binding tables alone; every input of a call is a `torch::from_blob` view over the descriptor's memory (no deleter, nothing owned, the pointer read anew per call), so the swap of the struct's memory is gone (`AbiProcessor.TensorsKeepTheStructsMemoryAcrossLibTorchInferences` asserts one input block per struct across uses); the input slots bind to the entry method's arguments after `self` by name where the entry's tensors record or the canonical name matches one and by argument position otherwise (a record name the method lacks is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` listing the arguments; a bound argument must be a tensor; every argument without a default value must be bound, a trailing argument with a default stays with it, so RAVE's `decode(z, from_forward=False)` binds its one input), the outputs bind by position to the method's returns (a tuple of tensors, one tensor or a tensor list; the schema's arity is checked where it says); the schema carries no shapes, so an output's dtype and shape are checked against the record on the warm-up's first result (`ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming both shapes) and every result is checked again per call (`ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE`, nothing written); the result tensors are the engine's own and are copied once into the output descriptors' memory (a `memcpy` of a contiguous result, a `copy_` into a view over the memory otherwise); a module without the entry method is `ANIRA_ERROR_MODEL_LOAD` at prepare instead of a `c10::Error` at the warm-up.
- ExecuTorch runs as an adapter over `anira_tensor` (`src/engines/ExecuTorchAdapter.cpp`, replacing `ExecuTorchProcessor`): what one load shares is one data loader and one `Program` (the .pte parsed once), and each executor owns one `Module` over the shared program with its own loaded entry method (the runtime's `Module` is not thread-safe; its constructor over a shared program is the documented way to run one program from several); every method is loaded with the XNNPACK delegate's runtime options `workspace_sharing_mode = 0` (a workspace per delegate instance) and `weight_cache_enabled = false` (no process-wide weight cache), which the delegate reads at its init, so two executors never meet on the process-wide mutexes the prebuilt runtime otherwise takes around every call, at the price of a workspace and an unpacked copy of the weights per executor, and it is loaded, warmed up and executed under `NoThreadPoolGuard`, so the delegate captures no thread pool and every call runs inline on the inference thread that made it (the process-global pin of the XNNPACK thread pool to one thread through the deprecated `_unsafe_reset_threadpool(1)` is gone with it); both sides bind by position (the method meta carries no tensor names) and are checked against the method metas at prepare, whose sizes are the planned upper bound of every axis with nothing marking an axis dynamic, so an extent at or below the bound matches and one above it, or another rank or element type, is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming both shapes; every input of a call is copied from its descriptor into the instance's staging tensor (the program's inputs are the runtime's own) and every memory-planned result copied out into its output descriptor; the FIXED warm-up runs over the staging tensors.
- TensorFlow Lite runs as an adapter over `anira_tensor` (`src/engines/TFLiteAdapter.cpp`, replacing `TFLiteProcessor`): what one load shares is one `TfLiteModel` (the flatbuffer parsed once) and one options object, and each executor owns one interpreter with its signature runner; the model's first signature through its `TfLiteSignatureRunner` when the file has one (the slots bind to the signature's keys by name where the entry's tensors record or the canonical name matches one and by the runner's index order otherwise, which lists the keys in key order: `output_0` before `output_1`), the interpreter itself for a file without a signature (the tensor names, the interpreter's order), every input the record's engine dims differ from resized to them before the allocation, every tensor checked against its allocated dims and type at prepare, every block copied from and into the descriptors' memory with `TfLiteTensorCopyFromBuffer` / `CopyToBuffer` per call (the swap of the struct's memory is gone; the interpreter's own alignment keeps the copy until the aligned chunk storage), and `reset` re-initialising the model's variable tensors through `TfLiteInterpreterResetVariableTensors` before the first inference of a new stream of an exclusive handler (no bundled model has variable tensors: the call is proven to be made, not to matter).
- LiteRT runs as an adapter over `anira_tensor` (`src/engines/LiteRtAdapter.cpp`, replacing `LiteRtProcessor`): what one load shares is one environment (carrying anira's log level), one model and one compilation options object, and each executor owns one compiled model with its managed buffers; the model's first signature (LiteRT synthesizes one for a file without signatures, keyed by the tensor names), the slots bound to the signature's keys by name or by the key order of the signature (the names sorted, as TensorFlow Lite's signature runner lists them: `output_0` before `output_1`, while the bundled exports list `output_1` first in the file), checked against the signature tensors' ranked types at prepare (a dynamic extent of the file matches anything), one managed host buffer per signature tensor in the signature's index order typed with the record's engine dims, filled from and read into the descriptors' memory through a lock and a memcpy per call. The bundled gain model loads and runs on LiteRT now: the earlier warm-up refusal ("Cannot auto-resize tensor args_0_1: no dims_signature exists") was a rank-1 buffer for the export's rank-3 static gain, which the file's litert row now describes.
- The bundled fixtures describe their TensorFlow exports: `gain.model.json` and `stereo_gain.model.json` carry on their tflite and litert rows a `tensors` record with a layout for the static gain (the export holds it at rank 3, `[1, 1, 1]`, against the spec's `[1]`; the adapters' shape check refuses the rank-1 form and says so with both shapes) and, on the litert row, the keys `output_0` / `output_1` for the two outputs (LiteRT lists them in the file's order, the peak first); `stateful_accumulator.model.json` gains a tflite row (no record: the signature runner's key order is the declared order) and a litert row naming `processed_data` as `output_0` and `state_out` as `output_1`; `AbiState`'s three bundled-model cases run on TFLite and LiteRT too, and `AbiProcessor.Int16RingWithAConvertingProcessorOnEveryEngine` includes TFLite. The test suite's 2.x SimpleGain documents (`test/abi/fixtures.h`, `test/support/v2_documents.h`) carry TFLITE- and LITERT-qualified `tensor_shape` rows with the gain at `[1, 1, 1]` for the same reason, and `AbiTranslate.UpgradedSimpleGainEqualsTheV2Fixture` expects them.
- Session-exclusive is two halves now. The scheduling half stays in the scheduler: one chunk of the session in flight at a time and in order under the dispatch gate, with a reset at every new stream, for a model declared `ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL` or with a declared State pair. The ownership half moved from the model to the handler: the exclusive handler's prepared handle owns its executor (the built-in engines' `ExecutorPrepared` takes the loaded model's spare or makes one at prepare, a registered engine builds its own under `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE`), so a stateful model is pooled and loaded once like any other, with `instances` 0, and prepared per handler, where before it was prepared once per handler with `instances = 1` and never shared; exclusivity left the pool key (`engine::Model` no longer carries it, `engine::PrepareRequest` does) and `Core::create_session` no longer bypasses the pool for it. `AbiEngine.EqualConfigsShareOneLoadedModelStatefulOrNot`, `AbiEngine.ASessionExclusiveHandlerHasNoSharedSlotOnAnyEngine` and `Adapter.AnExclusiveSessionRunsOnItsOwnExecutorAndResetsWhenAsked` pin it.
- **Breaking (pre-release ABI 0.x):** `anira_processor_prepare_info` is `anira_prepare_info`, declared in the new header `anira/abi/lifecycle.h` (which `anira/abi/processor.h` and `anira/abi/engine.h` include, so no include changes) and shared by the processor's and the engine's prepare; `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_PREPARE_INFO_INIT` is `ANIRA_PREPARE_INFO_INIT`; the record grows two tail words, `flags` (`ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE`) and `reserved` (72 bytes on LP64, 48 on ILP32). Migration: rename the type in the signature of the processor's prepare function (`const anira_prepare_info* info`) and the initializer where a test fills one by hand; every field a processor read keeps its name and meaning, and a processor that ignores `flags` behaves as before.
- **Breaking (pre-release ABI 0.x):** `anira_engine_prepare_info` is `anira_engine_load_info` and `ANIRA_ENGINE_PREPARE_INFO_INIT` is `ANIRA_ENGINE_LOAD_INFO_INIT`: the record an engine reads to load a model (the variant and the row, the engine-side templates, the binding names, `instances`) is the load slot's now, its layout unchanged (64 bytes on LP64, 44 on ILP32), and its `instances` may be 0, for a model whose handlers are exclusive. Migration: rename the type and the initializer, and treat `instances` 0 as no shared slot: size no shared executor for it, and build what such a handler runs on at prepare (below).
- **Breaking (pre-release ABI 0.x):** the engine's prepare is split into a model level and a handler level. `anira_engine_prepare_fn` is `(const anira_prepare_info* info, void* loaded, void* user_data, void** out_prepared)` now, once per handler and loaded model, and the work the old prepare did, loading the model of the row and sizing its instances, moved to the new slot `load` (`anira_engine_load_fn`, `(const anira_engine_load_info* info, void* user_data, void** out_loaded)`, once per loaded model anira pools) with `unload` (`anira_engine_unload_fn`, `(void* loaded, void* user_data)`) giving it back; `init` (`anira_engine_init_fn`, `(const anira_init_info* info, void* user_data)`, once per engine object) and `anira_processor_init_fn` of the same shape are new on both descriptors, so `anira_engine_desc` has eight slots in the order `process`, `reset`, `prepare`, `unprepare`, `load`, `unload`, `init`, `release` (96 bytes on LP64, 56 on ILP32) and `anira_processor_desc` nine, `init` between `unprepare` and `release` (104 and 60); `anira_phase` gains `ANIRA_PHASE_INIT = 9`, `ANIRA_PHASE_LOAD = 10` and `ANIRA_PHASE_UNLOAD = 11` at its tail. Migration for a custom engine: move the model loading of the old `prepare` into `load` and hand what it loaded back through `out_loaded` (every later `prepare` receives it as `loaded`, every `process` and `reset` in `ctx->loaded`, `unload` gets it back); move what the old unprepare freed into `unload`; keep in the new `prepare` only what the engine holds per handler, or hand back NULL when it holds nothing; an engine of a stateful model (`ANIRA_MODEL_STATEFUL`, a declared State pair) that counted on `instances = 1` and kept its per-stream state behind the old prepared pointer builds that state in `prepare` when `info->flags` carries `ANIRA_PREPARE_EXCLUSIVE`, its own executor included, since its load now sees `instances` 0 and its calls carry `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE` with `instance` 0; a descriptor filled by name from `ANIRA_ENGINE_DESC_INIT` / `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_DESC_INIT` compiles unchanged with the new slots NULL, a positional initializer is rewritten by name. A processor changes nothing unless it wants `init`.
- **Breaking (pre-release ABI 0.x):** `anira_processor_phase` is `anira_phase`, and its terminator `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_PHASE_FORCE32` is `ANIRA_PHASE_FORCE32`. The enum names the phases of both descriptors, the engine call and the engine's `load` and `unload` among them, so like the records the two lifecycles share (`anira_init_info`, `anira_prepare_info`) it names no side; its values already did. The values keep their names and numbers, the value still travels in `anira_processor_ctx.phase` alone (an engine receives none, each of its slots being a callback of its own), and the web build's `enums.ts` exports `anira_phase`. Migration: spell the type `anira_phase` (`anira::ProcessorContext::phase()` and `anira::Processor::phase_bit` take and return it); a `switch` over the values compiles unchanged.
- **Breaking (pre-release ABI 0.x):** `anira_engine_ctx.reserved_ptr0` is `loaded`, what the loaded model's `load` handed back (NULL for an engine without a load slot), at the same offset 48 (`abi/layout-0.txt` and `web/src/abi/layout.ts` rename the row; the record stays 64 bytes), and `anira_engine_ctx.flags` carries its first bit, `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE = 1u` (the call is an exclusive handler's: it runs on what that handler's prepare built, no shared slot was claimed, `instance` is 0). Migration: an engine that read `reserved_ptr0` (nothing should have) reads `loaded`; an engine that assumed `instance` names a shared executor on every call checks the bit first, or reads what it needs from `prepared`, which is the handler's on every call.
- **Breaking (rename, pre-release API):** in `anira/anira.hpp` `anira::ProcessorPrepareInfo` and `anira::EnginePrepareInfo` are `anira::PrepareInfo` (one class for both descriptors, with `flags()` and `exclusive()`) and `anira::EngineLoadInfo`; `anira::InitInfo` is new; `Engine::prepare(const EnginePrepareInfo&)` returning `Engine::Prepared` is `Engine::load(const EngineLoadInfo&)` returning `std::unique_ptr<Engine::Loaded>`, whose `prepare(const PrepareInfo&)` returns `std::unique_ptr<Engine::Prepared>`; `Processor::init(const InitInfo&)` and `Engine::init(const InitInfo&)` are new virtuals with an empty base body. Migration: a `Processor` renames the parameter type of its `prepare`; an `Engine` splits its old `prepare` into a `Loaded` subclass that loads the weights and the shared executors in `load` and a `Prepared` subclass that `Loaded::prepare` returns per handler, keeping `process` and `reset` on the `Prepared`; what the old `Prepared` loaded once moves to the `Loaded`, what it keeps per handler or per stream stays, built under `PrepareInfo::exclusive()`; `EngineContext::loaded()` hands the `Loaded` back to a `Prepared` that kept no pointer to it.
- **Breaking (pre-release ABI 0.x):** a custom engine's id is its own, given at create: `anira_custom_engine_create(engine_id, desc, out, err)` takes it and `anira_pipeline_add_engine(pipeline, engine, err)` takes none. The id names the engine for the object's life, in every pipeline it is added to, every message and every plan report row, so the id and the object can no longer disagree: one engine under two ids, in one pipeline or two, is gone (two objects take its place, each with its own `init` and `release`; the two ids loaded twice as well, since the id is part of the variant's text). The create checks the id (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL id, an id without a `.` or with the prefix `anira.`, before the object exists, so a refused id never calls `release`); the addition refuses an engine whose id the pipeline already has, another object under the id or the engine already there, with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` naming the id, and takes no reference. The id is unique per pipeline, not per process: two plugin instances that each create an engine under one id add them to their own pipelines, and the two never share a loaded model, since the pool keys a registered engine's loaded model on the engine object; the id leaves the record's part of the key (`engine::Model::operator==`), as the object implies it. Migration: pass the id to `anira_custom_engine_create` and drop it from `anira_pipeline_add_engine`; a program that added one engine under two ids creates two engines. The registry's docs (`anira_model_config_add_model_path`, `anira_backend_id`, the head of `anira/abi/engine.h`), the guides (`custom_engines.rst`, `usage.rst`, `migration.rst`, `benchmarking.rst`, `troubleshooting.rst`) and the gates (`header_c.c`, `header_cxx17.cpp`, `test_rt_contract.c`) follow. Tests: `AbiEngine.CreateRefusals` (the id's refusals, and the id copied into the engine), `AbiEngine.AddRefusals`, `AbiEngine.ADuplicateIdIsInvalidState` (another object under the id, and the engine already there), `AbiEngine.OneEngineInTwoPipelinesSharesOneLoadedModel`.
- **Breaking (pre-release API):** in `anira/anira.hpp` an `anira::Engine` is constructed with its id (`explicit Engine(std::string id)`, read back by `id()`; the default constructor is gone), and `Pipeline::add_engine(impl)` takes the object alone. The id is checked when the object's C engine is created, at its first addition: an id that is no reverse-URI name, or anira's own, throws `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` there and creates nothing; an addition refused because the pipeline already has an engine with the id throws `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE`, and a C engine the call created goes again, answered by `release()`. Migration: a subclass passes its id to the base constructor (`MyEngine() : anira::Engine("com.example.my") {}`) and the additions drop their first argument. Tests: `AbiCxx.AddEngineRefusesANullEngineABadIdAndAnUnknownFlag` (the bad id before the C engine exists, another object under a taken id after it), `AbiCxx.AnEngineAddedToTwoPipelinesIsOneCEngine`; gate `anira_header_cxx20` pins the member-pointer types and `id()`.
- **JSON (pre-release):** the pair of an engine and a provider is spelled as two keys everywhere in JSON, `"engine"` and `"provider"`, as the C records spell it as two fields (`anira_backend_id`, `anira_provider_option_set`, `anira_plan_info`): a model entry pins itself with `"provider": "xnnpack"` beside `"engine": "executorch"` (a neutral entry has no `"provider"` key; `"cpu"` pins to the CPU path, and `"default"` and `"none"` are refused), and a `provider_options` set names its backend as `{"engine": "onnxruntime", "provider": "cuda", "options": {...}}`.
