The compound `engine:provider` string is gone from both file formats (a `:` in an engine word is refused naming the key) and survives as a label in messages and log lines (`backend_label` of `src/capi/words.h`). A set's `"provider"` is required and never `"cpu"`, the CPU path, which takes no options. Migration: move the suffix into the key. Tests: `AbiJsonModel.AProviderKeyPinsTheEntry`, `AbiExtRegistry.ProviderOptionsRoundTripInCAndJson`.
- **Internal:** `src/capi/translate.{h,cpp}` is split by what it does, since the plan resolution this pre-release put there translates nothing and outlives the 2.x runtime. `src/capi/validate.{h,cpp}` (the history follows it) holds the section-2 validator (`validate`, the rules, `Derived`), the plan table (`PlanKey`, `matching_plans`, the default-set rule), what the pipeline's processor and engines mean to it (`ProcessorFacts`, `EngineFacts`) and the per-slot facts it resolves: `ring_dtypes_of` and `host_domains_of` (formerly `make_ring_dtypes` and `make_host_domains`), `state_links`, and the new `anchor_of`, which the 2.x HostConfig of the host's own geometry reads. The translators into the 2.x configuration objects (`make_inference_config`, `make_host_config`, `make_core_config`, `make_context_config`) moved beside the exported bridge, into `src/capi/v3_to_v2.cpp` with the internal header `src/capi/v3_to_v2.h`, and go with the 2.x runtime at the cut-over. No behavior changes; `test_Translate` keeps its name, since it exercises the bridge.
- Docs: the guides describe the lifecycle the code has now, the same words on both sides. `docs/sphinx/custom_engines.rst` walks the engine's levels from the outermost in (`init` with `anira_init_info` once per engine object, `load` with `anira_engine_load_info` once per pooled model and `unload`, `prepare` with the shared `anira_prepare_info` and the loaded pointer once per handler and `unprepare`, `reset` for exclusive handlers, `process` on a claimed slot or under `ANIRA_ENGINE_CALL_EXCLUSIVE`, `release`), its gain engine in C filling the eight slots by name and in C++ returning a `Loaded` from `load` whose `prepare` returns a `Prepared`; `docs/sphinx/custom_preprocessing.rst` describes the processor's lifecycle with its `init` and the shared record, its examples taking `anira_prepare_info` / `PrepareInfo`; `docs/sphinx/usage.rst` names the records of `anira/abi/lifecycle.h`, the processor's `init` where it lists the processor slots and the engine's three levels where it describes the registration; `docs/sphinx/migration.rst` maps `BackendBase` onto `Engine::load` / `Loaded::prepare` / `Prepared::process` and carries the renames of this pre-release; `docs/sphinx/contributing.rst` states the naming scheme of the levels (`init` / `release`, `load` / `unload`, `prepare` / `unprepare`) and the callback shape of every slot of both descriptors as the convention every later descriptor keeps. The API reference carries the pages of `anira::PrepareInfo`, `anira::InitInfo` and `anira::EngineLoadInfo` and of the three C records in place of the two prepare records' pages.
- **ABI (unstable):** `anira_custom_engine_create` admits the id `anira.v2.custom`, the one id with the prefix `anira.` a host may create an engine under (the id of a 2.x custom backend: a 2.x model file's `"CUSTOM"` row names it, and `anira/compat/v2.hpp` creates its custom backend under it); every other `anira.`-prefixed id is still `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`. On the C path a custom row needs an engine on the pipeline like any other id, `anira.v2.custom` included: without one it is `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` at `anira_handler_create` ("custom engine 'anira.v2.custom' is not added to this pipeline"), so the engine-free pass-through the C handler ran for that row (the 2.x `BackendBase::process` of a legacy loaded model per session, never pooled, its slots bound by position) is gone, and so is its default-provider rule in the provider check: the engine added under the id serves what its descriptor lists. The bridge, which has no pipeline, keeps serving the id with the 2.x pass-through until the cut-over. The id is one `inline constexpr` in `src/capi/validate.h` (`k_v2_custom_engine`), shared by the JSON upgrade, the validator and the engine's create. Tests: the rigs that attached a 2.x `BackendBase` twin to a C handler's custom plan (`attach_processor`, gone) add engines to their pipelines: `test/abi/handler_support.h` has the pass-through engine (`passthrough_desc`, `make_passthrough`, `add_passthrough`: one object per pipeline, since the object is the pool's identity), which every `anira_test::Handler` adds under `anira.v2.custom` unless a rig hands it a `GateEngine` (a C engine object over a virtual body: the gate, a one-shot or a standing failure with the record the legacy adapter logged for a throw, the pass-through), and `SleepingParamFillEngine`; `test_State`'s `AccumulatorGate`, `test_Processor`'s `WindowSumEngine` and `copy_oracle.h`'s `ParamRampEngine` are gate engines (`oracle::settle` takes either gate; the 2.x gates stay for the 2.x rigs), `test/contracts/unload/module.cpp` adds a static pass-through descriptor, the custom plan's slots report `ANIRA_BINDING_ENGINE` where they reported `ANIRA_BINDING_POSITION`, `AbiEngine.CreateRefusals` creates `anira.v2.custom` and `AbiPrepare.StructuralRulesAtCreate` refuses its row without an engine. Both copy-oracle goldens are unchanged.
- Tests: the suite is partitioned for the 3.x cut-over, written as a comment above the test binaries in `test/CMakeLists.txt` (decision 22 of the M2 plan: every test file targets one API and moves once: through `anira/compat/v2.hpp`, rewritten on `anira/anira.hpp`, white-box over the private headers, retired with its subject, or API-free). `test_InferenceConfig.cpp` and `test_CoreConfig.cpp` compile into `test_utils` with the other white-box suites (the files keep their paths); the three struct-pool cases of `test_OneSidedStreaming.cpp` that read the 2.x session through `Core::get_sessions()` (`GeneratorPushDataNeverSubmits`, `AnalyserPushOnlyNeverStalls`, `TwoSidedPushWithoutPopIsGatedNotOverwritten`) move verbatim into `test/scheduler/test_OneSidedStreamingInternals.cpp` (suite `OneSidedStreamingInternals`, in `test_scheduler`) with the helpers they need; `test/support/v2_objects.h` (`anira_test::inference_config_of`) builds the 2.x `InferenceConfig` of a bundled file through the private translator `anira::capi::make_inference_config` instead of the exported bridge, and `test_InferenceThread`, `test_ProcessorPooling` and `AbiInferenceThread.ServesASessionWithoutAPool` take it (the two thread cases run the gain model's custom row on the 2.x pass-through instead of the HybridNN processors: they check that a user-driven thread runs one block), so `test/support/extras_fixtures.h` serves only `test_InferenceHandler`, `test_JsonConfigLoader` and `test_ExtrasFixtures`, which the cut-over rewrites or retires. `docs/sphinx/contributing.rst` names the partition.
- Naming: the guides, the README, the configure-time messages and the tests say engine where they said backend
 for an inference engine (backend is the pair, an engine on a provider, everywhere the 3.x vocabulary applies; ExecuTorch's own word backend is quoted as its delegate); the test suites `ExecuTorchProcessor` / `LibTorchProcessor` are `AdapterExecuTorch` / `AdapterLibTorch`, `BackendSwitch` is `PlanSwitch`, `BackendLinkage` is `EngineLinkage`.
- **Breaking:** the engine word reaches the file layout, the installed tree and the log groups. The engine room moved from `src/backends` to `src/engines` and its namespace from `anira::backend` to `anira::engine` (internal; the 2.x headers `anira/scheduler/Core.h`, `SessionElement.h` and `InferenceManager.h` name the new namespace); `cmake/backends.cmake` and `cmake/backends/<engine>.cmake` are `cmake/engines.cmake` and `cmake/engines/<engine>.cmake`; `test/backends` is `test/engines`, and its test binary `test_engines` (was `test_backends`; `CMakePresets.json` and the mobile runner's lists follow); `docs/sphinx/custom_backends.rst` is `custom_engines.rst`. The installed package: the engines' headers go to `<includedir>/anira-engines/<engine>/` (was `anira-backends/`), the package files that define the `anira::<engine>` targets are `aniraEngineHelpers.cmake` and `aniraEngineTargets.cmake` (were `aniraBackendHelpers.cmake` and `aniraBackendTargets.cmake`; `aniraConfig.cmake` includes both, so a `find_package(anira)` consumer changes nothing), and the CPack component of the engine libraries is `deps-engines` (was `deps-backends`). An engine's log records carry the group `anira.engine.<engine>` (was `anira.backend.<engine>`; the constants `anira::log_group::k_engine_libtorch`, `k_engine_onnx`, `k_engine_tflite`, `k_engine_litert` and `k_engine_executorch`, were `k_backend_*`). Migration: a build that adds the installed engine include directory by hand, a packaging script that selects the `deps-backends` component, or a log filter on `anira.backend.` spells the new name. Not renamed: the repository anira-project/backends, its release archives and the `ANIRA_BACKENDS_*` variables.
- **Breaking (ABI, unstable):** the engine word on the ABI and C++ names. `anira_enabled_backends` is `anira_enabled_engines` (it lists engines, one row per engine on the CPU path; C++ `anira::enabled_backends()` is `anira::enabled_engines()`). `anira::Engine::available(const InitInfo&)` is `anira::Engine::query(const InitInfo&)`, the word of the descriptor's `query` slot it fills. Migration: rename the calls and the override; nothing else changes.
- **Breaking (ABI, unstable):** a custom engine and a custom provider have enum values of their own, and one pair rule names both axes. `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` join `anira_engine` and `anira_provider` at `0x7FFFFFFE`, the value just below the `_FORCE32` sentinel, so a later built-in engine or provider is appended below them and never renumbers CUSTOM. Wherever an engine or a provider is named (a model entry and its pin, the default engine and provider, `anira_backend_id` candidates and capability rows, `anira_plan_info`, `anira_edge_info`, `anira_provider_option_set`, `anira_engine_load_info`, `anira_processor_ctx`) the id is non-NULL if and only if the value is CUSTOM: a custom engine is `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` with its reverse-URI id (was `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` beside it), a provider the enum does not name is `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` with its name (was `ANIRA_PROVIDER_DEFAULT` beside it), a built-in value takes a NULL id, `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` names no engine and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` no provider, and every setter and the candidate list of `anira_pipeline_add_inference` refuse any other combination with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`; the getters and records report CUSTOM (`anira_model_config_model_engine`, `_default_engine`, `_model_provider`, `_default_provider`, the plan report, the capability rows, the processor context), and `anira_enabled_engines` never lists it. The three setters of alpha.1 take the pair: `anira_model_config_add_model_path(config, engine, engine_id, path, out_index, err)`, `anira_model_config_add_model_bytes(config, engine, engine_id, bytes, size, ownership, release, ctx, out_index, err)` and `anira_model_config_set_default_engine(config, engine, engine_id, err)` (it gains the `anira_error*` of its siblings), and the three custom twins `anira_model_config_add_model_path_custom`, `_add_model_bytes_custom` and `_set_default_engine_custom` are gone. No function of the ABI carries `_id` in its name: every getter reads the whole pair through a status and two out-parameters (`INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a NULL handle, a NULL value out-parameter or an index out of range; the id out-parameter may be NULL; written on `ANIRA_OK` only): `anira_model_config_model_engine(config, model_index, &engine, &engine_id)` (was a value getter returning the engine, beside `anira_model_config_model_engine_id`, both of alpha.1), `anira_model_config_default_engine(config, &engine, &engine_id)`, `anira_model_config_model_provider(config, model_index, &provider, &provider_id)`, `anira_model_config_default_provider(config, &provider, &provider_id)`; `_model_engine_id`, `_default_engine_id`, `_model_provider_id` and `_default_provider_id` are gone, and the processor's pair accessors are `anira_processor_engine(ctx, &engine, &engine_id)` and `anira_processor_provider(ctx, &provider, &provider_id)` (were `anira_processor_engine_id` / `anira_processor_provider_id`, the id alone). 227 promised names. A candidate `{ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE, NULL}` no longer stands for every custom entry: name each custom engine by its id (the default candidate set lists the custom engines its entries name); the 2.x bridge's candidate `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` keeps the 2.x CUSTOM rows (`anira.v2.custom`). C++: the enum and string overloads of `add_model_path`, `add_model_bytes`, `default_engine`, `model_provider` and `default_provider` keep their spelling and map onto the pair (`model_provider(i, provider, id)` and `default_provider(provider, id)` are `model_provider(i, id)` and `default_provider(id)` for a custom provider); the getters return `anira::EngineRef{kind, id}` / `anira::ProviderRef{kind, id}` (`model_engine(i)`, `default_engine()`, `model_provider(i)`, `default_provider()`, `EngineLoadInfo::model_engine(i)` / `provider()`, `ProcessorContext::engine()` / `provider()`), `kind` `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM` / `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CUSTOM` for a custom one, and the `*_id()` getters are gone. Migration: `anira_model_config_add_model_path_custom(cfg, id, path, &i, &err)` is `anira_model_config_add_model_path(cfg, ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM, id, path, &i, &err)` (the bytes and default-engine twins likewise); a built-in engine's call adds a `NULL` id; a candidate or a pin that spelled a custom engine as NONE beside an id, or a custom provider as DEFAULT beside a name, spells CUSTOM; a reader that tested `engine == ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` for a custom engine tests `ANIRA_ENGINE_CUSTOM`; `anira_model_config_model_engine(cfg, i)` is `anira_model_config_model_engine(cfg, i, &engine, &id)` (the `_id` getters fold into their value getter the same way), and in C++ `model_engine(i)` / `model_engine_id(i)` read `model_engine(i).kind` / `.id`.
- **Breaking (ABI, unstable):** the provider axis gets the engine axis's NONE, and the CPU path is a provider of its own. `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE = 0` is no provider, the zero value (`ANIRA_BACKEND_ID_INIT`, `ANIRA_PLAN_INFO_INIT`, `ANIRA_EDGE_INFO_INIT`, `ANIRA_PROVIDER_OPTION_SET_INIT` and a zeroed record hold it): a neutral model entry (`anira_model_config_set_model_provider(cfg, i, ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE, NULL)` unpins, the getter answers NONE) and no default provider (`anira_model_config_set_default_provider(cfg, ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE, NULL)` clears, the getter answers NONE); a candidate or a provider option set naming it is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, as `ANIRA_ENGINE_NONE` is refused as an entry's engine. `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU = 1` (JSON `"cpu"`) is the engine's plain CPU path, no delegate or execution provider: ONNX Runtime's `CPUExecutionProvider`, LibTorch, TFLite, ExecuTorch without a delegate, LiteRT's CPU accelerator; XNNPACK stays a provider of its own. Every built-in engine serves it as its first capability row, and `anira_enabled_engines`, the capability rows and edges, the plans (`anira_plan_info`), `anira_engine_load_info` and the processor context report CPU where they reported the default provider; the default candidate set is every engine of the build on CPU, each custom engine an entry names, and every pin, and under it every entry is one plan: a pinned entry on its pin, a neutral one on its engine's home provider (CPU for a built-in engine and for a custom engine that serves it, else the custom engine's first listed provider); a plan on a provider its engine cannot use here (a built-in engine's provider the capabilities lack, a custom engine's declared provider its query cleared) is dropped under the default set, as an entry for an absent engine is skipped, a variant left without a plan is `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`, and a named candidate on such a provider is still refused. `ANIRA_PROVIDER_DEFAULT` is removed, not aliased. A custom engine serves exactly what its descriptor lists: no list serves CPU alone, a list serves CPU only when it lists `"cpu"`, and the query's bitmask covers the listed providers alone (no provider is served without a bit beside a list any more). `"default"` and `"none"` are no provider's name: a model file's `"provider"` and `"default_provider"`, a provider option set's `"provider"` and a descriptor's providers list refuse them; the CPU path takes no provider options, as the default provider took none. Migration: a candidate or a pin that said `ANIRA_PROVIDER_DEFAULT` for the CPU path says `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU` (JSON `"cpu"`); an unpin or "no default provider" says `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` (JSON: leave the key out); a reader that tested `provider == ANIRA_PROVIDER_DEFAULT` tests `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE` for a neutral entry or no default provider and `ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU` for a plan's, a row's or a load's CPU path; a record left at its `_INIT` value or zeroed now names no provider, so a candidate built that way sets `provider = ANIRA_PROVIDER_CPU`; a custom engine that relied on the default provider being served lists `"cpu"` beside its other providers, or lists nothing. C++: `anira::ProviderRef{}` and `ProviderOptions::Set{}` start at `ANIRA_PROVIDER_NONE`. Both enums are renumbered into one order, which every list follows (the word tables, the JSON word lists of the docs, `anira_enabled_engines` and the capabilities, the default candidate set): `anira_engine` is NONE 0, ONNXRUNTIME 1, EXECUTORCH 2, LITERT 3, LIBTORCH 4, TFLITE 5 (were LIBTORCH 2, TFLITE 3, LITERT 4, EXECUTORCH 5), CUSTOM `0x7FFFFFFE`; `anira_provider` is NONE 0, CPU 1, CUDA 2, WEBGPU 3, VULKAN 4, DIRECTML 5, COREML 6, XNNPACK 7 (were CUDA 1, WEBGPU 2, DIRECTML 3, COREML 4, XNNPACK 5, VULKAN 6), CUSTOM `0x7FFFFFFE`; a value after the last built-in one and below CUSTOM is reserved for a later built-in engine or provider. The 2.x `anira::InferenceBackend` keeps its values (the bridge maps it by a switch, never by value). Migration: rebuild against the new header; code that stored an engine or a provider as a number (a file, a wire format) maps it by the enumerator's name. Tests: `AbiPrepare.StructuralRulesAtCreate` (a NONE candidate), `AbiEngine.UnderTheDefaultSetANeutralEntryRunsOnItsEnginesHomeProvider` (the three home providers, the dropped plan, the empty table, the named candidate), `AbiTranslate.EnabledEnginesMatchTheBuild` (the engine order), `AbiEngine.TheProvidersListIsCopiedAndAnswersServes` (a list serves what it lists, the reserved words), `AProviderTheEngineDoesNotListIsNotSupportedAtCreate` (CPU refused beside a list without it), `ThePipelinesCapabilitiesListTheCustomEnginesRows`, `AbiJsonModel.AProviderKeyPinsTheEntry` (`"cpu"`, the reserved words), `AbiExtRegistry.ProviderOptionsRoundTripInCAndJson` and `ProviderOptionSetsAreReadAtTheCallersStrideAndCheckedAtSet` (NONE and CPU refused).
- Tests: `test_InferenceHandlerApi.cpp` and `test_OneSidedStreaming.cpp` are 2.x hosts of `anira/compat/v2.hpp` (the partition's group (a)): they include the shim alone, spell `using namespace anira::v2` and `ContextConfig(2)`, and run over the C handler. The one-sided mocks are `anira::Engine` objects under `anira.v2.custom` through one `KernelEngine<Kernel>` template (ParamFill, MeanPlusParam, a counting copy); the two `#ifndef ANIRA_WITH_RTSAN` regions are gone, so the non-realtime and blocking generator cases run under RTSan; an out-of-range custom-latency index expects `anira::Error` (`ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`); the custom-processor constructor takes a `PassthroughEngine`; `GeneratorPushDataNeverSubmits` and `AnalyserPushOnlyNeverStalls` return to suite `OneSidedStreamingStandalone` with what the handler shows (the calls, the ring fill, one hop of demand submitting once, the entry count through `anira_handler_num_entries(handler.native())`), their struct-pool halves staying in `OneSidedStreamingInternals`. `test_Compat` gains the extras' CNN and HybridNN processors copied verbatim against `anira::v2` (bit-equal to the 2.x class on every oracle engine; HybridNN reads the layout-applying shape getter), the single form that carries one slot per side, and the generator's clamped Static count.
- **Docs:** `migration.rst` is the compat page: "Running 2.x code on the 3.x runtime" takes the place of the bridge section, with the shim member by member, what is not source-compatible (a `BackendBase`, the owning `BufferF`, the per-sample ring reads, code that stored or indexed the 2.x `InferenceBackend` integer, `std::invalid_argument` catches, ...), what behaves differently, the classes one by one and when the 2.x classes go; the bridge keeps a subsection until the cut-over. `usage.rst`, `custom_preprocessing.rst` and `custom_engines.rst` point 2.x code at the header, `anira.hpp`'s preamble names `anira::v2::JsonConfigLoader`, the `Engine(std::string id)` and `Pipeline::add_engine` docs state the `anira.v2.custom` exception to the `anira.` prefix rule, and four test comments that still named the default provider say the CPU path (`v2_objects.h`, `handler_support.h`, `test_Handles.cpp`, `test_AdapterExecuTorch.cpp`). `docs/CMakeLists.txt` removes the pages breathe-apidoc would write for the nested classes of the shim (`LegacyProcessor::Prepared`, `PassthroughEngine`'s private `Loaded` and `Prepared`, `InferenceConfig::Defaults`) and of `anira::SpecView` (`Axis`, `Window`, `TimeRatio`), as for the nested classes before them.
- **Breaking (ABI, unstable):** the Hard geometry is `double`: `anira_contract_create_hard(double block_min, double block_max, double rate, ...)` and `anira_contract_hard_set_geometry(contract, double block_min, double block_max, double rate)` (both since 0.1, signature changed), the read-back `anira_contract_hard_geometry` writes `double*` outs, and `anira::Hard::block_min` / `block_max` and `ContractHandle::hard_geometry` take doubles. Migration: a C caller passing integers keeps compiling (the implicit conversion is exact for every `uint32_t`), a caller of the read-back declares its two outs `double`; C++ code with integer literals or `uint32_t` assignments is unchanged. `block_min = 0` with a non-zero `block_max`, legal before, is refused: write `block_min = block_max` for a fixed block or a smaller positive one.
- **Compat (C++, header-only):** `anira::v2::InferenceHandler::prepare` accepts a fractional `HostConfig::m_buffer_size` as 2.x did and passes it through to the Hard geometry (`block_min` is half the block when smaller blocks come and the block is one sample or less); a block that is not finite and above 0 is still `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG`. `AbiCompat.AFractionalHostConfigIsRefused` is now `ANonPositiveHostConfigIsRefused`.
- **Breaking (ABI, unstable):** a Hard call that carries more than the declared block on a stream (`ceil` of the block: `block_max` scaled from the anchor to the stream's hop, `ceil(b)` of a fractional block `b`) is refused with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` where it was silently truncated to the tail (a push larger than the send ring kept its last samples) or ran past the rings the latency was sized for. Every Hard entry that moves a Streamed slot checks it, on the push side and the pop side: `anira_handler_process`, `push_data`, `pop_data`, their `_multi` forms and the four `_wait` twins. The check is a compare against a figure computed at prepare (`StreamPort::m_max_samples`, from the same rational the latency calculation takes), runs after the descriptor checks and before anything moves (no ring is touched, nothing is submitted, the delivered counts are 0), is recorded last-wins in `anira_handler_rt_error` and logged once per kind as a contract violation; Static and State slots are unaffected. The 2.x shim (`anira/compat/v2.hpp`) inherits it: a 2.x host that passes more than its `HostConfig` buffer size (`ceil`) is refused through the shim, where 2.x truncated; the library's own 2.x `anira::InferenceHandler` path is untouched and stays tolerant. Migration: declare the largest block you pass as `block_max` (`contract.hard_geometry(block_min, block_max, rate)`; on the shim, `HostConfig(largest_block, rate, true)`). Documented in `anira/abi/handler.h` (the per-call rule, now an enforced bound, and every Hard entry), `usage.rst` section 1.3 (*Geometry*, *Fractional blocks*) and 3.2 (*The Hard entries*), `latency.rst` and the compat page of `migration.rst`. Tests: `AbiHandlerTensor.AHostBlockAboveTheDeclaredBlockIsRefused` (the bound passes, one above and four times the bound are refused on every entry under the geometry 1..8 on a hop of 8 and under 0.25 on a hop of 1, the rings unchanged, one record, the stream going on after it) and `AbiCompat.ABlockAboveTheBufferSizeIsRefused` (the shim refuses 513 samples on a buffer size of 512 where the 2.x class takes them); `AbiPrepare.LatencyRulesAreConfigAtPrepare` pops its 4096 primed zeros in blocks of the declared 512, `OneSidedStreamingStandalone.GeneratorPushDataNeverSubmits` declares the hop it pops in one call as its block, and `OneSidedStreamingTest.GeneratorResetReanchors` pulls block by block until one pull submits (it passed one hop on a block of 512 and, refused, tested nothing). The C handler copy oracle keeps every call within its block now; the scripts that passed more were rewritten, their golden transcripts recorded again once, and `AbiHandlerCopyOracle.MatchesTheV2CopyPath` runs every script on the 2.x copy path as the referee.
- **WebAssembly:** an idle inference Worker parks instead of spinning a core. `InferenceThread::notify_work()`, the wake word, runs after every enqueue into the inference queue (`Core::enqueue_inference_or_drop`, a stateful session's next dispatch, the drain's requeue) and in `request_stop()` / `stop()`; on WebAssembly it bumps a word in shared memory and wakes every Worker parked on it (`memory.atomic.notify`), and `run_loop()`'s last backoff phase parks there with `memory.atomic.wait32` for at most 5 ms instead of `yield` + `sleep_for`, which spin on the clock in Emscripten's libc. A missed notify costs a parked Worker at most those 5 ms, never a hang; the worklet and the main thread never wait. Natively nothing changes: `notify_work()` is empty, the polling loop still sleeps and the blocking one still wakes on the queue's semaphore.
- **Web (`@anira-project/anira` 3.0.0-alpha.2):** the TypeScript package runs over the C ABI and `anira/anira_em.h`. `runtime/`: `createAniraWasm` (from `factory.ts`) installs the hook object `Module.anira` of each instance over a per-instance `HookRegistry` keyed by `js_key` and checks `_anira_em_hooks_version()` against it (and, given `abiVersion`, `_anira_check_abi`); `Heap` (views over the shared memory, the `>>> 0` rule, `BigInt64` views, UTF-8 in and out); `AniraError` / `callWithError` over an `anira_error` in heap memory read through `layout.ts`; `allocInstanceStack` / `enterInstance` (`_anira_em_alloc_thread_stack`, then `_anira_em_init_instance` as the first C call of every Worker and worklet instance); `JSProcessor`, `JSEngine`, `readStageContext`, `readLogRecord`. `core/InferenceThread` creates the thread on main and stops it by polling `has_exited`. The Worker protocol carries `js_key` and engine id (`registerEngine` / `registerStage`, an engine's `init()` awaited first) and runs `_anira_inference_thread_run_loop` on `start`; Worker and worklet log records are forwarded to main. `AniraAudioWorkletBase` makes one `_anira_handler_process` per render quantum over planar host tensors (`_anira_tensor_init_host_planar`) built once and rebuilt only when the quantum or a channel count changes, reports a changed quantum (`'quantumChanged'`, the host re-prepares with `block_min = block_max =` the quantum), waits only on opt-in (`waitMs`, `_anira_handler_process_wait`) and runs a processor's pre/post/reset from its `stageClasses`. `JSBackend` is the minimal engine base (the call's tensors through `layout.ts`, `_anira_tensor_data_f32` / `_num_elements`); `ONNXRuntimeWebBackend` is registered as `org.anira.onnxruntime-web` and reads its model entry with `_anira_model_config_model_path` / `_model_bytes` (its warm-up is the contract's now). `AniraWeb` is the runtime half: `create`, `registerJSEngine`, `addJSStage`, `spinUpInferenceWorker(contextPtr)`, `configureAudioWorklet(ctx, handlerPtr, options)`, `setLogHook`; `helpers.ts` has `getAniraVersion` (`_anira_version_string`), `drainAniraLog` and `startLogPump`. The four vite entries and the package's subpath exports are unchanged.
- **Build:** AniraWeb links `anira::anira` alone: no wrapper archive, no `--whole-archive`, no `-sEXPORT_KEEPALIVE`; the export list pulls every archive member it names. `build_web` compares the module's `.d.ts` with `abi/symbols-0.txt`, `abi/symbols-draft.txt` and `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt` (plus `_malloc`, `_free`) in both directions: a missing name and an extra export both fail. `codecov.yml` ignores `src/platform` in place of the wrappers, and `clang_tidy.yml`'s comment names only `src/platform/`.
- **Release:** `on_tag.yml`'s `publish_npm` checks before it publishes: `gen.py --check` on the committed C ABI files and, after its wasm build, the same `.d.ts` export check as `build_web` (exactly the names of `abi/symbols-0.txt`, `abi/symbols-draft.txt` and `abi/symbols-emscripten.txt`, plus `_malloc`, `_free`). Its `needs:` gains `build_web` (now also a reusable workflow, `workflow_call`), so the node ABI gates and the npm build of the tag pass before anything is published; a hyphenated version such as `3.0.0-alpha.2` goes to the `next` dist-tag.
- **Docs:** the Anira Web guides describe the 3.x package only. `docs/sphinx/web-api/`: `installation_building` (the exports, the `wasm-abi` preset and its gates), `architecture` (the module instances on one memory, the hook object and the trampolines, the inference Workers with the wake word and the `has_exited` stop protocol, the worklet's quantum-sized Hard contract), `basic_usage` (a complete setup on the raw exports: `AniraWeb.create`, a context with no pool threads, a model config naming `org.anira.onnxruntime-web`, `registerJSEngine`, a Hard contract of block 128, handler create and prepare, an inference Worker, the worklet, the stop and the teardown; the built-in ONNX Runtime path), `custom_pre_post_processing` (`addJSStage`, the hook bits and where each runs, the stated `stageFlags`), `custom_inference_backends` (`JSBackend`, `registerJSEngine`, a worker file), `custom_audio_worklets` (the tensor process, `stageClasses`, the quantum change). The reference pages of the deleted classes are gone; `AniraWeb`, `AniraAudioWorkletBase` and `ONNXRuntimeWebBackend` are rewritten, `JSBackend`, `InferenceThread`, `Heap`, `HookRegistry` and `AniraError` are new, and the function list names the new exports. `web/README.md` and the root README's web section show the 3.x setup (installed from the `next` dist-tag), and `migration.rst` gains the web section, from the 2.x TypeScript classes to the raw calls.
- **Generator:** a 64-bit sentinel define of `web/src/abi/enums.ts` (`ANIRA_DYNAMIC`, `ANIRA_UNBOUNDED`) is typed `bigint` instead of its literal type, which the documentation's TypeDoc pass (sphinx-js) cannot serialize now that the package root re-exports the enums.
- **Breaking (ABI, unstable):** a flexible window is pinned from one anchor hop for the whole model, where each flexible window was pinned on its own from `block_max`. The anchor hop is the anchor elements one inference consumes; the anchor, every Streamed tensor with a declared time ratio and every flexible window follow it (a tensor's hop is the anchor hop times its ratio, 1/1 for a flexible window without one), and a fixed window without a declared ratio keeps its own hop, its ratio derived from it, as before. anira takes the largest anchor hop that leaves every such tensor a whole hop within its window at or below the host block (`block_max` as the rational `LatencyCalculator::rationalize` recovers, rounded down), else the smallest above it, and without a geometry the smallest: a time ratio `num/den` makes the anchor hop a multiple of `den` (reduced), each window bounds it. The rule runs in `pin_anchor_hop` (`src/capi/validate.cpp`), for `anira_handler_prepare` and the 2.x bridge alike; it takes over the separate check of declared ratios against fixed hops. Every configuration accepted before keeps its windows, except a flexible window without a declared ratio whose anchor hop is not `block_max` (a fixed anchor of another hop, an anchor window clamped by its range, no geometry): it follows the anchor hop now, where it took the block, or its own `window_min` without a geometry (a fixed anchor of 2048 and a flexible output under a block of 512 ran the output at 512 per inference, a quarter-rate stream; it runs 2048). Accepted now: a block that scaled to a fractional hop runs one inference every few calls (an output at `time_ratio(1, 2048)` on a fixed anchor of 2048 under a block of 1024; a flexible window under a fractional block, refused before with "gives a fractional hop"), and a flexible window with a declared ratio beside a fixed anchor whose hop is not the block (refused before as "does not match its hop"). Still refused, with `ANIRA_ERROR_CONFIG` naming the tensor, the hops it needs and the hops the tensors before it allow: no anchor hop fits every window and ratio (`tensor 'out': time ratio 1/2 and window [1, unbounded] need an anchor hop that is a multiple of 2 in [2, unbounded], but tensor 'in' fixes the anchor hop at 3`), a window no whole hop fits (`time ratio 2/1 and window 3 admit no whole anchor hop`), and a time ratio of 0 (`time ratio 0/5: a Streamed tensor advances with the anchor`, refused before as a mismatch). Migration: a flexible window that must advance at another rate than the anchor declares its time ratio. Documented in `anira/abi/config.h` (`anira_tensor_spec_set_time_ratio`), `usage.rst` sections 1.1 and 1.3 (*Flexible windows*, *Fractional blocks*) and the bridge's *Windows* note in `migration.rst`. Tests: `AbiTranslate.TheAnchorHopIsTheLargestStepAtOrBelowTheBlock` (3000 rounds down to 2048, 512 and 0.25 up to the step, `window_max` caps below the block), `AFixedAnchorSetsTheHopOfEveryFlexibleWindow`, `NoAnchorHopFitsEveryTensor`, `ATimeRatioScalesTheAnchorHopIntoTheFlexibleWindow` (was `ATimeRatioScalesTheFlexibleHopAndMustDivide`, whose block 1024 was refused), `AFractionalBlockPinsTheFlexibleWindowFromTheAnchorHop` (was `AFractionalBlockMaxGivesAFractionalFlexibleHop`, refused; a quarter of a hop per block), `AFlexibleWindowIsPinnedFromTheContractGeometry` (its output without a geometry follows the anchor hop, 192, where it took 1) and `AbiHandlerTensor.AFlexibleWindowUnderAFractionalBlockRunsAtTheAnchorHop` (a flexible pass-through under the block 0.25 prepares, reports the fixed window's latency and moves the stream whole).
- Docs: the v3 roadmap (section 11 of `docs/anira-v3-architecture.md`, with the matching notes in `docs/anira-v3-c-abi-decision-record.md`) is amended to Fares' decision of 2026-10-29. M2 lists what it delivered and what still ends it: PR 9 (the web hooks), PR 11 (the lean web runtime, brought forward from M5: Wasm Workers, Wasm Audio Worklets, generated TypeScript bindings, one configuration for native and web), PR 10, 10b, 12 and 13, with the exit criterion and `alpha.2` unchanged. A new milestone M2b, after `alpha.2` and exiting with `alpha.3`, completes the Hard contract and adds model variants: PR 4 (MEASURED / UNTIL_STABLE budgets, moved out of M2, so PR 12 no longer depends on it), PR 4b (inline execution), PR 7b (State initial values) and the variant axis (one plan per variant and candidate, per-plan budgets with the worst case reported, `thl::RCU` natively and the atomic on Wasm, a stateful switch clearing state at the next boundary). M3 Async now exits with `alpha.4`; M4 gains the emsdk 6.x move at its end (the prebuilt ONNX Runtime WASM archive must be rebuilt with it, anira-project/backends#24); M5 is folded into M2b and M6, which opens with typedoc, the Sphinx C-API page, the JSON benchmark sweep, `[[deprecated]]` on `compat/v2.hpp` and the abidiff baseline at `beta.2` before the freeze. `docs/sphinx/migration.rst` states the `[[deprecated]]` timing to match. A pre-existing corruption of the architecture document (four NUL-delimited `CONTEXTRY` placeholders left by the machine-to-context rename) reads "machinery" again.

### Deprecated

- The 2.x classes that stay installed: `anira::PrePostProcessor`, `anira::BackendBase`, `anira::InferenceConfig`, `anira::CoreConfig`, `anira::HostConfig` and `anira::JsonConfigLoader` (the 2.x `anira::InferenceHandler` is removed). The 3.x handler is `anira/abi/handler.h` with the C++ face of `anira/anira.hpp`; 2.x code compiles against `anira/compat/v2.hpp`. A later pre-release makes the remaining 2.x headers private.
- Every entity of `namespace anira::v2` (`anira/compat/v2.hpp`), the 2.x source-compatible face: removed one minor release after 3.0.0; no `[[deprecated]]` attribute through the alpha releases, one on every entity before 3.0.0 (the roadmap revision of 2026-10-29).

### Fixed

- **Examples (CLAP):** activate applies the Backend parameter to the new plan report (`apply_backend`, the path of a parameter change too). Before, the handler ran on the model's default plan while the host showed the parameter's default "Bypassed", until the first change. The dry path's delay of `DryWetMixer` is the reported latency again: the read position trails the write position modulo the delay line's own size (latency plus one block), where it took the block size, and with a latency above the block (767 samples for GuitarLSTM over blocks of 1 to 512) `buffer_size - latency` wrapped below zero. At a latency equal to the block (the earlier fixed contract) the dry path was not delayed at all. A minimal CLAP host (48 kHz, blocks of 1 to 512) checks both: at 0 % wet the output is the input delayed by the latency on every sample, and so it is at 100 % wet on the default Bypassed plan with constant blocks (with varying blocks, the misses of the block-range model, #228, remain). JUCE's `juce::dsp::DryWetMixer` delays sample by sample and had no such bug; the plugin asserts that the latency fits its 32768-sample delay line (`k_max_dry_latency`).
- **LibTorch on Linux aarch64:** the LibTorch engine's init now pins OpenBLAS to one thread (`openblas_set_num_threads(1)`, looked up with `dlsym`) beside `torch::set_num_threads(1)`. The Linux aarch64 LibTorch package does its matrix products in OpenBLAS (the x86_64 packages in MKL, which `torch::set_num_threads` governs; macOS in Accelerate, Windows arm64 in Eigen), and `torch::set_num_threads` does not reach OpenBLAS's own worker pool: every product of an inference fanned out over one thread per core, whose workers wait for the next one spinning in `sched_yield`, so beside anira's inference threads they took every core, efficiency cores included. Measured on an M1 under Asahi Linux (the bundled RAVE funk-drum model at its 2048-sample window and 42.66 ms budget, 44.1 kHz, host blocks of 512, fed in real time for 15 s): 331 of 1291 blocks missed, though one inference alone takes 12 to 28 ms of its 46.4 ms hop; with OpenBLAS on one thread, none. The setting is OpenBLAS's, so it holds for the whole process; a build without OpenBLAS has no such symbol and is left as it is. `test_AdapterLibTorch` gains `InitPinsOpenBlasToOneThread` (skipped where the build carries no OpenBLAS); `docs/sphinx/custom_engines.rst` names the setting among the built-in engines' inits.
- **JUCE example, RAVE encoder and decoder (`MODEL_TO_USE` 7):** `processBlock` no longer reads the four latent offsets with `getParameterAsValue(...).getValue()`, which on the audio thread allocated a `ValueTreePropertyValueSource` per call and added and removed a listener on the parameters' `ValueTree`, beside the message thread's use of the same tree, four times per latent frame. The offsets are atomics now, stored by the parameter listener like the other parameters. `prepareToPlay` also resets the count of input samples towards the next latent frame, so after a re-prepare the frames are popped in step with the encoder's new stream instead of with the remainder of the old one. A latent frame the encoder misses (`pop_data` returns `ANIRA_MISSED` with the frame zero-filled) now counts and is passed to the decoder like any other: the loop used to wait for it as if it were not ready yet, which left its count ahead of the encoder's time-aligned stream for good, so every later pop came too early and the decoder was fed out of step. Driven in real time at 48 kHz with blocks of 2048 by two probe engines of RAVE's shapes, one encoder inference of 300 ms put 544 of 586 blocks behind, the decoder missed 121 times and the measured delay wandered (10237, 2048, 0 samples); with the frame passed on, 4 encoder misses during the stall, no decoder miss, and the delay stays at the reported 10237 samples.
- **Process exit with an inference in flight (Linux, macOS):** the core's exit handler now stops the inference pool and waits up to two seconds for the inferences in flight before the exit handlers registered ahead of it run, the destructors of the static state an engine's runtime builds at its first load among them (ONNX Runtime's environment and type tables: the models load in the session's create, before the prepare starts the pool and registers the handler). Before, the pool ran on until the library's fini pass (the unload hook), so an ONNX Runtime inference still running at `exit()` could run over its torn-down type tables ("GetElementType is not implemented", the engine call failing with `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE`) and then crash the process: `LibraryUnloadDeathTest.ExitWithABuiltInInferenceInFlightExitsNormally` died with SIGSEGV on the TFLite + ONNX Runtime shared CI leg, the one leg whose built-in engine there is ONNX Runtime, when the runner was slow enough to leave an inference in flight. The SIGSEGV was a consequence of the same race, not a second fault. The unload hook still joins the pool at exit without a bound, and an unload (`dlclose`) keeps its bounded wait and its abort. `test/contracts/unload` gains `ExitWaitsForTheInferenceBeforeTheRuntimesStaticsGo`, whose module engine keeps static state built before the prepare and exits with its own status when an inference finds it torn down: deterministic, it fails without the change. `docs/sphinx/troubleshooting.rst` describes the exit.
- **CI (Windows):** the shared Windows legs no longer race while copying the runtime DLLs. Every test binary of `test/` used to copy `ANIRA_SHARED_LIBS_WIN` into the one output directory they share, as a PRE_BUILD step of its own, and parallel links failed on the same file: "Error copying file (if different) ... shm.dll ... Permission denied" while `test_scheduler.exe` linked. One target, `anira_test_runtime_dlls`, now copies the DLLs once, and every test binary depends on it. The target waits for every target that builds a listed DLL: anira itself, by its path in the list, and the targets the `$<TARGET_FILE:...>` entries name (tanh-lib's core, gtest). Without that wait it copied `anira.dll` before the library was linked. A build without DLLs has no such target. The single-target copies of the examples, the benchmarks, the unload harness and `anira_abi_link` each write into a directory of their own, so they stay.
- **Web:** `measureRenderGeometry` no longer under-reports a burst rarer than its window. CI's device calls back with 481 frames: at a quantum of 480 Chromium renders one quantum per callback and two back to back once every 480 callbacks (about 5 s), so the probe's 300 ms of runs said 480 against a worst case of 960, and a handler prepared for it would miss a quantum every 5 s. The probe now also measures the frames per device callback: its worklet counts its calls in a shared word and notifies it, a Worker waits on the word (`Atomics.wait`) and times every wake with `performance.now()` (the worklet scope has only `Date.now`, whose 1 ms cannot tell 480 frames from 481: 0.02 ms per callback), and a least-squares fit of the runs' starts on the callback grid gives the period (holes where runs merged skipped, the first start and starts a stall delayed left out). The burst is the longest run or the frames per callback over the quantum rounded up, half a frame taken off so an exact fit stays one quantum, whichever is more; the probe watches 300 ms after its lead-in and on until 16 run starts or more settle that number within three standard errors and a tenth of a frame, at most 2 s (about 1 s in headless Chromium). Measured there (renderSizeHint 2048, 2047, 480, 256 and 128 over its 2048-frame callbacks): 2048.0 +- 0.2 frames per callback after 1 s, 0.07 after 2 s; 2047 now gives 4094, the same case as CI's 481/480; twelve fresh contexts gave 2048 each, and the loaded node unheld 2048 where its runs said 512. The early stop on evenly paced calls (about 50 ms) is gone: an exact fit needs the frames per callback too. A page whose policy refuses a blob Worker gets the runs alone. `render-geometry.test.ts` covers 481/480 -> 960 with no double in the window, 480/480 -> 480, 441/128 -> 512, 192/128 -> 256, 2048/128 -> 2048, a quantum of 2048 over a callback of 1024 -> 2048, a merged callback's hole, a delayed start and an unsettled short window; `live.html` keeps the 480 case and adds 2047.
- **Web:** `measureRenderGeometry`'s probe no longer reports a burst short on a loaded audio thread. It split the runs of back-to-back `process()` calls at a fixed gap (half a quantum, at least 1.5 ms), and a call late by more than that inside every callback, as a model's JavaScript processor that unfolds an inference's input once per callback makes one, cut each run short: re-measured with `{ probe: true }` while streaming, a GuitarLSTM page went from a latency of 6139 to 5887 samples (a burst of 1920 against 2048), Steerable-NAFX from 3071 to 3069, and on Firefox, where the probe is the only source, such a burst means missed quanta. A run now ends where a gap reaches halfway from one quantum's duration to the longest gap of the window, the gap between callbacks, wherever the window shows runs (its longest gap at least two quanta, reached at least twice, those gaps adding a tenth of the window); an evenly paced context keeps the fixed gap. In headless Chromium (bursts of 16 quanta), loads of 0.3 to 0.8 ms per call with a call late by 3 to 12 ms every 5th, 7th, 11th or 16th, the fixed gap measured 5 to 15 quanta and the new rule 16 every time. `live.html` probes three times under two such loads beside a streaming node and requires the burst read or more (with the fixed gap it measured 1920); `render-geometry.test.ts` covers loaded schedules, callbacks of 480 and 441 frames, and an evenly paced context with a late call.
- **Web (tests):** the live browser test (`web/test/browser/live.html`) no longer asserts the numbers of one machine's audio device. A CI runner's device buffer is 441 frames (`baseLatency` 9.1875 ms at 48 kHz), not a multiple of 128: `measureRenderGeometry` reads a burst of 512 (rounded up to whole quanta), and the probe measured 640, since on a loaded runner two callbacks ran almost back to back. The test now asserts that the probe is between the burst read and two quanta more (it never under-reports), asserts the misses at block_max 128 only where the burst exceeds that handler's latency (else it logs a skip), always asserts no miss at the measured geometry, and re-prepares mid-stream to a block_max past the latency (four bursts, doubled as needed), so the latency read back must change. `audio_nodes.rst` notes that a device buffer need not be a multiple of the quantum.
- **Web:** audio nodes created at once on one audio context (`await Promise.all([h1.createAudioNode(ctx), h2.createAudioNode(ctx)])`) all failed with "the audio node was not created in time", every time, offline and live, in Chromium and Firefox. The context's first-node setup (loading `worklet-scope.js`, then `emscriptenRegisterAudioObject`) was remembered only once it had finished, so each creation in flight registered the context again: one context became several module handles, each of which started its own worklet thread on the context's one `AudioWorkletGlobalScope`, and no node completed. The setup is now memoised per context as a promise, set before its first await, which the creations in flight share (a failed setup is dropped, so the next node retries); each creation keeps its own completion words. Tested with 2 and 4 nodes created at once (offline in Chromium and Firefox, live in Chromium), each rendering its own gain, and with two creations while another node of the context is destroyed (`web/test/browser/concurrent.html`, `web/test/audio-node.test.ts`).
- **Web:** out of memory is an error status, not an abort of the module. With the fixed memory (`ALLOW_MEMORY_GROWTH=0`) Emscripten's malloc aborted on failure (`ABORTING_MALLOC`'s default), so a handler whose models did not fit (an AUTO pool of 16 Workers times 16 instances of a RAVE model) killed the module with an uncatchable `RuntimeError: Aborted(OOM)`. The anira target now carries `-sABORTING_MALLOC=0` in its `INTERFACE` link options on WebAssembly (AniraWeb gets it from there; a consumer that prefers the abort passes `-sABORTING_MALLOC=1` after it, the last `-s` wins): malloc returns NULL, `operator new` throws `std::bad_alloc`, and the firewall of every C entry returns `ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY`. ONNX Runtime catches a `std::bad_alloc` itself and reports it as an `Ort::Exception` ("Load model from ... failed:std::bad_alloc"); the adapter maps that to `ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` too (it was `ANIRA_ERROR_MODEL_LOAD`). A pool's Wasm Worker whose region (its 4 MiB stack and thread-locals) cannot be allocated is `ANIRA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` naming the region; it read as "the operating system refused to create an inference thread of the pool", which is now kept for a Worker the browser refuses. No path aborted in the tests. The node gate `anira_em_oom` fills the 512 MiB heap, requires a handler on the bundled gain config refused with the status (once on a context whose pool must start again, then three times without a pool), a handler prepared once the memory is back, and the heap as free as before each time (the RAVE exports of the web example pass it too); it measures the core at 169 MiB, a gain handler at 1.4 MiB beside the pool's 4 MiB Worker stacks, and 16.4 / 16.8 MiB per instance of the funk-drums / djembe RAVE models. `build_web` fetches the model fixtures before the wasm gates. The browser test's `oom.html` does the same through the typed layer (an `AniraError`).
- **Web (build):** the module's runtime never exits. A `-pthread` link of emsdk 4.0.23 without the `noExitRuntime` symbol defines `keepRuntimeAlive()` as `runtimeKeepaliveCounter > 0` (`src/lib/libcore.js`: pthreads must be able to exit), although `EXIT_RUNTIME` is 0: so the first Emscripten callback into C that ran with no keepalive held (an `emscripten_set_timeout` callback) ended in `maybeExit` → `exit` → `proc_exit`, which terminated the pthreads and set `ABORT`, and every later callback into C (a timer, an audio worklet's creation) was dropped without a message. The library itself now depends on the `noExitRuntime` symbol (`src/platform/emscripten/em_runtime.cpp`, `EM_JS_DEPS`, pulled into every WebAssembly link of anira by the `INTERFACE` link option `-Wl,--undefined=anira_em_runtime_anchor`), so `keepRuntimeAlive()` is `noExitRuntime || ...` with `noExitRuntime = !EXIT_RUNTIME`, in every module that links anira and in every instance of its glue (the page, the Workers, the worklet); AniraWeb links `-sEXIT_RUNTIME=0`. An app that sets `EXIT_RUNTIME=1` owns its runtime's lifetime. The node gate `anira_em_runtime` (a `--no-entry` consumer with none of AniraWeb's flags; a program with `main()` includes `noExitRuntime` on its own, and so does Emscripten's default incoming Module API) runs a second `emscripten_set_timeout` callback after a first one; without the anchor it fails. The Worker reaper of this release uses `emscripten_set_timeout` again. The browser test's `boot-stop.html` creates four audio nodes and renders through the last one after the reaper's timers fired; without the flag it fails (checked: OOM, the reaper stopped after its first timer).
- **Web (build):** the console sink is in every WebAssembly link of anira. It is switched on by its TU's static constructor (`em_console.cpp`), and an object of the static archive that nothing references is not linked, its constructor with it: a consumer that called only `anira_log`, with no export list, printed nothing through it. The library now carries `-Wl,--undefined=anira_em_console_anchor` in its `INTERFACE` link options on WebAssembly, so the object is pulled into every consumer's module. The node gate `anira_em_console` (`test/abi/em_console.c`, part of `anira_abi_wasm_gates`) links the archive that way and requires its warning once, as `console.warn`; without the anchor it fails (checked: the sink is not linked, nothing reaches the console through it).
- **Web:** without a JavaScript log hook every record showed on the console as an error: tanh-lib's platform sink writes stderr, which the Emscripten glue prints as `console.error` for every level. On WebAssembly the platform sink is now anira's console sink by level (`src/platform/emscripten/em_console.cpp`, a log sink of `anira/utils/Logger.h` that calls `console.debug`, `.info`, `.warn` or `.error` with tanh-lib's line format, `[warn][rt][anira.scheduler] message`), on from the module's start; tanh-lib's own platform sink stays off there. `anira::detail::set_platform_sink_enabled` switches it, so a context's `ANIRA_LOG_FLAG_DISABLE_PLATFORM_SINK` and the JavaScript log hook hold it off as before. tanh-lib is not edited. The browser test's warning count: without a hook, once, as `console.warn`.
- **Web:** stopping a context's Wasm Workers while they still boot logs nothing and leaks nothing. `stop()` terminated a Worker still loading the glue or importing its module, which Firefox reports as console errors (`NS_ERROR_FAILURE` from `AniraWeb.js`, `uncaught exception: undefined`), and left its 4 MiB region allocated for good. Now the stop cancels the boot (`em::cancel_boot`: the bootstrap's record, which outlives the thread object, holds the state the entry and the cancel race on and the left word), the Worker finishes its boot without entering the loop and stores its left word after its last C frame, and a main-thread reaper (a timer every 100 ms while cancelled Workers remain) terminates it and frees its region and record; one whose boot has not finished after 10 s is terminated where it stands with a warning, its region left allocated. The browser test's `boot-stop.html` stops 64 contexts of two Workers 0 to 200 ms into their boot (an engine whose `init` takes 150 ms) and fails on any console error or uncaught exception: none in Chromium and Firefox; the cycles would exhaust the module's memory if a region were leaked (checked: a reaper that does not free fails it with OOM), and a context afterwards boots and enters its loops.
- **Web:** every record reached the console twice, a warning once through the log hook (`console.warn "[anira.scheduler] ..."`) and once through tanh-lib's platform sink, whose stderr the glue prints as `console.error "[warn][rt][anira.scheduler] ..."`, so harmless warnings showed as errors. The JavaScript log hook now takes the platform sink's place while it is set (`anira_em_set_log_hook` holds the platform sink off through the same counted hold as a context's `ANIRA_LOG_FLAG_DISABLE_PLATFORM_SINK`, `hold_platform_sink_off`, and releases it with the hook), so `anira.setLogHook()` shows each record once at its level and `setLogHook(null)` restores the platform sink. The browser test counts a core warning: once through the hook, once through the platform sink after the hook is removed.
- **Web (build):** the wasm build no longer deletes `web/licenses/onnxruntime/LICENSE` and `ThirdPartyNotices.txt`. Its post-link step cleared the directory and copied the two files from the ONNX Runtime archive only when present, and the backends' wasm archive ships neither, so every wasm build removed the tracked copies from the checkout, and a package built from that tree shipped without ONNX Runtime's license though it still links ONNX Runtime. The step now leaves the directory in place, overwriting the two files only when the archive has them; `PACKAGE.txt` is rewritten as before.
- **WebAssembly:** the core's mutexes exclude across the module's threads (issue #212). The module was linked with `-sSHARED_MEMORY=1` and no pthreads, whose libraries' `pthread_mutex_lock` is a stub that returns 0, so every `std::mutex` of anira (the core's lifecycle and engine mutexes, the context and handler registries of `src/capi`, the log-sink registry) and of libc++ locked nothing between the main thread, the inference Workers and the worklet. With `-pthread` they are futex mutexes. The node gate `anira_em_mutex` (`test/abi/em_mutex.c`, in `anira_abi_wasm_gates`) runs 1000 rounds of context, pipeline, C engine, handler and prepare, built and torn down, on the main thread and a second thread at once, after one round alone that creates the core's state.
- Head-of-line blocking in the inference pool: a thread that dequeued a job whose loaded model had no free instance waited for one in the claim loop with the job in hand (`engine::Loaded::claim_and_run`), polling even under `ANIRA_WAIT_BLOCKING`, and served nobody else meanwhile, so one slow model with few instances parked every thread of the pool and the jobs of every other model sat in the queue behind them (four threads, two custom engines of 5 ms per inference, one instance each: the other model's block took 25 ms under `ANIRA_WAIT_SPIN_BACKOFF` and 35 ms under `ANIRA_WAIT_BLOCKING` with nine jobs of the slow one in flight, 5 ms alone). Dispatch is instance-aware now: an inference thread runs a job of a stateless model only with an instance claimed for it (`engine::Loaded::try_claim` / `release`, `engine::Prepared::run_claimed`); a job that finds every instance busy goes on the model's wait list (`src/scheduler/InstanceWaitList.h`, one per pooled loaded model, `SessionElement::PlanSlot::m_waiting`; intrusive through the job's struct, `ThreadSafeStruct::m_waiting_session` / `m_waiting_next`, so it never allocates, under a spin lock of a few pointer writes that works between WebAssembly instances too) and the thread returns to the queue; the thread that holds an instance runs the model's waiting jobs, oldest first, before it gives the instance back, and the push-then-claim of a waiting job against the release-then-look of the holder (all sequentially consistent) keeps a job from waiting while an instance is free, so no thread polls for an instance under either wait strategy. The other model's block now takes 5.1 ms with the slow model loaded under both strategies, its throughput unchanged (197 inferences/s). A session's waiting jobs stay counted for its drain (a released session's are skipped by the holder like its queued ones); the in-flight count of the unload hook covers a thread from the dequeue until it is done with every job it ran, and a job on a wait list from its push until a thread takes it to run it or give it up; a thread told to stop gives the waiting jobs it takes up (zeros, `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE`, the record that the thread was stopped before an instance was free) instead of running them, so a stop still waits only for the inferences inside an engine. Exclusive (stateful) sessions, which never claim, and 2.x backends are dispatched as before; the claim loop stays for a caller that runs a shared call directly (`Prepared::run`). `Core::acquire_loaded_locked` fills the plan slot (the loaded model and its wait list) instead of returning the loaded model. Tests: `AbiInstanceDispatch.AnotherModelsJobIsNotHeldUpBehindASlowModel{SpinBackoff,Blocking}` (the other model's median delay with the slow one loaded within twice its delay alone; 4.9x and 7.0x before) and `AbiInstanceDispatch.AReleasedSessionsWaitingJobsAreSkippedNotStranded` (a handler destroyed while two of its jobs wait returns once the held inference finishes, and the waiting jobs never reach the engine; three engine calls before).
- A custom engine's `query` (`anira_engine_query_fn`, C++ `Engine::query`) ran off the main thread. Its documented contract is [main-thread], at `anira_handler_create` and at the capabilities entries, but the provider check that asks it (`check_providers`, `src/capi/handler.cpp`) ran again at every `anira_handler_prepare`. So an engine with a providers list had its query called from every thread that prepared a handler, concurrently when two handlers were prepared at once; TSan reported the race on a test engine's counter. `anira_handler_create` now keeps the answers its queries gave, per engine id (`anira::capi::QueryAnswers` on the handler, `QueryUse::Ask`), and `anira_handler_prepare` checks the plans against those answers without calling a query (`QueryUse::Reuse`). A create also asks each engine once, where the plan check and the `provider_options` check each asked it before. A built-in engine's providers are still checked against the context's capabilities as they are at prepare. Behaviour change: a custom engine whose query answer changes between a handler's create and its prepare is judged by the create's answer; create a new handler to ask again. The query slot's documentation (`abi/anira.yml`, `Engine::query`, `custom_engines.rst`) and `anira_handler_prepare`'s say so. Test: `AbiEngine.TheQueryRunsAtCreateAndPrepareReusesItsAnswer` creates two handlers on the main thread, clears the query's bit, then prepares both at once on two other threads, three rounds. The query ran twice, never off the main thread, and every prepare succeeds; a third create asks again and is refused. Against a prepare that queries, it fails and TSan reports the race in the query.
- A shared call that found every instance of its loaded model busy spun on the busy flags without backoff and never looked at its thread's stop flag (`engine::Loaded::claim_and_run`): it burned a core for as long as another thread held the instance (a 200 ms wait used 200 ms of CPU time), and a pool stop could not finish while the thread that held the instance was blocked inside its engine, since the stop joined the threads one by one and the waiting one never left. The claim loop now waits with the inference thread's own backoff (`src/utils/Backoff.h`, shared with `InferenceThread::exponential_backoff`: 4 immediate retries, 32 with a pause hint, then a yield and a 100 us sleep per retry; the same 200 ms wait now uses a few percent of it) and gives up with `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_STATE` once its thread is told to stop (`engine::StopSignal`, the thread's `should_exit`); the chunk then fails like any failed engine call (zeros, `ANIRA_ERROR_ENGINE` on the handler's latch) with a log record saying the thread was stopped before an instance was free. Every pool stop (`Core::shutdown`, the last session's release, a shrink) tells all its threads first and joins them after, through the new `InferenceThread::request_stop()`. The audio thread never reaches the claim loop. Tests: `Adapter.TheClaimLoopWaitsForABusySlotWithoutBurningACore` (the waiter's thread CPU time over a 200 ms wait stays below a quarter of it), `Adapter.TheClaimLoopGivesUpWhenItsThreadIsToldToStop`, and `AbiHandler.APoolStopDoesNotWaitForAThreadWaitingForABusyInstance` (one instance, two threads, the holder blocked in a gate engine: the waiting thread leaves within the bound at `Core::shutdown` while the holder is still inside the engine); all three fail against the old loop.
- `anira_handler_get_available_samples` collects the completed inferences for an output without a ring too (Static, State), as its documentation says; it returned 0 before collecting, so a push-only host that polled a Static output through it (the 2.x idiom `get_available_samples(0)` on an analyser) never saw the value change. Found by `test_OneSidedStreaming`'s analyser cases through `anira/compat/v2.hpp`.
- A 2.x single-form call (`InferenceHandler::process` / `push_data` / `pop_data` with a tensor index) could hand the planar adapter a slot whose channel array was NULL with a count above 0 (the handler's per-slot arrays keep what earlier calls left), which `PlanarFloatAdapter::present_slot` dereferenced; a NULL channel array is now a slot the call does not carry, an empty tensor, as the adapter's header already promised. Found by a full local clang-tidy sweep with the LLVM 20.1.0 wheel (clang-analyzer-core.NullDereference); CI's clang-tidy 20.1.8 does not report the path, so the merge queue passed without it; the guard stays as the header's promise made true.
- A checkout that had fetched the example-model fixtures once never saw a pin bump: `extras/fetch-models.cmake` skipped every destination directory that existed, so only CI (whose cache key hashes the file) and fresh checkouts got the new commit, and an older checkout kept, for example, an example-models tree without `StatefulAccumulatorNetwork` or the `.pte` exports, failing ten tests of `desktop-tests-debug` (`AbiState`'s bundled-model cases, `ExtrasFixtures.StatefulAccumulator`, the LiteRT and ONNX Runtime binding cases of `test_engines`, `AbiEngine.FlagsRoundTripThroughThePlanReport`) on "no such file" or on the gain export's older output names. Every fetched tree now carries a stamp, `.anira-pin`, with the URL and the commit it was fetched at, and a configure refetches a tree whose stamp is missing or differs (staged, then swapped in; offline, the old tree stays with a warning); the RAVE download is checked against its pinned SHA256 the same way. The first configure after this change refetches every tree once, since none carries a stamp yet.
- The built-in engine objects were kept for the core's life and freed by the unload hook, at process exit too: on macOS, where libanira stays mapped and dyld runs an image's C++ static destructors before its terminators, a failed create left an ONNX Runtime environment behind that the hook released after ONNX Runtime's own statics were gone (`OrtEnv::Release` takes a static mutex: `mutex lock failed: Invalid argument`, the process aborting after `LibraryUnload.FailedCreateLeavesNoState`). The core holds the objects weakly now: every loaded model of the engine holds the one object, the last to let go frees it on its own thread, so a failed load or the last unload frees the runtime's environment right there, and the next load makes a new object whose init runs again (ExecuTorch's `runtime_init()` stays once per process). Test: `CoreLifecycleTest.OneEngineObjectPerBuiltInEngineWhileSomethingHoldsIt`.
- The prepares of one custom loaded model could overlap: a pooled loaded model is every equal handler's, and a handler's prepare runs the engine's `prepare` on its caller's thread outside the core's lifecycle lock, so two handlers prepared on two threads reached the same loaded pointer at once. `Loaded::prepare` and a custom engine's unprepare hold a lock per loaded model now (`Loaded::prepare_mutex`), and the docs of `anira_engine_prepare_fn` and `anira_engine_unprepare_fn`, of `Engine::Loaded::prepare` and of the guide say that the prepares and unprepares of one loaded model never overlap (those of different loaded models may). Test: `AbiEngine.PreparesOfOneLoadedModelNeverOverlap`.
- A custom engine with a layout record saw the spec's extents in every `process` and `reset` call (the chunk's descriptors are built from the spec's shapes) while its load record's templates carried the engine's, against the promise of `anira_engine_ctx.inputs`; the descriptor adapter hands the engine a copy of the call's descriptors with the engine-side extents of the templates over the same memory now (`DescriptorPrepared::engine_view`, one copy per shared slot sized at prepare, nothing allocated per call). Test: `AbiEngine.ProcessSeesTheEngineSideExtentsOfALayoutRow`.
- A processor that filled `reset` alone (or `reset` beside the hooks) passed `anira_handler_prepare` with flags 0 and ran its reset on the driving thread: the real-time rule of prepare requires `ANIRA_PROCESSOR_FLAG_REALTIME_PRE_POST` for a filled `reset` now, as for a filled `pre_process` or `post_process` (the refusal names the phase), which is what the flag's doc promised. Test: `AbiProcessor.TheRealTimePromiseIsCheckedAtPrepare` (the reset-only cases).
- An `anira::Engine` object could have two live C engines: the object found its C engine through the handles its `Pipeline`s held, so a `Pipeline` destroyed while a handler created from it lived expired the object's view, and the next registration created a second C engine beside the first (a second init, a release on an object still in use, no shared loaded model). The object now keeps a detached handle of its C engine (`anira_custom_engine_detach`), which names it for as long as anything holds it, and a registration that lost the race against the C engine's release creates a fresh one; `Pipeline` holds no handles of its own.
- A built-in plan's prepare that threw inside `Core::create_session` (an exclusive session's own executor, its warm-up) left the pool entry it had acquired with no session holding it, since the rollback walked the session's plan table and the slot was not in it yet; the slot is placed before its prepare runs.
- A worker that dequeued a job before a session's drain looked and was preempted before registering it (a laggard) could hold the last reference to the session and, at its exit, decrement a counter inside the session it had just freed; a session could die on an inference thread. The in-flight count is taken before every enqueue and given back by the worker that finished the job or by the drain that completed it, on a counter the worker holds beside the session and decrements after it dropped its reference: the drain waits for every job outside the free pool, laggards included, and a session dies on the control thread that released it.
- The engine's reset boundary is the plan's, not the session's: after `anira_handler_reset` every plan of an exclusive handler is reset at its own first inference of the new stream. Before, the first plan to run adopted the session's stamp, and a plan switched to afterwards ran its executor on state from before the reset.
- `anira_handler_prepare` runs the processor's init before any model loads (a refused init no longer throws the loads away), checks every plan's provider against what its engine serves now (a context probed again since the create is seen; `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_SUPPORTED` as at create), validates the variant once instead of twice, and publishes the session to the Hard entries and reset only after its last callback returned (the getters answer inside the callbacks as before; a process call that races a prepare, which the contract forbids, is refused with `ANIRA_ERROR_NOT_PREPARED` instead of reading a table under construction).
- A built-in engine logs the text of a failing chunk for the first failure since the last success and no more (the scheduler latches the status and counts the rest); a persistent failure no longer floods the log queue with one record per chunk.
- LibTorch checks the output shapes at load for a record without a warm-up too (one forward on the probe), instead of at the first chunk.
- LiteRT binds a slot without a name match by the key order of the signature (its tensor names sorted, as TensorFlow Lite's signature runner lists them), so one file binds the same on both engines.
- ExecuTorch sets the XNNPACK delegate's runtime options for the process at the engine's init (`executorch::runtime::set_option`: a workspace per delegate instance, no weight cache, what every method load names in its map too) and reads them back (`get_option`), warning when the runtime refuses them or reports other values, so a renamed option key of a later release cannot bring the shared workspace and its mutexes back in silence (`engine::executorch_xnnpack_options` reads them for the tests).
- Docs: the aliasing promise (`ANIRA_ENGINE_FLAG_STATE_ALIAS`) says what holds: the addresses stay put across the chunks of aliasing plans (a flipping plan's chunk in between moves the pair), and what a failed call of an aliasing engine left in the buffer is the engine's (the last-good rule of a flipping pair does not apply); the Engine class doc lists the flag, `EngineContext::inputs()` names the load record, the capabilities enumerators say their rows' strings are valid until the next probe, and the ONNX Runtime bullet below names `AppendExecutionProvider_CUDA_V2`.
- The `provider_options` context extension was consumed per kind, not per backend: a set for `litert:gpu` or `executorch:xnnpack` was accepted in any build with ONNX Runtime, copied into the matching plans' pool key and then ignored, which could cause a needless second load; a custom engine never saw its set, since the load record had no options field; a set for the default provider, a misspelled backend word and a count of options without arrays were silently ignored; and `anira_ext_provider_options.sets` was indexed at the library's record size although its doc promised each record read within its own `struct_size` (harmless with one version of the record, wrong the first time it grows), as `anira_pipeline_add_inference`'s candidates were. Now every set is checked against the backend it names (its engine must read the kind, its provider must be served here), a custom engine that lists `"context:provider_options"` receives its backend's set in `anira_engine_load_info.option_keys` / `option_values` (`EngineLoadInfo::option_keys()` / `option_values()`), the options join a loaded model's record only where its engine reads them, a malformed set is `ANIRA_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT` at the set call (`anira_context_config_set_ext`) and at parse, both arrays are read at the stride of their first record's `struct_size`, and the options of a set are kept in key order, so two sets with the same pairs are one key of the pool whatever their order. Tests: `AbiExtRegistry.ProviderOptionSetsAreReadAtTheCallersStrideAndCheckedAtSet`, `AbiContext.AProviderOptionsSetForAnEngineWithoutAReaderIsRefused`, `AbiEngine.AProviderOptionsSetReachesARegisteredEngineThatConsumesIt`, `AbiEngine.AProviderOptionsSetIsCheckedAgainstItsBackendAtCreate`, `AbiEngine.CandidatesAreReadAtTheCallersStride`.
- **Docs:** the doc of `ANIRA_PHASE_INFERENCE` said it is the word a failure, a log record or a report row uses for the engine call, but no report row carries a phase and nothing took a message's words from the enum. One table now spells every phase, `k_phase_words` / `phase_word` of `src/capi/words.h` beside the provider and engine words (the lower-case names of the `anira_phase` values, `pre_process` to `unload`), and every message anira writes about a slot takes the phase's word from it: a processor phase that fails or that an accessor refuses, a processor's or an engine's refused init, load or prepare (`the engine 'x' refused load: it returned N (...)`), a failed engine call (`inference failed in session N: the engine of plan P returned S`), and the ring-dtype and real-time-promise refusals that name a processor phase. The texts are unchanged; the enum's doc states the rule and the inference value's doc what is logged. Test: `AbiProcessor.EveryPhaseHasOneWord`.
- The TFLite leg of the bundled gain model read the peak scalar on output slot 0: the 2.x processor ran the interpreter itself, whose output order for the `from_concrete_functions` export is [peak, stream], and bound by position; the adapter runs the export's signature, whose keys keep the declared mapping, and the shape check at prepare refuses a swapped pair (`[1]` against `[1, 1, 512]`) on every engine. The C-against-2.x oracles could not see it, since both sides bound alike; `AbiProcessor.Int16RingWithAConvertingProcessorOnEveryEngine` now runs on TFLite.
- `InferenceManager::set_plan_backends` / `SessionElement::set_plan_backends` throw `std::logic_error` on a prepared session instead of replacing a table the driving and the inference threads read without synchronization (the before-prepare rule was documented, not enforced); `InferenceManager::prepare()` clears `last_block_missed()`, as its documentation says. Tests: `PlanSwitch.ThePlanTableIsTheSessionsFromCreate`, `PlanSwitch.ABackendWithoutAPlanLeavesTheSelection` (the 2.x selection by backend on a table with no row for it).
- `AbiPrepare.ThePlanReportIsLoggedAtInfo` asserts its records under `ENABLE_LOGGING` only, like every other record assertion in `test_abi` (a build with `ANIRA_WITH_LOGGING=OFF` compiles the plan-report log out).
- Docs: `logging.rst` and the error and log strategy say what a real-time refusal returns now (the failure status, the count 0) and that a missed block is not a refusal; `ANIRA_TIMEOUT` and `ANIRA_MISSED` reference each other (a `_wait` entry's timeout is `ANIRA_MISSED`); `contributing.rst` names the clang-tidy changed-files rule and refers to each branch's ruleset instead of "ten contexts".
- **A backend switch while audio runs could run two engines for one chunk, or none.** `InferenceThread::inference()` loaded the session's backend atomic again in every engine block, and the blocks were independent: a `set_inference_backend` / `anira_handler_set_plan` that landed during an engine call ran a second engine on the same chunk when the new backend's block came later in the function (double cost, a missed budget, a Stateful model stepped twice), and one that landed between two blocks could skip every block, leaving the chunk's output tensors as its struct last held them. `inference()` now takes the backend as a parameter and dispatches through one `switch`: exactly one processor runs per call.
- **One chunk could be handled under three different backends.** `Core::pre_process`, the inference thread and `Core::post_process` each loaded the session's backend on their own, so a switch that landed while a chunk was queued or in flight told its `pre_process`, its `before_inference` / `after_inference` hooks, its engine and its `post_process` different backends (a 2.x pre/post processor that fills tensors per backend then fed one backend's layout to another). The chunk is now stamped with one load at submission (`SessionElement::ThreadSafeStruct::m_plan`, the dense plan index, written in `Core::pre_process`) and every later step resolves the stamp through the session's plan table: a switch applies from the next submitted chunk on, and a chunk that is queued or in flight finishes on the backend it started on. Documented on `InferenceHandler::set_inference_backend` and `InferenceManager::set_backend`, whose doc no longer claims a session reinitialization.
- **`anira_handler_set_plan` from several threads could leave `anira_handler_get_plan` naming one plan while another plan's engine ran.** The entry is `[thread-safe]` but stored the dense index and the session's backend separately; two interleaved calls could keep the index of one and the backend of the other until the next call.
